## Finalized findings

### BR-0444: P2 - Move storage and provider probes off the Compose thread

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: performance/responsiveness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0393
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L1694-L1789,L2013-L2103` in synchronous SAF and import-location inventory
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SafUriPermissions.kt:L40-L53`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ImportLocationManager.kt:L123-L171`, and BR-0429
- Evidence: Opening SAF Links constructs its remembered list during composition, reads custom-set and CD-source files, enumerates persisted grants, and calls `canAccessSafUri` for every tracked URI. Those checks synchronously invoke external `ContentProvider.openInputStream` or `openFileDescriptor`, including cloud and removable providers, on the main looper. Opening the import-location picker separately runs `getActiveRoot().walkTopDown().filter(...).sumOf(...)` inside every rendered volume item, repeating the complete unbounded tree traversal once per candidate and again for the confirmation dialog. None of this work has a dispatcher, timeout, cancellation boundary, scan budget, cached snapshot, or progress state.
- Trigger: Open SAF Links with one slow, offline, or unresponsive document provider, or open Set new location after importing a large tree with many extracted mission and audio files and multiple candidate volumes
- Impact: A routine Advanced Settings dialog can freeze the only UI thread long enough for visible jank or an ANR. Recomposition and multiple volume rows multiply storage metadata calls, and a provider controlled outside the app can hold the settings UI indefinitely without a cancellation path.
- Expected: Compose renders from lifecycle-owned asynchronous snapshots; filesystem and provider probes run on bounded IO work with cancellation, deadlines, deduplicated scans, progress, and explicit unavailable states.
- Suggested fix: Launch one lifecycle-scoped IO operation per dialog generation, compute source usage once, cap or incrementally aggregate traversal, and publish immutable results to Compose. Probe unique URIs concurrently only under a small limit and provider timeout, cancel on dismissal, and retain cached metadata so recomposition performs no external I/O.
- Validation: Use exact-limit and very large imported trees, several volumes, slow removable storage, duplicated URIs, provider delays, hangs, exceptions, permission revocation, dialog dismissal, navigation, and activity recreation. Measure main-looper stalls, require bounded cancellation and one source-tree scan per generation, and keep the UI responsive with accurate progress and inaccessible-provider reporting.
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0406): The recorded-demo section's initial inventory correctly calls `InputDemoManager.listStagedDemos` on `Dispatchers.IO`, but its local `refresh` calls the same function directly from the main-thread coroutine after Add or Delete. Delete All is more direct: its button handler calls `deleteAllStagedDemos` on the main looper, and that method first inventories every staged demo. Inventory does not read only the header; `readHeader` streams every line of every `.dximdemo` to recompute duration. Long recordings, per-frame-state recordings, or many retained demos therefore turn ordinary post-action refresh and bulk deletion into unbounded app-private file reads and regular-expression work on the Compose thread.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0406): Retain exact-limit, long, many, and per-frame-state D1 and D2 recordings, then exercise single delete, delete all, and add-to-game. Require inventory and duration work to stay off the main looper, cancel when the page leaves composition, avoid reparsing unchanged files, and keep frame latency bounded while reporting progress or stale metadata explicitly.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0407): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/VisualReplacementPolicy.kt:L21-L30` with `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt` in game-creation composition and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ModManager.kt:L795-L820,L1659-L1683` in enabled-mod ZIP inventory
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0407): `CreateGameDialog` computes `VisualReplacementPolicy.summaryForPvp` directly inside `remember` during composition. The summary calls `enabledVisualReplacementSummary`, which opens every enabled DXA or mission ZIP and iterates every entry to count visual-replacement assets, with no IO dispatcher, work budget, cancellation, or cached manifest. The host-confirm and resume creation flows also compute the same summary on the main thread. Many enabled archives or one archive with a very large central directory can therefore freeze game creation, and scan exceptions silently turn that pack's count into zero.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0407): Create exact-limit and very large enabled-mod sets, archives with many entries, duplicate and malformed entries, slow storage, corrupt ZIPs, dismissal, recomposition, host confirmation, resume, and Activity recreation. Require no archive open or iteration on the main looper, one cancellable cached scan per mod generation, bounded progress and memory, explicit scan failure rather than a false zero, and accurate notice metadata.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0412): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L434-L442,L483-L509,L547-L551` in confirmed danger-zone actions
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0412): All three confirmation callbacks execute directly on the Compose main looper. Reset walks and rewrites every discovered D1 and D2 pilot through JNI. Clear Game Data recursively walks every file set to preserve player data, deletes all other descendants, iterates and deletes every managed CD source, recursively deletes the entire mod and generated mission/cache trees, revokes grants, and rewrites manifests. Delete All Player Files enumerates every set and legacy game directory. None uses an IO dispatcher, lifecycle-owned worker, progress state, timeout, or cancellation boundary, and the callbacks kill the process immediately after the synchronous calls. Large extracted missions, mods, recordings, or many pilot/save files can therefore freeze the only launcher thread throughout an operation that may traverse and unlink thousands of files, inviting an ANR or process termination with a partially completed destructive operation.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0412): Populate internal and removable import roots with exact-limit and very large set, mission-cache, mod, audio, pilot, and save trees, then exercise every confirmed danger action with slow storage, deletion failures, backgrounding, activity recreation, and process interruption. Require bounded main-looper frame time, lifecycle-safe worker ownership, explicit progress and per-target results, deterministic recovery from partial cleanup, and restart only after the requested operation reaches a verified terminal state.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0413): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L1623-L1685` in confirmed Storage Inspector deletion
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0413): The Storage Inspector's confirmation callback also performs deletion on the Compose main looper. A linked mission entry constructs and loads `ModManager`, deletes the source and sidecars, recursively deletes the complete extracted mission tree through `MissionZipExtractionStore.removeOwner`, and rewrites both extraction and mod manifests before returning; the missing-source branch still recursively deletes that tree and rewrites its manifest. Large durable mission archives or slow storage therefore make an ordinary single-entry Remove action block rendering for the complete unlink operation, without progress, cancellation, or lifecycle-owned work.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0413): Remove linked owners with exact-limit and very large extracted trees, slow storage, dismissal, backgrounding, recreation, and injected descendant and manifest failures. Require deletion and metadata work off the main looper, bounded frame latency, lifecycle-safe progress, and an explicit terminal result.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0415): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AutoselectEditorPage.kt:L165-L201,L204-L327,L460-L517` with `android/app/src/main/cpp/android_autoselect.cpp:L69-L168,L180-L213,L290-L324,L327-L379` in synchronous pilot inventory, comparison, and writes

Initial load reads and parses every D1 pilot to select a summary, scans them all again for mismatch count, then repeats both scans for D2. Every single item crossing during touch drag or D-pad reorder calls `checkChanges`, which synchronously reopens and parses every pilot for the active game; auto-scroll can trigger another complete scan on successive 16 ms iterations. - Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0457): Composition constructs `AudioSourceManager`, whose initializer reads and parses the complete audio registry on the main looper. Require registry and descriptor reads, parse, stats, deletes, and writes on lifecycle-owned cancellable IO work, bounded descriptor bytes, immutable generation publication, progress or explicit unavailable state, and bounded main-looper frame latency.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Compose now renders the affected storage features from lifecycle-owned asynchronous snapshots rather than performing provider, archive, registry, pilot, manifest, hash, traversal, or deletion work during composition and click callbacks. SAF probes run on `Dispatchers.IO` with unique-key deduplication, concurrency three, per-probe cancellation signals, and three-second deadlines. Import-root usage is computed once per generation with 100,000-entry and depth-64 bounds. Recorded and classic demo inventory, visual-replacement summaries, dangerous cleanup, Storage Inspector removal, autoselect pilot scans and writes, file-set usage and mutation, game-process playlist preparation, import hashing and manifest writes, setup automation storage work, CD configuration publication, mission text, and music registry and file-detail operations now use cancellable background work with explicit loading, busy, unavailable, or terminal state where applicable. MainActivity awaits playlist preparation before native startup, preserving publication order without blocking its UI looper. Scoped Kotlin quality and compilation passed when implemented. The closeout run passed all JVM unit tests and debug APK assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64. Focused emulator SAF validation reached SetupActivity readiness and native game startup and passed its live descriptor assertion. The default SAF gameplay script then stalled twice at the later native `skip_briefing` step after all launcher and mount checks had succeeded; no provider-delay fault-injection matrix was run in this tranche.

### BR-0087: P2 - Release the manifest I/O object after a successful SAF mount

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime/file-descriptor
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0043
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/physfs_archiver_saf.c:L476-L521,L621-L632` in `SAF_openArchive` and `SAF_closeArchive`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/physfsx_android_shared.c:L29-L65`; PhysicsFS `src/physfs.c` `openDirectory`, `nativeIo_destroy`, and successful handle closure; and PhysicsFS `src/physfs.h` `PHYSFS_Archiver.closeArchive` ownership contract
- Evidence: PhysicsFS creates a native `PHYSFS_Io` for the `.saf_manifest.json` file and transfers it to the archiver when `SAF_openArchive` succeeds. The framework destroys that object only when no archiver succeeds; after success its directory handle retains only the archiver callbacks and returned opaque pointer. Built-in archivers retain the input I/O in their archive object and destroy it from `closeArchive`, as the public contract requires. SAF instead reads the full manifest, returns a `SafArchive` that has no input-I/O field, and neither destroys the now-unused `io` before returning nor from `SAF_closeArchive`. Closing the archive frees only parsed entries and the archive object. The native I/O destructor is therefore never reached, leaving its platform file handle, duplicated path, metadata allocation, and I/O allocation alive even after unmount or `PHYSFS_deinit`
- Trigger: Successfully mount any `.saf_manifest.json`, then unmount it or deinitialize PhysicsFS; repeated retained-process initialization or mount cycles make the leak accumulate
- Impact: Every successful SAF-manifest mount permanently consumes one native file descriptor and several allocations until process death. Repeated preview, service, or future set-switch cycles can exhaust the descriptor table and cause unrelated asset, save, audio, or provider opens to fail
- Expected: A successful archiver assumes ownership of the supplied manifest I/O and destroys it exactly once, either immediately after it has copied all required bytes or during archive closure; failure ownership remains consistent with PhysicsFS
- Suggested fix: Since parsing eagerly copies all manifest state, call `io->destroy(io)` only on the successful path after `parse_manifest` has returned a complete archive and before returning that archive. Alternatively store the I/O pointer in `SafArchive` and destroy it from `SAF_closeArchive`, matching the built-in archivers. Audit every failure branch so PhysicsFS, not SAF, retains responsibility when `openArchive` returns `NULL`, and prevent double destruction
- Validation: Add a mount/unmount loop around a valid manifest and compare `/proc/self/fd` plus a tracking allocator before the first mount and after every unmount; repeat through `PHYSFS_deinit`, malformed-manifest failure, mount rejection, and allocation-failure injection, asserting the input I/O is destroyed exactly once on every path and no descriptor count grows
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. After the SAF archiver eagerly parses and copies a valid manifest, the successful `SAF_openArchive` path now destroys the transferred input `PHYSFS_Io` before returning the independent archive table. All failure paths still return without destruction so PhysicsFS retains its documented rejection-path ownership, and archive closure continues to free only copied entries. The maintained SAF emulator regression now inspects the live game process descriptor table immediately after mount and fails if `.saf_manifest.json` remains open; bounded launcher-readiness polling prevents ADB stalls from silently exceeding the test budget, and a focused descriptor-only mode preserves the existing full gameplay test. Scoped C and PowerShell quality passed, debug APK assembly passed for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and the focused emulator lifetime test passed with cleanup and exact file restoration. The default full SAF gameplay mode reached the same successful descriptor assertion twice but later stalled in its unrelated `skip_briefing` automation step.

### BR-0252: P2 - Enforce the mono/stereo PCM contract before game playback

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/audio-format
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0139
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/pcm_decoders.c:L79-L112,L167-L199` in OGG and FLAC result publication
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/pcm_decoders.h:L13-L30`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/digi_tsf_music.c:L273-L325,L565-L647`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/CMakeLists.txt:L342-L434`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/test_fingerprint.c:L181-L241`
- Evidence: The public result contract describes `channels` as 1 or 2, but both OGG paths and both FLAC paths copy the decoder-reported channel count directly without validation or conversion. The pinned dr_flac API accepts up to eight channels and returns all channels interleaved, while stb_vorbis permits multichannel streams and its full-file helpers likewise return interleaved source channels. The paired game music loader accepts that result unchanged. Its renderer treats every count of two or more as stereo and indexes frame `idx` at `idx * 2` and `idx * 2 + 1`, rather than `idx * channels`. For a valid six-channel stream with N frames, it therefore interprets center, LFE, and surround samples as later stereo frames, consumes only the first 2N values of the 6N-value buffer, and stretches that misframed prefix across the declared N-frame duration while reporting successful playback.
- Trigger: Select a valid multichannel OGG or FLAC as custom game or jukebox music through either a PhysicsFS path or an absolute playlist entry
- Impact: Both games successfully start a supported file format but play channel-shuffled, truncated-source audio at the wrong effective content progression instead of the intended track; diagnostics still report the original rate, frame count, and channel count, obscuring the incompatible renderer stride.
- Expected: Every successful `pcm_decode_result_t` satisfies its documented mono/stereo invariant, with explicit deterministic downmixing for supported multichannel inputs or an actionable rejection before ownership reaches playback.
- Suggested fix: Add one checked PCM-output policy shared by file and memory decoders. Validate positive rate, frame count, and channel count; either request or perform a documented stereo downmix with correct source stride and checked allocation arithmetic, or free the decoded buffer and reject channel counts outside one and two. Keep fingerprinting and game playback on the same declared representation and make playback defensively reject any impossible result.
- Validation: Add channel-distinct three-, six-, and eight-channel OGG and FLAC fixtures plus mono and stereo controls. Decode through file and memory APIs, then exercise paired D1/D2 PhysicsFS and absolute-playlist playback with captured PCM. Require exact documented downmix samples and duration or explicit rejection, correct ownership cleanup, no successful incompatible state, and unchanged mono/stereo fingerprints and music.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. File and memory MP3, OGG, and FLAC decoding now pass through one shared output-status gate before returning ownership. Successful results require allocated PCM, a positive sample rate and frame counts, a retained count no larger than the declared total, checked sample-count arithmetic, and exactly one or two channels. Unsupported channel layouts return a distinct status and release the decoded allocation. The shared Android music loader used by both D1 and D2 checks the contract again, requires a complete playback buffer, and reports that mono or stereo is required instead of installing incompatible renderer state. Decoder inputs whose integral sizes cannot be represented are rejected, CD result publication uses the same gate, and freeing a result clears all metadata. Focused tests accept mono and stereo, reject 0, 3, 6, and 8 channels, reject invalid rate and frame relationships, retain existing mono/stereo fingerprint expectations, and exercise the real MP3 file and memory decoder paths. Scoped code quality, the focused native fingerprint suite, `git diff --check`, and debug APK assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. No emulator playback sweep was run in this remediation tranche.

### BR-0253: P2 - Fail Android startup when selected PhysicsFS content cannot mount

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/storage-lifecycle
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0139
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/physfsx_android_shared.c:L14-L30,L32-L69,L72-L109` in write-directory and search-path setup
- Related: `d1/misc/physfsx.c:L29-L52`, `d2/misc/physfsx.c:L29-L61`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FileSetManager.kt:L167-L180`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ModManager.kt:L624-L664`, and `android/game_scripts/test_launch_to_automap.json5:L72-L73`
- Evidence: The helper returns `void` and discards the result of setting the common preferred write directory, creating the per-game directory, switching to that isolated write directory, mounting both directories, registering the SAF archiver, mounting the selected active set and optional SAF manifest, and adding the base and data search roots. Enabled mod mounts are the only checked operations, but failure merely logs and continues; earlier successful mods remain active. Paired `PHYSFSX_init` callers then return from Android initialization without any status gate. PhysicsFS documents that a failed `PHYSFS_setWriteDir` leaves the prior directory unchanged, so failure to switch to the per-game directory can leave writes rooted at the common files directory. A selected external set that disappears or an invalid enabled archive can likewise fail its mount while fallback roots or only a subset of mods remain searchable. Launcher tests prove generated path-file shape and the nominal mount path, but no native result reaches the launcher when the selected configuration was not established.
- Trigger: Revoke or unmount an external active-set directory during launch, make the per-game directory unwritable, corrupt or truncate an enabled DXA or mission archive, remove a generated mod root after path-file creation, or inject failure into any selected-content mount
- Impact: The game can launch with stale fallback assets, silently omit the selected set, or combine only part of an enabled mod stack while presenting the requested configuration as active. A failed per-game write-directory switch can also place pilots, saves, and configuration under the shared root, defeating D1/D2 isolation and risking collisions or apparently missing state on the next successful launch.
- Expected: Startup publishes success only after the per-game write root and every required selected content source are established under the requested ordering; any failure either rolls back the partial configuration and reports an actionable launch error or follows an explicit user-approved degraded-mode policy.
- Suggested fix: Return a structured setup result through paired `PHYSFSX_init` and game startup. Check every initialization, write-directory, mkdir, registration, and mount result with its exact path and PhysicsFS error. Build selected mounts transactionally, recording each successful addition so a later failure can unmount in reverse order and restore the prior write root. Classify optional legacy roots separately from the active set and enabled mods, and surface failure to the launcher instead of relying only on native logs.
- Validation: Add injectable PhysicsFS wrappers and fail every setup operation individually. Launch both games with unavailable internal and external sets, a read-only or failed per-game directory, corrupt and disappearing mods, a later failure in a multi-mod order, and SAF registration or mount failure. Assert nonzero startup and a precise visible error, no fallback game-data admission, no partial mod set, rollback to the original search and write roots, no cross-game pilot/save/config writes, and unchanged nominal search order in the existing automap and mod-loading flows.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Android search-path initialization now returns a structured operation, path, and PhysicsFS diagnostic through the paired D1 and D2 startup gates. PhysicsFS initialization, preferred and per-game write-root selection, directory creation, required search roots, SAF archiver registration, any present SAF manifest, the selected active set, and every enabled mod now fail closed. Required mounts are recorded and removed in reverse order after any later failure, the prior write directory is restored, and optional base and data compatibility roots are admitted only after required setup completes. Native fatal errors are atomically retained across process exit and shown by the launcher as a `Launch Blocked` dialog. Focused native fault injection covers nominal ordering, absent selection, every setup stage, active-set failure, SAF-manifest failure, and a late multi-mod failure with rollback. Scoped code quality, warning-clean `/W4` focused compilation, the focused native test, all JVM unit tests, and debug APK assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. The broader host suite passed 22 of 24 tests; the two failures are the pre-existing `graphics_config_transaction_tests` stack-cookie exit and `chromaprint_db_config_tests` crash already recorded during prior remediation.

### BR-0410: P2 - Separate album fingerprints from the physical-disc parser contract

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: integration/schema
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0363, R1-CHUNK-0365
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/assets/known_discs.json5:L914-L920,L944-L959,L1342-L1387` in representative assigned album records
- Related: the first album at `android/app/src/main/assets/known_discs.json5:L578-L594`, the assigned physical discs at L1390-L1416, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DiscIdentifier.kt:L56-L98`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L108-L141,L397-L448`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDiscImport.kt:L402-L459`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L1368-L1414`
- Evidence: The shared `discs` array contains 32 `type: "album"` fingerprint collections, including 22 in the assigned range. Album objects omit the physical-disc `game` field and their tracks omit `sha1`; two assigned albums are empty. `FingerprintBridge` deliberately accepts this schema when flattening audio fingerprints, but `DiscIdentifier.loadDatabase` maps every array element as a physical disc with mandatory `getString("game")` and mandatory per-track `getString("sha1")`. One missing field throws out of the whole map into a catch that returns `emptyList()`. The first album occurs before two assigned physical-disc records and 28 others; therefore none of the 30 valid physical records can ever be returned by either maintained import path.
- Trigger: Import or register any BIN/CUE disc while the frozen bundled `known_discs.json5` is installed, including the assigned Descent II Infinite Abyss disc
- Impact: Physical-disc identification silently returns no match for every known release. Imports fall back to timestamped custom source IDs, omit the known label and legacy disc ID, skip direct database track-name lookup, and depend on less-specific fingerprint fallback even when the exact maintained disc hashes are present.
- Expected: Each consumer selects and validates only the record variant it supports, and one valid album record cannot invalidate physical-disc records or vice versa.
- Suggested fix: Give physical discs and fingerprint albums separate top-level arrays or a versioned discriminated schema. Make `DiscIdentifier` explicitly select physical records before requiring `game` and `sha1`, validate each selected record transactionally, and fail initialization with an actionable diagnostic for malformed records rather than converting the whole database to an ordinary no-match state. Keep `FingerprintBridge` on the album-capable projection.
- Validation: Load a database containing only physical discs, only albums, the current mixed ordering, albums before and after discs, empty albums, and malformed records of each type. Require every valid physical hash set to identify identically regardless of album population, every album fingerprint to remain available to the native matcher, malformed selected records to fail explicitly, and both path and SAF import flows to persist the expected disc ID, label, legacy ID, and track names.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Physical identities remain exclusively in `known_discs.json5`, while the album generator now publishes 33 fingerprint collections under the separate `albums` contract in `known_albums.json5` without rewriting the physical asset. `DiscIdentifier` parses only the physical array, transactionally validates identity, game, track type, track number, SHA1, and duplicate-track invariants, and reports indexed initialization failures instead of converting a malformed database into an ordinary no-match result. `FingerprintBridge` explicitly projects both assets into the native matcher and binds both to its database identity; direct physical track-name lookup remains physical-only. The maintained assets contain 36 valid physical discs, no album-shaped physical records, 33 albums, 477 published album tracks, and two valid empty albums. Two forced generator runs produced identical album bytes and left the physical asset unchanged. Focused JVM tests cover physical-only parsing, the maintained `d1d2` game variant, misplaced albums, malformed physical records, combined fingerprints, album-only input, and empty albums. Scoped code quality, PowerShell syntax validation, Kotlin compilation, unit tests, debug asset packaging, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0515: P2 - Keep granted FileProvider URIs immutable

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0456, R1-CHUNK-0471
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L1541-L1648` in external mission-document cache identity, cleanup, and viewer launch
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZip.kt:L17-L23,L48-L92,L260-L338`, `android/app/src/main/res/xml/file_paths.xml:L2-L9`, `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:L75-L84`, `android/ai tool plans/asset management/plan_mission_zip_external_readmes_20260610.md`, and BR-0018 and BR-0456
- Evidence: The viewer receives read permission for a FileProvider URI backed by `cache/file_view/<Integer.toHexString(key.hashCode())>-<safeLeaf>`, where the key is the archive path plus attacker-controlled constituent path. Java's 32-bit string hash is not a unique identity: with one fixed archive prefix, `Aa/readme.pdf` and `BB/readme.pdf` have identical hash codes and identical sanitized leaves, and the JDK 21 probe produced the same `bb45dff9-readme.pdf` cache name for both. Opening the second document deletes and rewrites the first URI's backing file while the first viewer still holds its grant and may open or reread lazily. Even without a collision, post-copy cleanup preserves only the newly created file when total cache size exceeds 64 MiB; two ordinary 40 MiB documents cause the first granted file to be deleted immediately rather than retained for the documented 24-hour lazy-reader window. The design explicitly warns that external viewers can open lazily, but focused tests cover selection and MIME only and never cache identity, eviction, concurrent viewers, or grant lifetime. BR-0456 separately covers incomplete bytes before grant.
- Trigger: From one mission archive, open `Aa/readme.pdf` and then `BB/readme.pdf` in an external viewer, or open two distinct documents whose retained cache sizes together exceed 64 MiB while the first viewer has not finished reading
- Impact: A viewer can display bytes from a different mission constituent under the first document's URI, fail partway through with a missing file, or change content while open. Users cannot rely on the readme or manual they selected, and a crafted mission can deterministically substitute one document for another after the launcher grants access.
- Expected: Each launched view receives a collision-resistant, immutable content generation that remains readable for the documented grant window; later extraction and cache pruning cannot replace or delete bytes still owned by an active or conservatively retained grant.
- Suggested fix: Create a fresh cache generation per successful open using a collision-resistant content or random identity plus the preserved extension, publish it atomically as required by BR-0456, and never reuse a pathname that may have an outstanding grant. Track conservative lease timestamps or generations and prune only files older than the full retention window; if the cache cap would require removing a younger granted generation, reject or defer the new extraction instead of invalidating an existing URI. Revoke grants when ownership is explicitly complete where Android viewer behavior permits.
- Validation: Add a pure cache-identity test with known Java hash collisions and sanitized-leaf collisions, plus instrumentation viewers that delay first open, keep a descriptor open, reopen the URI, and read while a second document launches. Exercise two 40 MiB files, age and size boundaries, rapid repeated taps, concurrent distinct and same documents, process recreation, Android cache eviction, failed publication, and malicious path variants. Require every granted URI to expose exactly its original complete bytes for the retention contract, no URI reuse across distinct generations, no cleanup of a younger grant, bounded cache behavior, and eventual safe reclamation.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0471): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/res/xml/file_paths.xml:L2-L9` with `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ConfigImportExport.kt:L55-L65,L124,L482-L504` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L684-L738,L825-L879,L1122-L1132,L2260-L2271`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0471): The assigned resource exposes each cache subtree by stable root name and relative path. Config export always rewrites `touch_layout.json`, `controller_config.json`, or `dxx_redux_config.json` directly in `config_exports` before granting the resulting URI. Advanced Settings likewise copies debug logs, crash reports, and input-demo artifacts under the unchanged source basename in the mounted `file_view`, `debuglog_exports`, `crashlog_exports`, or `inputdemo_exports` directory. FileProvider consequently returns the same content URI whenever a producer reuses one of those paths. A recipient that delays its first open, reopens later, or holds a descriptor while the user edits and re-exports a config or recopies a changing active log can observe the later generation, truncation during rewrite, or mixed lifetime instead of the bytes selected for the first grant. This is the same post-grant identity defect as the mission viewer; BR-0456 and BR-0443 separately cover incomplete bytes before a URI or Downloads artifact is published.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0471): Share each fixed-name config, then change its contents and share it again while instrumented recipients delay open, hold a descriptor, and reopen the first URI. Repeat for an actively changing debug log and same-name demo/log copies, with synchronized concurrent exports and process recreation. Require a unique immutable URI generation per action, exact original bytes for every outstanding grant, atomic complete publication before grant, no mutation from later shares, bounded reclamation after a conservative lease, and unchanged read-only provider containment.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. All launcher FileProvider producers now publish through one synchronized generation store. Each share or view action writes and syncs an exact-length temporary file inside a fresh UUID directory, renames it before URI creation, preserves a bounded safe extension-bearing name, and never reuses a previously granted path. Persistent generation timestamps provide a conservative 24-hour lease across process recreation; cleanup deletes only expired generations and legacy files. Each used publication root has a 64 MiB bound, but admission fails without deleting an unexpired grant when the bound is full. Focused tests cover the known Java hash collision, sanitized-leaf and repeated-name generations, path containment, live-generation budget rejection, exact expiry, and incomplete publication cleanup. Existing config-export and mission ZIP tests, scoped Kotlin quality checks, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0513: P2 - Use canonical save metadata labels in Save Details

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0454
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSaveExplorer.kt:L1092-L1105,L1165-L1183` in Save Details music and save-kind labeling
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupResumePanel.kt:L73-L80`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupConfigFiles.kt:L182-L212`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/android_save_meta.h:L24-L31`, `d1/main/digi.h:L102-L105`, `d2/main/digi.h:L104-L107`, `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/SaveExplorerTest.kt:L176-L204`, and `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicLaunchPolicyTest.kt:L8-L105`
- Evidence: Both engines define music type 1 as built-in, 2 as Redbook, and 3 as custom. The launcher mirrors that contract by mapping MIDI mode to 1, CD mode to 2, Files mode to 3, and treating a resume override of 2 as requiring CD track order. Save Details instead maps type 2 to `MIDI`, so the displayed source contradicts the exact value that Load restores. The same dialog formats save kind through `resumeSaveKindLabel`, whose default is `Manual save` and which omits the valid native `auto_periodic` kind. Its compact row uses a separate helper that correctly labels `auto_periodic` as `Periodic`, so one save is described differently depending on whether its details are open. The focused detail test covers only `auto_exit` and music type 2 without asserting the Music row, while launch-policy tests independently prove that 2 is CD.
- Trigger: Open Save Details for any D1 or D2 save created with Redbook/CD music selected, or for a periodic autosave
- Impact: The explorer tells the user that a CD-backed save will restore MIDI even though Load restores CD, and presents an automatic periodic recovery point as a deliberate manual save. This can cause the wrong save to be selected or retained and makes music-source restoration and autosave provenance appear broken when the underlying metadata is correct.
- Expected: Every valid native music type and save kind has one canonical user-facing label shared by compact rows, details, resume choices, and launch policy, with type 2 identified as CD or Redbook and `auto_periodic` identified as a periodic autosave.
- Suggested fix: Centralize Kotlin label mappings beside a documented mirror of the native enums, use one save-kind formatter in both compact and detail rows, label music types with current launcher terminology (`Base game MIDI` or built-in for 1, `CD` for 2, and Files or custom for 3), and add a synchronization note or contract test against the paired native constants.
- Validation: Assert detail and compact labels for every native save kind and music type 0 through 3, including `auto_periodic`; load saves from each music mode and require the detail label, written `MusicType`, CD-order flag, custom-files flag, and paired D1/D2 runtime source to agree. Add unknown-value cases that remain explicitly unknown rather than falling through to a valid provenance label.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. A shared Kotlin metadata-label contract now documents the paired native music constants and supplies the only music-source and save-kind label mappings. Music types 0 through 3 render as None, Base game MIDI, CD, and Files; unknown values stay Unknown. Manual and all five native autosave kinds share one formatter across compact explorer rows, Save Details, and resume choices, including Periodic auto-save, while unknown kinds no longer fall through to Manual save. Bridge decoding preserves unknown music values for accurate presentation, and config launch policy uses the shared constants for MIDI, CD, and Files behavior. Focused Save Explorer, resume-panel, and music-launch-policy tests, scoped Kotlin quality checks, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0512: P2 - Bound and de-cycle provider directory traversal

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/resource-exhaustion
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0452
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupFileImport.kt:L38-L79,L88-L141` in SAF tree breadth-first traversal
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L2909-L2953`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ImportTreeScanner.kt:L7-L50`, and `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/ImportTreeScannerTest.kt:L8-L78`
- Evidence: Both scanners enqueue every child reported with the directory MIME type but retain no visited or active document-ID set. A DocumentsProvider can return the current parent, an ancestor, or two IDs that reference one another, making the breadth-first loop query and enqueue the same directories forever. Even an acyclic provider can expose arbitrary breadth and depth: every directory row is materialized, every importable URI is retained, counters use wrapping `Int`, and there is no directory, row, result, depth, elapsed-time, provider-query, or cancellation budget. The caller runs this non-suspending traversal on an IO coroutine and shows its large-directory warning only after traversal returns; cancelling the activity scope does not insert a cancellation check into the loop. Focused tests exercise only one batch's classification and the post-scan warning threshold, not traversal identity, budgets, cancellation, or provider behavior.
- Trigger: Choose a tree from a custom, remote, or malformed DocumentsProvider that reports its parent as a directory child, returns an A-to-B-to-A directory cycle, repeats IDs through multiple parents, stalls individual queries, or exposes an extremely large directory graph
- Impact: One selected provider tree can keep an IO worker and provider queries active indefinitely, grow the queue, row lists, and retained URI list until memory exhaustion, duplicate later imports, and leave the launcher stuck in scanning state even after navigation or lifecycle cancellation.
- Expected: Directory traversal visits each stable document identity at most once, obeys explicit depth, directory, row, result, byte-independent work, query-time, and elapsed-time limits, cooperates with coroutine cancellation, and returns a bounded diagnostic failure before any import begins.
- Suggested fix: Move traversal into a cancellable suspend helper, pass a `CancellationSignal` to provider queries, check coroutine activity between rows and directories, and maintain a visited set keyed by validated provider document identity. Apply conservative shared limits for depth, directories, rows, retained candidates, and elapsed work with checked counters; reject cycles, repeated identities, null or malformed IDs, and limit exhaustion rather than warning only after completion.
- Validation: Use a controllable DocumentsProvider for self, ancestor, two-node, duplicate-parent, and changing-ID cycles; huge breadth and depth; repeated importable IDs; null and malformed metadata; slow and nonreturning queries; cancellation at every query and row boundary; and counts around every limit. Require bounded time and memory, one visit and candidate per identity, checked counters, prompt cancellation, no import on incomplete scans, and a recoverable user-visible diagnostic while ordinary finite trees retain deterministic order.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-02. Folder import now uses one suspendable breadth-first traversal with validated unique document IDs, explicit depth, directory, row, and result limits, coroutine checks between directories and rows, a `CancellationSignal` and deadline for every provider query, and a whole-scan deadline. Any repeat, malformed identity, exhausted limit, or deadline stops before import and produces a recoverable user-visible diagnostic; lifecycle cancellation is propagated. Focused tests cover deterministic finite traversal, a two-node cycle, result, row, depth, and directory boundaries, and cancellation. The focused JVM suite, scoped Kotlin quality checks, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0048: P2 - Stream the exact bounded audio window into Chromaprint

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: performance/resource-lifetime
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0020, R1-CHUNK-0035, R1-CHUNK-0117
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_cd.c:L377-L406` and `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_fingerprint.c:L118-L166`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/fingerprint_gen.c:L26-L28,L121-L135`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/pcm_decoders.c:L258-L275`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L149-L182,L229-L376`, and `game_data/fingerprint_disc_tracks.ps1:L144-L203`
- Evidence: For every audio track, the CLI allocates and reads the complete raw track after already streaming it once for SHA-1. The Android JNI path likewise allocates and reads the complete raw track into one contiguous buffer. Both call `fingerprint_from_cd_sectors`, which allocates an equally large PCM buffer and copies the complete track again, even though `fingerprint_from_pcm` feeds Chromaprint at most the first 120 seconds. The checked-in corpus already contains five-minute tracks requiring about 50.5 MiB per copy and at least 101 MiB at the two-copy peak. A valid 74-minute single-track audio CD requires about 747 MiB per copy and roughly 1.5 GiB before Chromaprint's own state; the JNI's nominal 750 MiB single-buffer cap still permits that two-copy peak, making an ordinary supported layout especially likely to terminate the Android process
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0117): The common PCM API supports mono and decoder-selected sample rates, but its supposed 120-second bound is the fixed total-int16 constant `120 * 44100 * 2`. It therefore feeds 240 seconds of 44.1-kHz mono, only 110.25 seconds of 48-kHz stereo, and other format-dependent windows. In contrast, the pinned Chromaprint 1.5.1 fpcalc computes its default bound as `120 * sample_rate` per-channel frames and multiplies by the actual channel count only at `chromaprint_feed`. The common wrapper also narrows the decoder's `size_t total_samples` to `int` and multiplies that signed value by `channels` before clamping, so sufficiently long decoded inputs encounter narrowing or signed-overflow behavior even though only a bounded prefix is needed.
- Trigger: Fingerprint a CUE whose audio track is long, especially a single-track or otherwise extended CD-DA image; fingerprint a supported long mono or non-44.1-kHz audio file; or run the batch on a host with limited available memory
- Impact: Metadata generation performs a redundant full-track read and copy, can consume gigabytes for valid media, and may report out-of-memory instead of fingerprinting a disc; on Android the same valid import can exceed the app's memory limit and kill the process even though the algorithm intentionally needs only a bounded prefix. Supported file formats can also generate a different time window than fpcalc solely because of channel count or sample rate, reducing interoperability and making work or encoded output longer or shorter than the documented policy.
- Expected: SHA-1 remains a streaming full-track operation, while Chromaprint receives only its documented bounded prefix through a bounded buffer or streaming feed; full duration is derived safely from the validated sector count without retaining the complete track
- Suggested fix: Combine hashing and prefix collection in one sector pass, cap retained PCM to the exact 120-second frame count, remove the full-buffer copy in the CD wrapper, and expose a streaming or bounded-prefix fingerprint API with explicit frame and channel units. Keep checked arithmetic for sector, byte, and sample conversions
- Validation: Measure peak resident memory and read volume for current five-minute tracks and synthetic 120-second, 121-second, and valid full-disc tracks; assert memory stays near the fixed prefix size, each source sector is read once, duration remains full-track duration, and fingerprints match the current implementation for valid complete inputs. Add mono and stereo vectors at 22.05, 44.1, and 48 kHz just below, at, and above 120 seconds plus inputs beyond the signed-count boundaries; require an exact 120-second fpcalc-compatible prefix, checked rejection of unrepresentable lengths, and no extra decode or allocation work.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. The CD CLI now collects at most the first 9,000 sectors while performing the existing full-track SHA-1 pass, so every sector is read once and the retained audio is fixed at 120 seconds. Android JNI reads only that same bounded prefix, and the CD wrapper fingerprints it directly without a second full-track PCM allocation while deriving duration from the checked full sector count. MP3, OGG, and FLAC file and memory decoders now retain and decode only the requested prefix while preserving the full frame count for duration. The shared API uses `size_t` frame units, computes the fpcalc-compatible `min(total_frames, 120 * sample_rate) * channels` feed with checked arithmetic, and rejects unrepresentable feed counts and durations. Nine focused fingerprint tests passed, including all requested rates and channel counts around the boundary, file and memory prefix parity, and a 74-minute CD duration from the fixed prefix. The fpcalc comparison, fingerprint-audio enumeration integration, all 19 native CTests, scoped code quality, Android debug assembly for all three native ABIs, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0049: P2 - Reject partial fingerprint manifests when the CLI fails

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/test-tool
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0020, R1-CHUNK-0072
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/fingerprint_disc_tracks.ps1:L176-L203`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_cd.c:L351-L358,L382-L390,L408-L428,L439-L441`, `game_data/fingerprint_disc_tracks.ps1:L215-L263`, and the checked-in `game_data/CD images/*/track_fingerprints.json` corpus
- Evidence: The native CLI emits a JSON record for each open, allocation, or fingerprint failure, increments `errors`, and exits nonzero. The batch script captures that exit code but, whenever stdout contains even one JSON line, writes all lines to `track_fingerprints.json`, reports the disc successful, and never inspects the exit code or per-record `error` fields. Because the CLI emits JSON on its ordinary failure paths, almost every partial failure takes this success branch. A later run skips the existing manifest, the AcoustID phase silently ignores records without a fingerprint, and the script has no final nonzero exit based on its failures list
- Trigger: Process a multi-track disc where any BIN cannot open, an audio allocation fails, Chromaprint fails, or one track otherwise emits an error while at least one JSON line is produced
- Impact: The canonical generation workflow publishes and then reuses an incomplete fingerprint manifest while reporting success; missing tracks can flow into database generation and duplicate matching without identifying the failed disc or forcing regeneration
- Expected: A manifest is committed only when the CLI exits zero, every expected parsed track appears exactly once, every audio record has a valid fingerprint and duration, and no record contains an error; failures leave no reusable final artifact
- Suggested fix: Parse stdout strictly into a temporary manifest, check the native exit code first, reject every error record and count or identity mismatch, atomically replace the final file only after complete validation, make the script exit nonzero when any disc fails, and do not treat an existing partial file as a completed skip
- Validation: Run a multi-track fixture with one successful track and one injected open, allocation, or fingerprint failure; assert the script reports failure, exits nonzero, performs no AcoustID request, and leaves no final manifest. Retain a complete fixture that atomically publishes the expected track count and is safely skipped on a later run
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0072): `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L719-L757` in `fingerprint_audio` exit handling and sidecar publication
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0072): The same native CLI returns nonzero after omitting any file it cannot fingerprint. The mission script throws only when that nonzero result contains zero successful records; with a mixed result it deliberately warns, converts the successful subset into tracks, and writes `chromaprint_info.json5`. A later run accepts the matching `source_sha1` and does not fingerprint the omitted member again, so one transient decoder failure becomes a reusable incomplete mission manifest.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0072): Use a mission containing one valid audio member and one member that makes the native decoder fail; assert nonzero batch status, no final sidecar or database update, and successful complete publication after the bad member is repaired.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. Disc fingerprint generation now rejects a nonzero native exit before parsing or enrichment, strictly validates a JSON-array manifest against the CUE's unique track numbers and types, rejects error records, invalid SHA-1 values, missing tracks, duplicates, and incomplete audio fingerprints, and atomically replaces the final file only after validation. Existing manifests receive the same validation before they can be skipped, failed forced reruns preserve a prior complete artifact, and any disc failure exits nonzero before AcoustID. Mission generation already checked the native exit and staged directory publication; its sidecars now add a strict `complete: true` marker to prevent matching legacy or partial sidecars from being reused. The new disc-and-mission publication regression passed partial nonzero output, zero-exit missing and error records, complete one- and two-track publication, safe skip, invalid-cache regeneration, prior-artifact preservation, mission repair, and mission completeness cases. Extraction-cache provenance, AcoustID regeneration, fingerprint-audio enumeration, scoped PowerShell quality, parsing, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0100: P2 - Let explicit AcoustID lookup recover from transient failures

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: reliability/retry-state
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0048, R1-CHUNK-0085, R1-CHUNK-0396
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCache.kt:L32-L36,L139-L154` in persisted web-lookup completion state
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L878-L908,L1028-L1073,L1169-L1193`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AcoustIdClient.kt:L55-L142`, and `android/ai tool plans/music/plan_zip_music_metadata_browser_study_20260609.md:L378-L387`
- Evidence: `hasAcoustIdLookup` is true for every nonblank status, including `failed`, and `lookupTrack` immediately returns such an entry without calling the service again. The timestamp is stored but never used for expiry or retry. More broadly, `AcoustIdClient.lookupFingerprint` returns the same null for a genuine empty result and for exhausted network exceptions, HTTP errors other than retryable server responses, malformed responses, or API error statuses; the caller normally persists all of those as `no_match`. Although the plan intentionally caches failure status to avoid accidental repeated traffic, both the per-track `Lookup` button and explicit `Lookup all` action are then unable to retry until archive identity changes or app data is cleared.
- Trigger: Perform the first explicit lookup while offline, during a timeout or service error, with a temporarily rejected key or malformed response, then restore normal service and press `Lookup` or `Lookup all` again
- Impact: Web identification becomes permanently disabled for that track and can misleadingly report a transient service failure as an authoritative no-match, with no in-app recovery path
- Expected: Success and genuine no-match are distinguishable from retryable failure, automatic operations respect backoff, and an explicit user action can retry failed or expired results
- Suggested fix: Return a typed AcoustID result separating match, authoritative no-match, retryable failure, permanent configuration error, and cancellation. Persist attempt time and retry policy, let explicit actions override cached failures, expire no-match according to a documented policy if desired, and never swallow coroutine cancellation into a cache status.
- Validation: Inject offline I/O, timeout, 429 and 5xx exhaustion, 4xx key error, API error JSON, malformed JSON, empty valid results, match, and coroutine cancellation; retry each after recovery through per-track and bulk actions and assert only authoritative states suppress requests according to policy
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0085): `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCacheTest.kt:L99-L126`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0085): The sole AcoustID cache test records only `ACOUSTID_STATUS_OK` and verifies successful persistence. It has no failed or no-match status, elapsed-time, explicit retry, service recovery, or cancellation case, so the permanently suppressing failure state remains untested.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0085): Persist each typed success, no-match, retryable failure, configuration failure, and cancellation outcome, then invoke per-track and bulk lookup after recovery and policy expiry; require only authoritative unexpired states to suppress the request.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0396): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AcoustIdClient.kt:L55-L142` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L878-L908` in request execution and caller-side status publication
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0396): `lookupFingerprint` performs synchronous `OkHttpClient.execute` inside a suspend function without linking coroutine cancellation to `Call.cancel`, then catches every `Exception`, including `CancellationException`. Its maintained caller also catches every `Exception` and records `ACOUSTID_STATUS_FAILED`. Cancellation during a retry delay therefore reaches the caller as an ordinary failure and writes the permanently suppressing status; cancellation while the blocking call is in flight cannot stop its network and parsing work and can allow cache side effects before the canceled `withContext` returns.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0396): Cancel per-track and bulk lookups during rate-limit delay, DNS/connect, response read, retry backoff, JSON parsing, and immediately before cache publication. Require prompt `Call.cancel`, no retry or cache mutation after cancellation, no failed or no-match status, and no retained IO worker or request after dialog dismissal.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. AcoustID lookup now returns typed match, authoritative no-match, retryable-failure, and configuration-failure outcomes; retryable HTTP, I/O, malformed-response, and API-rate failures receive bounded backoff, while other client and API errors remain distinguishable. Only match and no-match cache states suppress another explicit request, so both per-track and bulk actions retry new, legacy, transient, and configuration failure states after recovery. The production transport is coroutine-cancellable, cancels its OkHttp call, propagates cancellation through request and backoff paths, and checks coroutine activity immediately before cache publication. Six focused client tests and all seven fingerprint-cache tests passed; the full JVM suite passed 554 tests with one skip, debug APK assembly built all three native ABIs, scoped Kotlin quality checks passed, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0170: P2 - Bind extraction caches to an unambiguous source identity

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-integrity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0067, R1-CHUNK-0068, R1-CHUNK-0069, R1-CHUNK-0070, R1-CHUNK-0072
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/extract_all_cds.ps1:L81-L110` and `game_data/extract_all_gog.ps1:L121-L139` in source selection and cache reuse
- Related: `game_data/generate_regression_specs.ps1:L225-L249,L290,L356-L365`, BR-0008, and BR-0020
- Evidence: The CD helper skips solely when `data_tracks` and `track_hashes.json` exist, before it identifies or hashes the current CUE, ISO, referenced BIN files, extractor binary, or extraction policy. If several CUE or ISO files exist, it silently chooses unsorted element zero and prefers any CUE over every ISO. The GOG helper keys its reusable directory only by extensionless basename and also stores no installer or tool identity, so replacing an installer in place or adding same-basename `.exe` and `.pkg` sources reuses old or shared output. The spec generator then hashes every current CD source or the current installer while deriving expected files from the stale cache, allowing a newly generated spec to bind one source generation to another generation's extracted bytes. The current corpus happens to have one CUE or ISO in each of 34 folders and four unique GOG basenames, but the ordinary same-name replacement path remains unguarded.
- Trigger: Replace a CUE, BIN, ISO, EXE, or PKG without deleting its output; change extraction code or filtering while retaining the directory; add multiple disc descriptors to one folder; or place `.exe` and `.pkg` installers with the same basename together
- Impact: The scripts can report and hash old or wrong extracted data as the result of a different current source, and regenerated regression specs can record the new source digest alongside stale expected files, hiding media changes and producing misleading pass or failure evidence
- Expected: A cache is reusable only when one explicitly selected source set, all referenced payload digests, extractor build identity, and extraction-policy version match its recorded provenance; ambiguous input sets fail rather than depend on enumeration order
- Suggested fix: Define one deterministic input-selection contract and reject unsupported multiplicity. Publish a completion manifest beside each cache containing canonical source names and SHA-256 values, referenced CUE payload identities, tool or source revision, policy version, and output inventory; validate it before skipping. Include the full source identity in GOG cache ownership, preflight basename collisions, and coordinate transactional publication and prior-cache preservation with BR-0020.
- Validation: Replace each source type with same-name same-size and different-size content, edit a CUE reference or BIN, change the policy or tool revision, supply CUE-plus-ISO and multi-CUE folders, and add same-basename EXE/PKG installers. Assert stale inputs re-extract or fail clearly, ambiguous inputs never choose by incidental order, valid unchanged caches skip, and generated specs bind exactly the current source and output generation.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0068): `game_data/extract_dos_demos.ps1:L51-L89,L115-L134` in package definition and cache reuse, with the production model at `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DemoInstallerPackages.kt:L63-L78`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0068): Reuse is based only on output-directory name and the presence of any one major game file; it records no archive digest, DOSBox identity, scripted-input revision, expected-file policy, or exact output manifest. The helper also duplicates the production package table but omits the required D2 demo file from both D2 polling lists. The current four archives match the production digests, but replacing one in place or changing either table leaves an old or policy-incomplete cache eligible for reuse.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0068): Replace an archive with same-name same-size and different-size content, mutate the DOSBox binary, scripted inputs, and expected-file policy, and introduce drift in either package table; require re-extraction or a clear failure unless a completion manifest proves the exact source, tool, policy, and outputs still match.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0069): `game_data/extract_mac_cd.ps1:L70-L98,L387-L448` in CUE selection, cache reuse, and publication
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0069): Reuse depends only on the existence of `data_tracks`, before identifying or hashing the CUE, referenced BIN, Python, machfs, unar, or extraction policy. Multiple CUE files are reduced to unsorted element zero, and no output inventory is recorded. Replacing media or tools, changing policy, or leaving an empty or partial directory therefore reuses bytes from an unrelated or incomplete generation.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0069): Replace the CUE, referenced BIN, each tool, and policy independently, supply multiple CUEs, and retain empty, partial, extra, or stale outputs; require deterministic source selection and a completion manifest binding exact source, dependency, policy, and output identities before reuse.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0070): `game_data/extract_mac_demos.ps1:L48-L94,L115-L124,L171-L191` in duplicated package identity, cache reuse, and oracle provenance
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0070): The helper duplicates six package names and expected-file lists but never validates the documented archive SHA-256 values. Any one existing game file makes a cache reusable regardless of current archive bytes, unar identity, extraction policy, or the remaining expected files. The oracle records only an archive basename and the generic label `unar`, not the archive digest or exact tool identity, so replacing a same-name source or tool can silently redefine regression truth. All six local inputs matched the README hashes and the current oracle matched its three local output trees during review, but the script does not enforce those identities.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0070): Replace each archive and cached tool independently with same-name same-size and different-size bytes, change the expected-file policy, and retain partial output. Require re-extraction or a clear failure unless a manifest binds the exact source hash, verified tool version and digest, policy, and complete output inventory; require oracle records to carry and validate the same provenance.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0072): `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L670-L689` in existing-sidecar cache acceptance
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0072): A cached mission sidecar is rejected only when `source_sha1` is both present and unequal. A syntactically valid legacy, manually edited, or partially migrated sidecar with no hash takes the unchanged-source branch: `-SkipAcoustId` accepts it immediately, while the normal path reuses its fingerprints and rewrites it with the current source hash without reading the archive. The new hash can therefore bless metadata that was never derived from those source bytes.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0072): Remove, empty, and malform each source-identity field in an otherwise valid sidecar, then replace the archive contents under the same name; require re-extraction or explicit cache rejection and never allow reuse to manufacture a current completion identity.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31 in the worktree. All extraction completion manifests now bind canonical SHA-256 source identities, referenced CUE payloads, exact extractor and dependency identities, the shared helper identity, an explicit policy version, and a complete output inventory before cache reuse. Disc selection rejects unsupported multiplicity while retaining the one validated CUE-plus-ISO form where the CUE's sole payload is that ISO. CD track hashes are stored inside the validated generation and copied outward only from that bound artifact; regression-spec generation rejects source, policy, script, or output drift. GOG same-basename EXE/PKG inputs fail preflight. DOS and Mac demo archives enforce their maintained hashes, and Mac oracle output carries archive, tool, and policy provenance. Mission sidecars require matching source name, SHA-1, and SHA-256 and cannot bless missing legacy identity. Focused provenance, CD and GOG batch, publication, spec-generation, fingerprint-budget, and AcoustID tests passed; bounded extraction, dependency verification, extraction workflow, all 34 current regression-spec validation, scoped code quality, PowerShell parsing, `git diff --check`, and the complete 19-test native extraction CTest suite passed. The broader paired-game native host wrapper exceeded the 120-second command cap without output; no product C or C++ changed, and the directly relevant maintained native suite completed successfully.

### BR-0004: P1 - Bridge runtime game state out of the separate game process

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness
- Found by: R1-PREFLIGHT-002
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/multiplayer/MatchmakingService.kt:L453` in `startGameStateUpdates` and `pollAndSendGameState`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:L52`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L1717`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L702`, `server/src/ws_handler.rs:L1379`, `server/src/lobby.rs:L165`, and `server/src/stats.rs:L125`
- Evidence: `MainActivity` runs in `:game`, while the WebSocket-owning `MatchmakingService` singleton and its `activityRef` remain in the default process with `SetupActivity`. The host starts the update job at `MatchmakingService.kt:L1109`, but every poll returns at L478-L480 because that process's activity is not `MainActivity`. The separate game-process singleton receives `MainActivity` at L702 but has no WebSocket and never starts the job. On the server, only `UPDATE_GAME_STATE` transitions `Starting` to `InGame`, populates the current level, and refreshes `last_state_update`; absent an update, the lobby is not late-joinable and stale cleanup removes it five minutes after creation. The same process split also makes the in-game proxy and connection-info providers at `MainActivity.kt:L1437-L1443` read empty game-process singletons
- Trigger: Start an online matchmaking game as host and remain in the native game process for the first three-second state poll and subsequent 30-second polls
- Impact: The lobby remains `Starting`, mid-game joining never becomes available, live level and player counts are never published, in-game network diagnostics omit proxy and connection data, and the server eventually expires the active lobby with `LOBBY_EXPIRED`
- Expected: The default-process matchmaking owner receives authoritative runtime state from `:game` and keeps the server lobby and diagnostic snapshot current for the lifetime of the multiplayer game
- Suggested fix: Define one explicit same-UID IPC boundary. Prefer a bound service or narrowly scoped non-exported messages that let `:game` publish native state and request snapshots from the default-process matchmaking and proxy owner. Keep the WebSocket and UDP proxy under one owner, remove process-local reads that pretend the singleton is shared, and make service restart or process death produce an explicit disconnected state
- Validation: Run an instrumented two-process host session for longer than five minutes and assert that the server observes `Starting -> InGame`, fresh updates at the configured interval, correct current level and player count, a late join succeeds, proxy diagnostics are non-empty when proxying, and stopping either process cleans up without a stale lobby
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. The existing non-exported default-process foreground service now exposes a same-UID Messenger boundary. The `:game` process reads native host state on its main thread after three seconds and every 30 seconds, publishes complete network state to the default-process `MatchmakingService`, and asynchronously requests current proxy and connection diagnostic snapshots for its overlays. The WebSocket, matchmaking state, and UDP proxy remain under one default-process owner; `MainActivity` no longer reads a process-local matchmaking singleton. Session gating accepts state only from a registered host, malformed diagnostic snapshots fail to empty state, owner disconnect clears cached diagnostics, and clean unbind or game-process death sends `END_GAME` once for an online hosted lobby, including death before periodic updates are enabled. Focused state, diagnostic, and lifecycle tests and the complete Android JVM suite passed. An emulator confirmed distinct default and `:game` processes, the live cross-process binding, binding removal after killing only `:game`, and service removal after final stop. Scoped quality, all 17 native extraction tests, both Windows game builds, and JDK 21 debug APK builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. A live five-minute online match and late join were not available in the local smoke environment; independent P1 verification remains a campaign closure action.

### BR-0020: P2 - Fail import when any requested archive file fails extraction

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0010, R1-CHUNK-0011, R1-CHUNK-0015, R1-CHUNK-0017, R1-CHUNK-0019, R1-CHUNK-0021, R1-CHUNK-0024, R1-CHUNK-0025, R1-CHUNK-0026, R1-CHUNK-0027, R1-CHUNK-0028, R1-CHUNK-0029, R1-CHUNK-0030, R1-CHUNK-0031, R1-CHUNK-0067, R1-CHUNK-0068, R1-CHUNK-0069, R1-CHUNK-0070, R1-CHUNK-0071
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_gog_import.c:L256-L298` in `extract_inno_archive`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/sow_extract.c:L670-L750` in `sow_extract_impl`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/inno_reader.c:L1666-L1686,L2057-L2188`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L450-L529` in `pkg_extract_all`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/iso9660_reader.c:L413-L507` in `iso_extract_files_from_source`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/hfs_reader.c:L669-L725` in `hfs_extract_file`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_gog.c:L98-L188`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_cd.c:L338-L393,L533-L586,L608-L644`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/mac_hfs_extract.c:L103-L130,L133-L195`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/mac_hfs_extract.h:L8-L18`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.h:L58-L66`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/iso9660_reader.h:L73-L110`, `game_data/extract_all_gog.ps1:L121-L170`, `game_data/extract_all_cds.ps1:L81-L95,L119-L153`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_disc_import.c:L311-L392,L439-L547`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/GogImportBridge.kt:L70-L92`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L684-L819,L941-L1035`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupFileImport.kt:L280-L323`
- Evidence: The Inno loop counts each failure but returns `-1` only when no file succeeded; one successful file followed by any number of failed requested files returns a positive count. The Inno reader itself logs a GOG Galaxy external-size mismatch but returns success, and its buffered output path ignores `fclose`, so a truncated filtered file or delayed persistence failure can also be counted as extracted. The SOW loop similarly converts parser errors to end-of-input, continues after allocation, read, decompression, unsupported-method, and open failures, and returns the number of earlier successes. Its write path does not check `fwrite` or `fclose` at all and increments `extracted` even when the requested bytes were not persisted; the CD CLI treats every nonnegative SOW result, including zero or a partial count, as success. PKG extraction skips an entry whose destination cannot be opened, ignores `fclose`, and returns the count of other files; if every open fails it returns zero, which `extract_gog` reports as successful completion. The ISO reader likewise breaks an individual file's transfer on a sector read or output write error, leaves the truncated destination in place, increments `extracted`, and proceeds; destination-open failures are skipped. HFS extraction also writes directly to the final path, leaves a truncated destination after a fork read or output write failure, and ignores `fclose`; the Mac loose-file fallback returns on the first failed file without rolling back files it already completed. The Mac wrapper's public header exposes only one integer result and does not distinguish a missing `Install Descent` archive, zero matching STi2 entries, nested parse or extraction failure, or cancellation. The implementation sends every nonpositive nested result into direct-HFS fallback even though the plan specifies fallback when no installer is found; if a nested extraction writes early files and fails later, a positive loose-file fallback result masks that failure and publishes the mixed partial output as success. `extract_cd` reports any positive count as successful BIN/CUE extraction, treats every nonnegative standalone-ISO result including zero as success, and does not increment its error count when a listed BIN/CUE track extracts zero files. The batch scripts write the result manifests before checking exit status and skip existing output directories and manifests on later runs, so residue from a failed run can later be treated as complete. Kotlin likewise treats positive native counts as successful imports and does not compare every output with the analyzed or archive entry list
- Trigger: Import an Inno, SOW, PKG, ISO, or HFS source where an early file succeeds and a later requested file fails; corrupt a Mac `Install Descent` archive after one matching member while leaving at least one matching loose HFS file; use an unwritable output directory; extract a Galaxy entry whose filtered size differs from `external_size`; or inject a source read, destination write, or close failure
- Impact: The launcher reports a completed import and activates follow-on setup with an incomplete or inconsistent game-data set, while the only indication of missing files is a native log line
- Expected: Success means every selected file completed and passed its extraction checks; partial output is either rolled back or returned as an explicit failure with a machine-readable list of failed files
- Suggested fix: Stop on the first requested-file failure and return an error distinct from zero matches, stage outputs transactionally before replacing the set, and propagate the failed filename and reason to Kotlin. If partial imports are intentionally supported, represent that state explicitly and require caller validation before enabling Done
