- Trigger: Review, debug, or modify the prominently named root implementation instead of the compiled copy, or later add it to a source list without first discovering the duplicate definitions
- Impact: A plausible fix can compile nowhere and have no runtime effect; copying behavior from the stale file can reintroduce a JNI use-after-release or omit current import features; compiling both files produces duplicate native symbols
- Expected: Each JNI surface has one clearly built source of truth, and repository search cannot lead maintainers to an obsolete full implementation
- Suggested fix: Delete the root `jni_disc_import.c`, retain the implementation under `extract/`, and update any historical or operational references that still point to the old path. If a forwarding artifact is required, make it non-compilable documentation rather than a second implementation
- Validation: Confirm each `DiscImportBridge` native symbol has exactly one source definition, both D1 and D2 CMake targets compile that file, a clean Android build succeeds, and ISO, BIN/CUE, Mac, SOW, and StuffIt import tests still invoke the retained implementation
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. The obsolete 408-line root `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_disc_import.c` was deleted, leaving `extract/jni_disc_import.c` as the only implementation of the launcher bridge and preserving its complete current ISO-image, BIN/CUE, Mac HFS/STi2, SOW append-mode, StuffIt, callback, and shared-extension behavior. Both Android targets now reference one `DXX_DISC_IMPORT_JNI_SOURCE` variable, and configuration fails if a second root implementation reappears. Repository-wide source inspection finds exactly ten `DiscImportBridge` JNI definitions, each occurring once, matching Kotlin's ten native declarations. The built arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 D1 and D2 shared libraries each export exactly those ten bridge symbols. Scoped CMake quality, all 15 native support suites covering CUE/ISO, Mac, SOW, and StuffIt behavior, and clean debug APK builds for all three ABIs passed.

### BR-0028: P1 - Serialize MIDI seek with rendering

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: concurrency/resource-lifetime
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0013
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_midi_preview.c:L63-L78` in `nativeSeek` and `nativeGetState`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/midi_preview.c:L287-L379,L636-L694` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MidiBytesPreviewDialog.kt:L63-L75,L155-L168`
- Evidence: The Compose slider calls `nativeSeek` on the main thread while the MIDI render thread continuously runs `render_midi_frames`. Seek resets and reconfigures the non-thread-safe TinySoundFont instance, rewinds and advances the shared linked-list cursor, rewrites the shared playback double, and resets ring positions while rendering simultaneously mutates the same synth, cursor, time, and ring. No mutex, command queue, pause handshake, or atomic ownership protects these operations. The 100 ms state poll also reads the concurrently written `double s_playback_msec` and related plain integers without synchronization
- Trigger: Start a MIDI or HMP preview and release the position slider while playback is active, especially while the render thread is dispatching notes; repeated slider releases widen the race window
- Impact: Concurrent synth and cursor mutation is undefined native behavior that can corrupt preview state or heap internals, crash the launcher, return torn or nonsensical positions, and produce stale or corrupted audio
- Expected: The render thread has exclusive ownership of synth, message cursor, playback time, and ring reset; UI controls submit ordered commands and state queries consume a synchronized snapshot
- Suggested fix: Route seek, pause, resume, and stop through a render-thread command queue or guard all shared playback state with one lifecycle mutex and stop rendering during seek. Publish position, duration, and state through atomics or a locked snapshot, and define callback ordering during ring reset
- Validation: Add a preview test that performs rapid seeks while playing and while paused, verifies bounded monotonic state after each completed seek, and stress it under ThreadSanitizer where available plus AddressSanitizer on an Android or host OpenSL test shim
- Remediation status: Code-complete on 2026-07-21 and independently verified on 2026-07-27. The live implementation serializes JNI lifecycle and control calls, guards synth, MIDI cursor, playback time, and state snapshots with one playback mutex shared by rendering and seek, serializes ring reset against the OpenSL reader with a dedicated non-blocking callback guard, and publishes callback enablement atomically. Stop releases the playback lock before joining the render thread
- Resolution: Fixed and independently verified on 2026-07-27. Frozen-source tracing confirmed that active rendering, slider seek, and the 100 ms state poll could concurrently access the synth, event cursor, playback clock, state flags, and ring indices. Live-source tracing found one acyclic lock order: controls take `s_control_mutex` then `s_playback_mutex`, seek alone takes `s_ring_reset_mutex` inside that transaction, rendering takes only `s_playback_mutex`, and the OpenSL callback only try-locks `s_ring_reset_mutex`. Teardown disables output and releases the playback lock before joining, then reacquires it for cleanup. The seven synchronization contract tests passed before and after scoped code quality, all 15 native support suites passed, and the debug APK was rebuilt with D1 and D2 libraries for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64. ThreadSanitizer and a maintained on-device rapid-seek stress run remained unavailable

### BR-0030: P1 - Check MIDI audio initialization results before use

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0013
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_midi_preview.c:L29-L39` in `nativeStart`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/midi_preview.c:L382-L399,L419-L497,L531-L602`
- Evidence: The JNI bridge reports the native start result, but `osl_init` checks only object-creation calls. It ignores every `Realize`, `GetInterface`, callback registration, play-state, and initial `Enqueue` result, then dereferences the returned engine, play, and buffer-queue interfaces unconditionally. If realization or interface acquisition fails, the next function-table call can use a null or unusable interface. `render_thread_start` also returns no status: a failed `pthread_create` is logged while `midi_preview_start` still returns success with OpenSL running and no producer
- Trigger: Start a preview while OpenSL cannot realize an engine or player, cannot provide the required buffer-queue interface, rejects a queue operation, or the process cannot create another native thread because audio or thread resources are exhausted
- Impact: A recoverable audio initialization failure can become a native null-interface crash; thread-creation failure is falsely reported as playing and leaves an initialized silent player until a later stop
- Expected: Every fallible OpenSL and pthread step is checked before its result is used, partial objects are destroyed in reverse order, and start returns false with the preview fully stopped
- Suggested fix: Make `osl_init` and `render_thread_start` use a single checked cleanup path, verify each required interface and operation, set global objects only after successful initialization, and propagate a distinct failure to JNI. Keep stop idempotent for every partial state
- Validation: Wrap OpenSL and pthread entry points in a testable adapter, inject failure at every initialization step, and assert no null call, success result, live thread, queued buffer, or retained engine, mix, player, MIDI, or ring state remains; retain a real-device successful playback test
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. OpenSL initialization now builds engine, output-mix, player, play, and buffer-queue state in local handles; checks every create, realize, interface, callback-registration, initial enqueue, and play-state result before the next dependent use; rejects successful calls that return null required handles; and publishes globals only after the complete graph is usable. Failure destroys the partial player, mix, and engine in reverse order. Render-thread creation now reports failure, clears its running flag, and makes start invoke the same full-stop cleanup used by OpenSL failure. Cleanup is idempotent and nulls the freed MIDI buffer. Five new source contracts cover required result checks, unpublished partial handles, reverse cleanup, checked initial queues before playback, and propagated thread failure, extending the synchronization suite to 12 passing tests. Scoped quality, all 15 native support suites, and debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. The maintained 42-step D2 music-track emulator integration passed against the final APK, verifying OpenSL startup, advancing HMP playback, duration reporting, stop, and the surrounding music controls

### BR-0065: P1 - Convert Java and native strings correctly at JNI boundaries

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: compatibility/jni-boundary
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0027, R1-CHUNK-0028, R1-CHUNK-0029, R1-CHUNK-0030, R1-CHUNK-0031, R1-CHUNK-0037, R1-CHUNK-0038, R1-CHUNK-0039, R1-CHUNK-0040, R1-CHUNK-0041
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_disc_import.c:L26-L52,L201-L208,L293-L307,L427-L433` in `build_iso_listing_array`, `nativeGetCueTitles`, `extract_progress_cb`, and `nativeScanSowFiles`; `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_gog_import.c:L147-L176,L214-L232` in `nativeListFiles` and `gog_progress_cb`; `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_level_metadata.cpp:L1116-L1153` in `nativeAnalyzeLevelMetadata`; `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_main.c:L49-L78,L301-L356,L560-L585,L604-L686,L840-L918` in startup-string consumption, level-preview dispatch, introspection and automation, console JSON, and multiplayer setup; `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_music_control.c:L187-L244,L250-L304,L404-L415` in track names, state JSON, and track lists; and `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_resume_save.cpp:L500-L529,L611-L719,L745-L849` in resume and Save Explorer JSON output
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/cue_parser.c:L66-L79,L102-L109`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/iso9660_reader.c:L183-L245,L329-L358`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/sow_extract.c:L38-L121,L616-L658`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/mac_hfs_extract.h:L8-L18`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/mac_hfs_extract.c:L133-L169`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L294-L345,L358-L364,L466-L516`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.h:L25-L45`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/songs_android_shared.c:L202-L365`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/track_names.c:L25-L80,L137-L176,L235-L305`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DiscImportBridge.kt:L15-L20,L51-L67,L141-L148`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/GogImportBridge.kt:L13-L24,L38-L64`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L955-L1007,L1225-L1246`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L1684-L1725,L2646-L2659,L3520-L3555`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelPreviewActivity.kt:L117-L141,L211-L225`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicControlPanel.kt:L127-L155`, Android's JNI string guidance at `https://developer.android.com/ndk/guides/jni-tips`, and the JNI function specification at `https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/18/docs/specs/jni/functions.html`
- Evidence: `NewStringUTF` accepts Modified UTF-8, not arbitrary native bytes or ordinary UTF-8. Android's JNI guidance specifically requires file and network data to be filtered or converted before this call and states that CheckJNI aborts the VM on invalid Modified UTF-8. The import bridges pass CUE `TITLE` bytes, cleaned ISO identifier bytes, filesystem-derived SOW paths, CPIO entry names, and extractor filenames directly to `NewStringUTF` without knowing or validating their encoding. Ordinary UTF-8 encodes non-BMP characters with four bytes, which Modified UTF-8 does not accept, and the source formats can also contain legacy high bytes or malformed sequences. The level-metadata bridge adds the reverse mismatch: Kotlin builds a Unicode JSON `String`, `GetStringUTFChars` encodes it as Modified UTF-8, and the native code sends those bytes to nlohmann's standard UTF-8 JSON parser; a non-BMP request value is represented as Modified UTF-8 surrogate sequences and can be rejected as invalid JSON. Its response then passes nlohmann's standard UTF-8 `dump()` bytes, including native mission metadata, to `NewStringUTF`. The main bridge repeats both directions: replay, resume, pilot, preview-request, preview-data, introspection, automation, callsign, matchmaking identifier, host address, and mission values are obtained with `GetStringUTFChars` and used as native byte strings or paths, while preview error text, introspection output, and console JSON are passed directly from native strings to `NewStringUTF`. The music bridge additionally serializes CUE, fingerprint, mission, filesystem, and playlist-derived names as native JSON or a delimited byte string and sends those bytes through `NewStringUTF`; its bytewise JSON escapers preserve non-ASCII bytes without decoding or validation. The resume bridge likewise inserts filesystem paths and save-set path components into standard UTF-8 nlohmann JSON and sends the dumped bytes through `NewStringUTF`; its ASCII sanitizer does not cover those path fields. Bounded byte copies of callsign, identifier, host, mission, and music names can also split a multibyte sequence and retain an invalid native string. The bridges use returned references without consistently checking string creation or pending exceptions
- Trigger: Import or list a CUE, ISO, SOW, PKG, extracted archive entry, custom music file, fingerprint title, or mission soundtrack whose displayed name contains malformed bytes, a legacy-encoded high byte, or a non-BMP character encoded as standard four-byte UTF-8; open or refresh the music overlay or track label for that name; analyze level metadata whose Java request or native result contains a non-BMP source, mission, path, or metadata string; discover or list a save whose path or save-set directory contains a non-BMP UTF-8 name; launch a replay, resume, level preview, introspection, automation, multiplayer callsign, host, or mission value containing non-BMP text or crossing one of the fixed byte limits mid-sequence; or return native preview, introspection, or console text containing standard four-byte UTF-8
- Impact: A user-selected source can abort the app process under CheckJNI, including the default emulator configuration, produce mangled text or pending JNI exceptions on other runtimes, reject an otherwise valid level-metadata request as malformed JSON, or make a valid startup or preview file unreachable under the converted byte name; listing, progress, import, analysis, startup, preview, or introspection then fails at a text boundary that should decode or reject the value deterministically
- Expected: Java UTF-16 request strings are converted to validated standard UTF-8 before native JSON parsing; native UTF-8 and format-originated byte strings are decoded according to an explicit source encoding into validated UTF-16; and invalid input produces a checked operation error without a VM abort or partial result
- Suggested fix: Add bounded conversion helpers in both directions: read Java strings with `GetStringChars` and encode Unicode scalars as standard UTF-8 for native JSON, and validate or source-decode native text before emitting UTF-16 with `NewString`. Use the output helper for titles, listing paths, SOW paths, progress names, and serialized metadata; check every conversion, string creation, and pending exception before continuing. If a format intentionally supports only ASCII, reject non-ASCII bytes explicitly instead of passing them to `NewStringUTF`
- Validation: Run JNI instrumentation under CheckJNI with ASCII, accented legacy and UTF-8 names, embedded NUL where representable, BMP and non-BMP Java request fields, startup and preview paths, BMP and non-BMP native result fields and introspection JSON, overlong and truncated sequences, isolated continuation bytes, and format-invalid bytes across metadata JSON, CUE titles, ISO paths, SOW scan results, and progress callbacks; assert no VM abort, exact documented paths and round trips for valid text, explicit failure for invalid text, no partial Java arrays or output publication, and preservation of the original exception when allocation fails
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. Added one shared scalar-validating UTF-16 and standard UTF-8 codec plus JNI wrappers that use `GetStringChars` and `NewString`, preserve pending allocation exceptions, reject unpaired surrogates and embedded Java nulls before exposing C strings, and throw a checked `IllegalArgumentException` for malformed native UTF-8. Disc and GOG listing, title, progress, scan, extraction-path, metadata request and response, startup, preview, introspection, automation, console, multiplayer, music-state, track-list, resume, and Save Explorer boundaries now use the shared policy. Partial Java arrays stop immediately on failed string creation, and fixed native multiplayer fields truncate only at a UTF-8 code-point boundary. The host corpus covers ASCII, accented BMP, a four-byte non-BMP scalar, embedded-null policy in both directions, isolated surrogates, overlong, truncated, surrogate-encoded and out-of-range UTF-8, isolated continuation bytes, round trips, and insufficient output capacity. Scoped C, C++, CMake, and BOM quality passed; all 16 native suites passed; debug builds produced D1 and D2 libraries for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64; and the final 42-step music bridge emulator integration passed with no CheckJNI error

### BR-0067: P1 - Bound XAR TOC lengths before allocation and decompression

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/memory-safety
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0029, R1-CHUNK-0030
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L75-L95,L399-L424` in `xar_parse_header` and `pkg_open`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.h:L27-L48`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_gog_import.c:L147-L176,L307-L355`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L680-L820`
- Evidence: The XAR header controls two 64-bit TOC lengths, but validation requires only a nonzero compressed length. The reader narrows the compressed value to `size_t`, `int`, and zlib `uLong` independently, and converts the uncompressed value to `uLongf` before allocating `(size_t) toc_len + 1` without a checked addition. On a 32-bit Android ABI, an advertised uncompressed length of `UINT32_MAX` makes that allocation wrap to zero while zlib still receives a destination capacity of `UINT32_MAX`; on a 64-bit Android ABI, `UINT64_MAX` has the same shape. If the allocator returns its ordinary nonnull zero-size token, `uncompress` writes the attacker-supplied XML beyond that allocation. Large nonwrapping values also cause unbounded allocation, and neither length is checked against the package file span
- Trigger: Analyze a small XAR-looking PKG containing a valid compressed TOC but advertising `toc_uncompressed` as the platform `uLongf` maximum, or advertising compressed and uncompressed sizes beyond supported conversion and memory limits
- Impact: A user-selected installer can cause native heap overwrite and process termination during analysis before any game entry is shown; less extreme headers can force multi-gigabyte allocation attempts and memory-pressure denial of service
- Expected: Header lengths are representable in every allocator, read, and zlib type, fit the actual file, satisfy checked addition, and remain below a documented small TOC limit before allocation or decompression
- Suggested fix: Determine the regular-file size first; validate header size, supported version, compressed span, nonzero uncompressed size, checked `+1`, all type conversions, and a conservative TOC ceiling; read exactly with a loop; and inflate into a strictly bounded buffer while requiring the declared output length and terminal status. Keep broader archive budgets coordinated with BR-0018
- Validation: Add compressed and uncompressed lengths at zero, exact valid size, configured maximum, maximum plus one, `INT_MAX`, `UINT32_MAX`, `SIZE_MAX`, and `UINT64_MAX`, plus truncated physical spans and decompression-length mismatches; run on 32-bit and 64-bit Android under AddressSanitizer or equivalent and assert rejection before oversized allocation or any out-of-bounds write while both real packages still list correctly
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. PKG opening now determines the physical file size before parsing and rejects undersized headers, unsupported XAR versions, zero TOC lengths, lengths over the explicit 16 MiB TOC ceiling, allocator or zlib conversion overflow, output terminator overflow, compressed spans outside the package, shared memory-budget violations, and excessive expansion ratios before allocation. TOC reads now loop until the declared input is complete. Bounded streaming inflate requires terminal status, exact compressed-input consumption, and exactly the declared output size before XML parsing. The new malformed corpus covers zero, exact maximum, maximum plus one, `INT_MAX`, `UINT32_MAX`, `SIZE_MAX`, `UINT64_MAX`, truncated physical spans, truncated and trailing compressed streams, and shorter or longer declared output. Scoped quality, all 17 extraction host tests, all 24 D1 and 28 D2 native host tests, and debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. No real PKG fixture or Android sanitizer runner is present in the repository, so those two validation items were unavailable. Independent P1 verification remains a campaign closure action

### BR-0068: P2 - Require a bounded and complete PKG Scripts stream

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-integrity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0029, R1-CHUNK-0030
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L100-L165,L178-L244,L277-L314,L348-L375,L427-L441` in `xar_find_scripts`, `gz_open`, `gz_read`, `cpio_read_entry`, `pkg_scan_cpio`, and `pkg_open`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L450-L529`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.h:L27-L65`, `android/ai tool plans/CD, installer parsing/PKG_PARSER_PLAN.md:L12-L98`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/test_gog_fd.c:L1-L389`
- Evidence: The TOC parser uses unscoped substring searches, accepts decimal prefixes from `strtoull` without checking errors or closing tags, does not validate additions for heap and member offsets, ignores `scr_size`, and stores `scripts_length` without ever enforcing it. `gz_open` ignores seek failure and the stream reader continues to physical file EOF instead of the selected XAR member end. At physical EOF it marks the stream finished and returns a short prefix without distinguishing truncation. `cpio_read_entry` then maps zero output to the same success result as `TRAILER!!!`, so a gzip stream truncated cleanly at a CPIO entry boundary or a complete gzip payload with no CPIO trailer is accepted. When a trailer is seen, the caller also stops without explicitly proving gzip `Z_STREAM_END`, footer integrity, declared member consumption, or an allowed trailing-byte policy
- Trigger: Supply a PKG with overflowing or out-of-file Scripts offsets, a declared Scripts length shorter than its gzip stream, a seek that fails, a gzip or physical file ending exactly after a valid entry, a missing `TRAILER!!!`, or a corrupt or missing gzip footer not consumed before the CPIO trailer return
- Impact: Analysis and extraction can read beyond the TOC-selected XAR member, accept a truncated or structurally incomplete payload, omit later game files without reporting failure, and present the malformed installer as a valid partial package
- Expected: The exact `package.pkg/Scripts` file element is selected, every numeric field is parsed completely, its checked physical span bounds all reads, and success requires both one valid CPIO trailer and verified gzip stream completion within that span
- Suggested fix: Parse the bounded TOC structure with a small real XML reader or a scope-aware parser; validate full decimal fields, checked heap-relative arithmetic, encoding, size, length, and physical file span; give the gzip reader an explicit remaining-member count; distinguish clean stream end from I/O exhaustion and decoder failure; and require exactly one valid CPIO trailer followed by `Z_STREAM_END` and checksum validation under a documented trailing-data policy
- Validation: Add wrong-scope Scripts elements, malformed and overflowing decimal fields, seek failures, member spans ending before, at, and after gzip boundaries, missing and duplicate CPIO trailers, entry-boundary truncation, bad gzip CRC and ISIZE, concatenated streams, and unexpected trailing bytes; assert deterministic failure with no published list or output and retain exact extraction of the two real packages
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. The bounded TOC parser now selects one direct `package.pkg` entry under `toc` and one direct `Scripts` child, rejects ambiguous or unsupported XML constructs, requires the octet-stream encoding, parses unique offset, size, and length fields with complete checked decimal conversion, requires nonzero equal stored size and length, and validates checked heap-relative offsets against the physical package. The gzip reader now checks its seek, reads only the declared member span, treats physical or member exhaustion as truncation, and preserves decoder errors. Scan and extraction succeed only after a zero-size NUL-terminated CPIO trailer, verified gzip `Z_STREAM_END`, CRC and ISIZE validation, and exact declared compressed-member consumption. The documented trailing policy permits only up to 511 decompressed zero bytes for standard final CPIO block padding; nonzero or excessive padding, concatenated gzip members, and compressed trailing bytes fail. Synthetic XAR integration tests cover exact and nonzero offsets, malformed and overflowing numbers, size mismatch, wrong scope and encoding, short and long spans, missing and duplicate trailers, entry-boundary truncation, bad gzip CRC, concatenated streams, compressed trailing bytes, allowed padding, excessive padding, and a valid list and extraction control. Scoped quality, all 17 extraction host tests, all 24 D1 and 28 D2 native host tests, and debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. The retail D1 and D2 Mac packages extracted 7 and 15 files respectively, with every size and SHA-256 matching the checked-in oracle

### BR-0079: P1 - Route live TinySoundFont tuning through the render-thread owner

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: concurrency/memory-safety
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0039, R1-CHUNK-0040
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_main.c:L659-L673` in `nativeSetMusicGain` and `nativeSetMusicVoices`; `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_music_control.c:L106-L113,L374-L401` in music state, volume, and pause access
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/digi_tsf_music.c:L180-L269,L337-L455,L716-L729,L785-L821,L823-L905` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L2352-L2381,L2390-L2420`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicControlPanel.kt:L127-L155,L780-L795`
- Evidence: Main-looper callers mutate the live game synth while music is active. The debug gain setter calls `tsf_set_output` on the process-global synth and the voice setter calls `tsf_set_max_voices`; both also rewrite diagnostic globals. The normal music panel adds UI-thread volume and pause setters that write `g_volume` and `g_paused`, and its state refresh reads the pause flag. At the same time, a dedicated SDL render thread dispatches MIDI events through `tsf_channel_*`, renders samples with `tsf_render_short`, reads volume and pause state, counts active voices, and updates the same diagnostics. The code provides atomic operations only for the ring-buffer indices and render-running flag; there is no lock, command queue, stop handshake, or single-thread ownership around the synth, playback flags, volume, or diagnostics. TinySoundFont's mutable synth operations do not provide an independent cross-thread synchronization contract, and volatile is not used consistently and would not make the remaining accesses race-free in any case
- Trigger: Play MIDI, HMP, or custom game music and drag the in-game volume control, toggle pause and refresh state, or, in a debug build, send repeated exported `com.dxxredux.GAME_COMMAND` broadcasts for `gain` or `voices`, especially while notes are starting or ending
- Impact: Concurrent structural mutation and rendering of the native synth is undefined behavior that can corrupt synth or voice state, crash the process, produce audio corruption, lose or tear volume and pause changes, and return inconsistent diagnostics; normal touch controls expose the flag races in release behavior, while the exported debug receiver makes structural races repeatable without in-game interaction
- Expected: The render thread exclusively owns `g_tsf` and render diagnostics, and external tuning requests are applied at a defined render boundary with validated values and coherent published statistics
- Suggested fix: Submit gain, voice-limit, volume, and pause changes through a render-thread command queue or stop and join rendering before applying structural changes under one lifecycle lock. Keep synth creation, mutation, rendering, and destruction under the same ownership rule; publish pause, volume, and diagnostics through atomics or a synchronized snapshot, and reject non-finite gain values before enqueueing
- Validation: Add instrumentation that plays a dense MIDI fixture while rapidly dragging volume, toggling pause, refreshing state, and alternating debug gain and voice-limit broadcasts; verify every accepted command takes effect at a render boundary and playback remains valid, and run under ThreadSanitizer where supported plus AddressSanitizer on Android; include non-finite gain and exact-limit voice cases
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. Added a bounded FIFO for gain, voice-limit, volume, and pause commands, with the render thread applying every accepted command in order before pause checks or sample generation. Shutdown drains pending commands before releasing ownership, creation failure publishes configuration without mutating the synth, and inactive requests update configuration for the next render owner. TinySoundFont creation, reset, live mutation, rendering, and teardown now follow the start and join ownership boundary. Pause intent, callback-visible playback state, volume, and diagnostics use atomic publication; repeated pause toggles preserve queued intent; non-finite gain is rejected before submission; and voice limits retain the exact 8 and 256 boundaries. Eight focused ownership, ordering, shutdown, non-finite, exact-limit, callback-publication, and concurrent-command checks passed. Scoped quality, all 24 D1 and 28 D2 native host tests, and debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. ThreadSanitizer and Android AddressSanitizer execution were unavailable in the configured test runners.

### BR-0157: P1 - Verify every downloaded source and executable before use

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/supply-chain
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0062, R1-CHUNK-0068, R1-CHUNK-0069, R1-CHUNK-0070, R1-CHUNK-0071, R1-CHUNK-0076
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/CMakeLists.txt:L9-L67,L171-L258` and `android/get_deps/helpers/get_7zip.ps1:L45-L88` in external source and tool acquisition
- Related: `android/get_deps/tool_versions.conf:L163-L169`, `android/get_deps/check-updates.ps1:L1766-L1789`, `android/ai tool plans/CD audio/plan_chromaprint_song_recognition.md:L221-L244`, `android/tests/test_cue_iso.ps1`, `android/helpers/run_cue_iso_tests.sh`, `android/run_all_tests.ps1`, local primary `cmake --help-command file` documentation, and repository dependency-pinning instructions
- Evidence: The project declares CMake 3.16 as supported, downloads the Chromaprint release archive without `URL_HASH`, and downloads `minimp3.h`, `minimp3_ex.h`, `stb_vorbis.c`, and `dr_flac.h` without `EXPECTED_HASH`. Three downloads are trusted forever once a pathname exists; the STB check rejects only a zero-length file, so any nonempty altered cache is compiled. CMake's own documentation says HTTPS verification became enabled by default only in 3.31 and was previously off unless `CMAKE_TLS_VERIFY` was set. The 7-Zip installer is more direct: it downloads the unversioned `https://www.7-zip.org/a/7zr.exe` with no digest or signature check and immediately executes it to unpack a second archive that also has no digest. The plan explicitly required a 7-Zip SHA-256, but the configuration and updater have no hash fields. Both cached local executables are unsigned, and an existing bootstrap is accepted without content or version validation. Thus supported configurations can accept network or cache-controlled native bytes that are then compiled or executed by routine test, metadata, and asset workflows.
- Trigger: Configure the extraction tests with a supported pre-3.31 CMake and default TLS settings, receive altered content from the tag archive, raw-file, bootstrap-executable, or package endpoints, or place any nonempty modified decoder or bootstrap at its cached pathname before use
- Impact: A network, mirror, repository-tag, proxy, or local-cache compromise can inject native code that executes in developer or CI context during the routine build and test flow, with access to the checkout, build credentials, and host account
- Expected: Every remotely acquired source, archive, bootstrap, and executable is identified by an immutable release and verified against a repository-owned cryptographic digest before it can enter a compiler or process, independent of tool version, environment defaults, or an existing cache file
- Suggested fix: Pin repository fetches to full commits and release archives to committed SHA-256 `URL_HASH` values. Add `EXPECTED_HASH SHA256=...`, explicit `TLS_VERIFY ON`, bounded timeouts, and status checks to every CMake single-file download. Add separate versioned SHA-256 fields for the 7-Zip bootstrap and package, download to unique temporary paths, verify before the first execution or extraction, and revalidate every cached executable on every use; Authenticode may be an additional check but not a substitute for the pinned digest. Make the updater change URLs and reviewed hashes together. Keep source URL, version, digest, license, and update procedure in one dependency manifest, and make offline use consume only a previously verified cache.
- Validation: Configure and bootstrap with minimum and current tool versions through a recording proxy; substitute a wrong network body, mutate each cached file without changing its size, move a tag or unversioned bootstrap response, disable the network after verified population, and use expected clean inputs; assert every altered or unverifiable source, archive, and executable fails before compilation or execution and verified online and offline builds are byte-identical
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0068): `android/get_deps/helpers/get_dosbox.sh:L21-L79` in DOSBox-X acquisition and `game_data/extract_dos_demos.ps1:L23-L45,L200-L205` in cached executable resolution and launch
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0068): The helper discovers the first release asset whose name matches `mingw-win64`, downloads and expands it without a repository-owned digest or signature check, copies the first located `dosbox-x.exe` into the shared dependency cache, and later accepts any executable at that pathname. The demo extractor immediately runs those cached native bytes. Pinning only the release tag does not authenticate either the selected asset body or a subsequently altered cache.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0068): Substitute the release metadata, archive response, archive contents, and cached executable independently, including same-size mutations and multiple matching assets; assert each unreviewed identity fails before extraction or execution while a digest-pinned online install and offline cached reuse produce the same verified tool.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0069): `game_data/extract_mac_cd.ps1:L37-L58,L296-L332,L371-L380` in Python, machfs, and unar selection and execution
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0069): The legacy CD oracle path accepts any cached `unar.exe` or PATH-shadowed `unar.exe`, accepts whichever `python` or `python3` is first on PATH, and imports an unpinned `machfs` installation. It authenticates none of those executable or library bytes before giving them proprietary input and write access to the checkout, so altered local dependencies can execute and redefine regression-source output.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0069): Shadow Python and unar on PATH, mutate cached unar and the installed machfs package without changing their reported versions, and exercise verified online and offline setup; require repository-owned versions and digests before execution and byte-identical oracle output from the authenticated dependency set.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0070): `game_data/extract_mac_demos.ps1:L31-L43,L98-L105`, `android/get_deps/tool_versions.conf:L146-L152`, and `android/get_deps/helpers/get_unar.sh:L26-L60` in unar resolution, acquisition, and execution
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0070): The oracle generator reads only `UNAR_DIR_NAME`, accepts any file named `unar.exe` in the shared cache, and executes it without checking the configured version or a digest. The installer downloads an unversioned `unarWindows.zip` URL without a repository-owned hash and permanently trusts any pre-existing executable. The local cache reported v1.8.1 during review, but neither that version string nor the executable bytes are authenticated before a tool that parses proprietary archives and defines regression truth runs with developer permissions.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0070): Replace the unversioned response, archive contents, and cached `unar.exe` independently, including a same-version or same-size mutation; require a committed digest check before first and cached execution, and verify the pinned clean tool reproduces the checked-in oracle.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0071): `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L28-L37,L322-L341` in 7-Zip resolution and mission-archive execution
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0071): The fingerprint workflow prefers whichever `7za` or `7z` executable appears first on PATH, without version or digest validation. If neither exists, it invokes the already-unverified bootstrap covered by this finding and trusts the returned pathname. It then executes that native binary against user-supplied mission archives with write access to the checkout's generated-music tree, so a PATH shadow or altered cache bypasses the repository's dependency identity entirely.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0071): Place controlled same-name executables earlier on PATH, mutate the verified-cache candidate, and exercise online and offline 7z mission processing; require one repository-owned version and digest check before execution and identical output from the authenticated tool on every supported host.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0076): `android/helpers/generate-stuffit-corpus-manifests.ps1:L65-L128,L305-L333` in unar selection, identity recording, and execution, and the assigned manifest provenance fields at L4-L7
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0076): A caller-supplied `ToolDir` is accepted after checking only that `unar.exe` and `lsar.exe` pathnames exist, then is labeled with the hard-coded official package URL and SHA-256 without hashing either executable. The default path verifies the downloaded ZIP but permanently reuses already-extracted executables without comparing them back to that package, so a post-extraction cache mutation receives the same false identity. The generator executes those bytes to define every filename, size, payload hash, compression method, and archive range in the regression oracle, and writes only their self-reported version plus the unrelated constant package digest. The five assigned manifests all assert that official identity, while their C++ consumer ignores every provenance field.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0076): Replace cached and `ToolDir` executables independently, including same-version output and malicious lsar JSON, while retaining the verified official ZIP; require per-executable repository-owned hashes before launch, record the actual verified identities in each manifest, reject provenance fields that the test consumer cannot validate, and prove clean default and explicitly supplied tools reproduce byte-identical manifests.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. Central dependency metadata now pins reviewed SHA-256 identities for Chromaprint, each single-file decoder, the 7-Zip package and bootstrap, DOSBox-X, unar and lsar, embedded Python, and machfs. CMake uses full Git commits, archive and file hashes, explicit TLS verification, bounded downloads, status checks, and cached-file revalidation. Tool installers verify downloaded packages before extraction or execution, verify staged and cached executables or complete Python package trees on every use, and no longer accept PATH-shadowed tools. StuffIt manifests record both executable identities and the native consumer rejects mismatched provenance. The updater reports these dependencies as requiring a coupled URL and reviewed-hash update instead of rewriting identity fields independently. Focused same-size file mutation, missing offline cache, tree mutation, source-contract, and manifest-provenance tests passed; a stale decoder cache failed before compilation; verified online population and disconnected reuse passed; existing 7-Zip, unar, and lsar caches and a clean Python/machfs install revalidated. Scoped quality, all 17 extraction tests, both Windows game builds, and JDK 21 debug APK builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. Independent P1 verification remains a campaign closure action.

### BR-0172: P1 - Confine DOS demo archive entries to the staging root

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/path-traversal
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0068
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/extract_dos_demos.ps1:L94-L113` in `Expand-ZipCompatibleArchive`
- Related: `game_data/extract_dos_demos.ps1:L138-L151,L272-L273`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DemoInstallerPackages.kt:L63-L78`, and BR-0157
- Evidence: For every non-directory entry, the helper passes `Join-Path $DestinationPath $entry.FullName` directly to `ExtractToFile` with overwrite enabled. It neither rejects rooted or dot-containing entry names nor canonicalizes the result and proves containment beneath the staging directory. A focused probe using the helper's path construction showed that `../../escaped.txt` resolves outside `src` to a sibling of the run directory. Cleanup removes only `$tempBase`, so a sufficiently deep traversal can overwrite a writable file elsewhere on the host and leave it behind. The current archives contain no such names, but the script does not enforce their documented hashes and therefore cannot rely on that corpus property.
- Trigger: Replace any configured ZIP-compatible demo package with an archive containing an entry such as `../../escaped.txt`, followed by arbitrary additional parent components and a writable target name
- Impact: Running the reference-oracle helper can create or overwrite files outside its private staging tree with the invoking developer's permissions, including files in temporary or checkout locations reachable by relative traversal
- Expected: Every archive member is accepted only after its normalized destination is proven to remain below one uniquely owned staging root, before any parent directory or file is created
- Suggested fix: Reject rooted names, empty and dot path components, invalid separators, and names whose `GetFullPath` destination is not strictly beneath the canonical staging prefix using the host's path comparison rules. Extract only after that check, avoid following pre-existing reparse points within the private root, and combine this with BR-0157's package digest verification and BR-0018's budgets.
- Validation: Add forward- and backslash traversal, absolute, drive-qualified, UNC, mixed-separator, case-boundary, sibling-prefix, duplicate, and platform-relevant junction or symlink fixtures; assert failure before every outside write, no surviving residue, and unchanged bytes outside the root while the four verified packages still extract.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. `Expand-BoundedZipArchive` now preflights the complete central directory before creating output; rejects rooted, drive-qualified, UNC, backslash or mixed-separator, empty, dot, parent, Windows alias, control, and device names; and rejects canonical prefix escapes, normalized duplicate destinations, and file/child conflicts. Existing roots must be empty regular non-reparse directories, component creation rejects reparse or non-directory nodes, file creation cannot overwrite, and every failure removes the uniquely owned destination. The DOS demo workflow verifies each package digest, uses a GUID workspace, and guarantees process shutdown and workspace cleanup. The hostile corpus passed with no residue or outside changes, all four verified packages extracted, scoped quality and all 17 native extraction tests passed, both Windows game builds passed, and JDK 21 debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. Independent P1 verification remains a campaign closure action.

### BR-0038: P2 - Use the canonical fingerprint threshold in the duplicate matcher

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0016, R1-CHUNK-0035
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_match.c:L288-L301` in `main` and `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_fingerprint.c:L27-L48`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/assets/fingerprint_config.json5:L1-L15`, `game_data/update_known_discs_albums.ps1:L24-L35,L158-L174`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L52-L59,L84-L103`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/chromaprint_db.c:L24-L52`
- Evidence: The command documents its threshold argument as optional and hardcodes 0.40 when it is omitted. The repository's declared single source of truth sets 0.65 and explains that random-noise comparisons peak near 0.50, so the tool's default lies in the false-match region. The merger normally passes the configured value, but it also falls back to 0.40 when configuration loading fails. On the locally available 761-entry merged input, the matcher reported 51,571 pairs at 0.40 versus 1,577 at 0.65. Command-line parsing uses `atof`, so a value such as `nan` also passes both range comparisons and silently makes every `score >= threshold` test false. Android repeats the unsafe fallback: the runtime matcher is compiled with 0.40, `loadFingerprintConfig` catches missing or malformed canonical configuration and continues without setting 0.65, and the JNI/native setter silently ignores invalid or non-finite values while Kotlin logs them as configured
- Trigger: Invoke the documented command without a threshold, let either the merger or Android config loader fall back after missing or malformed configuration, or pass a non-finite or partially numeric threshold
- Impact: Database regeneration can classify tens of thousands of unrelated tracks as duplicates or, for NaN, classify none, producing incorrect exclusions and unstable album-to-disc mappings while the command exits successfully; Android can likewise accept false runtime identities under the unsafe compiled fallback while logging only a configuration warning
- Expected: Every matching surface consumes one validated finite threshold from the canonical configuration, and invalid or unavailable configuration fails instead of selecting a known-unsafe fallback
- Suggested fix: Require the caller to pass the canonical value or add an explicit config-file argument, remove the independent 0.40 defaults, and parse with `strtof` plus complete-consumption, range, and `isfinite` checks. Keep runtime, scripts, and the audit tool on one loaded configuration contract
- Validation: Add host and Android matcher integration cases at 0.40, 0.65, omitted, missing, malformed, trailing-junk, infinity, and NaN inputs; assert invalid configuration fails closed and every workflow uses exactly the configured threshold
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. The host matcher now requires an explicit threshold and parses it with `strtof`, complete-consumption, range, `ERANGE`, and finite-value checks. The database regeneration script loads the canonical configuration through a shared strict helper, fails on missing or malformed values, and passes the threshold using invariant round-trip formatting. Android strictly parses exactly one configuration object, requires both numeric finite fractions, checks that JNI accepts the threshold, and propagates initialization failure. The native database begins unconfigured, rejects loading until a valid threshold is supplied, and clears the configuration when an invalid setter call is attempted. Focused host, Kotlin, and native tests cover 0.40, 0.65, omitted, missing, malformed, trailing-junk, infinity, and NaN cases. Scoped quality, the full JVM suite, all 18 native support suites, both Windows game builds, and JDK 21 debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0041: P3 - Establish one owner for native and Kotlin extension policy

- [x] FIXED
- Type: maintainability
- Confidence: high
- Category: maintainability
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0016
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/game_file_extensions.h:L8-L17`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/game_file_extensions.c:L13-L47`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AndroidGameFileExtensions.kt:L3-L16`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/GameFileFormats.kt:L41-L224,L310-L316`, and `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/AndroidGameFileExtensionsTest.kt:L7-L21`
- Evidence: The C header calls its tables the single source of truth and says to align them with Kotlin, while the Kotlin wrapper says format knowledge lives in `GameFileFormats` and must be aligned with C. Both sides therefore claim ownership while duplicating game-import, disc-extract, and GOG-audio policy in different representations, and the Mac native list adds a third specialized set. The shared game and generic disc values currently agree, but the only focused test asserts Kotlin behavior and cannot detect native drift. History already shows the native disc table being removed, specialized, and re-added independently as import support changed
- Trigger: Add or reclassify a game format in `GameFileFormats` or one native table while following that file's source-of-truth comment, without discovering and updating every mirror
- Impact: Kotlin can classify and offer a file that native GOG, PKG, ISO, HFS, or StuffIt extraction silently omits, or native code can extract a format that launcher validation does not recognize; contradictory ownership makes the omission likely to survive review
- Expected: One artifact owns extension roles, every language consumes generated or mechanically verified values, and platform-specific exceptions are explicit rather than implicit list drift
- Suggested fix: Define the role flags in one neutral manifest and generate the compact C tables and Kotlin map, or designate one side as authoritative and add a cross-language parity test that parses the other. Rewrite both comments to state the actual ownership and document why the Mac set differs
- Validation: Add a test that compares every shared role and case-insensitive lookup across native and Kotlin representations, deliberately change one side to prove the test fails, and retain focused cases for GOG audio, generic disc extraction, and Mac-only exceptions
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. `GameFileFormats` now explicitly owns game-import, generic disc-extract, GOG-audio, and Mac-disc-extract roles. Native comments identify their tables as mechanically verified mirrors, while the Mac policy documents its auxiliary config, text, and palette exceptions and its deliberate separation from the generic ISO allowlist. The JVM parity test parses every native table and compares its normalized values with the authoritative Kotlin sets, verifies the exact Mac-only and generic-only differences, and retains case-insensitive game, GOG-audio, generic-disc, and Mac behavior checks. Removing one native entry made the parity test fail as intended before the table was restored. A registered native test independently covers case-insensitive native game and GOG-audio lookup. Scoped quality, the full JVM suite, all 19 native support suites, both Windows game builds, and JDK 21 debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0043: P2 - Fail audio fingerprinting when directory enumeration is incomplete

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/test-tool
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0017, R1-CHUNK-0072
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_audio.c:L79-L118` in `collect_audio_files`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_audio.c:L30-L36,L132-L187`, `game_data/fingerprint_music_packs.ps1:L308-L329`, `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L700-L742`, and `android/tests/test_fpcalc_and_acoustid.ps1:L121-L155`
- Evidence: Both `FindFirstFileA` and `opendir` failure return the same zero count as an empty directory, and `main` prints `[]` and exits zero. Direct execution against a nonexistent directory confirmed that behavior. Enumeration also stops silently at `MAX_FILES` and does not distinguish `FindNextFileA` or `readdir` errors from end-of-directory. The fingerprint generators accept a successful nonempty subset without comparing it to the extracted or directory audio count, so capacity or mid-scan omissions are publishable; the ANSI Windows API additionally makes unrepresentable paths another fail-open enumeration case
- Trigger: Pass a missing, unreadable, or non-ANSI Windows directory, inject a directory-read error, or fingerprint a directory containing more than 4,096 matching files
- Impact: The tool can report successful empty or partial analysis, causing music packs or mission archives to lose track fingerprints and later database matches without identifying which source files were never examined
- Expected: Success proves complete enumeration of the requested directory and every eligible file is either represented in output or causes a nonzero result with an explicit failure record
- Suggested fix: Return distinct empty, error, and capacity-exceeded states; use checked dynamic storage or reject on the first excess file; inspect terminal enumeration errors; use wide-character Windows enumeration with UTF-8 conversion; and have callers compare enumerated, fingerprinted, and expected counts before publishing metadata
- Validation: Test empty, nonexistent, unreadable, non-ASCII, exact-limit, one-over, and injected mid-enumeration-error directories on Windows and POSIX; assert only a genuinely empty readable directory succeeds with `[]` and every valid audio filename appears exactly once
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0072): `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L711-L742` in native result validation
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0072): The mission batch knows how many audio files `Extract-MissionArchiveAudio` published, but after invoking `fingerprint_audio` it never compares that count or the exact `SourceMap` filename set with the returned JSON objects. A successful nonempty subset produced after silent capacity or enumeration truncation is therefore accepted, named, and written as the complete mission sidecar.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0072): Extract a known set of audio members, then force capacity truncation, a mid-enumeration failure, a missing result, a duplicate result, and an unexpected result; require exact set equality before sidecar publication or database update.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. `fingerprint_audio` now distinguishes readable empty directories from open, terminal-read, close, allocation, conversion, and capacity errors. Windows uses `wmain`, `FindFirstFileW`/`FindNextFileW`, strict UTF-16 to UTF-8 conversion, and wide-path file reads before memory decoding; POSIX checks `opendir`, resets and inspects `errno` around every `readdir`, and checks `closedir`. Filename storage is exact heap-backed data under the explicit 4,096-file policy, and the first excess file fails before fingerprinting or JSON output. Both metadata generators now reject every nonzero tool result and use one shared validator requiring exact ordinal equality between expected and returned filename sets, with no missing, duplicate, unexpected, or incomplete fingerprint record. The Windows hostile matrix passed for readable empty, missing, non-directory, non-ASCII path and filename, exact-limit, one-over, and injected mid-enumeration failure cases, and proved one valid Unicode-named audio file appeared exactly once. Shared result validation rejected missing, duplicate, unexpected, and incomplete records. POSIX runtime execution was unavailable in the configured environment. Scoped quality and PowerShell parse checks, the mission budget regression, all 19 native support suites, both Windows game builds, and JDK 21 debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0225: P1 - Authenticate reconnecting UDP players independently of callsign

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/protocol-authentication
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0116
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/net/net_udp_android.c:L53-L72` in player-identity matching
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/d1x-rebirth/main/net_udp.c:L1049-L1089,L2042-L2178`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/d2x-rebirth/main/net_udp.c:L1059-L1099,L2056-L2195`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/net/net_udp_android.c:L145-L174`
- Evidence: The frozen branch replaces the base's exact callsign-plus-full-socket-address reconnect match with a first pass that ignores the UDP port and an Android fallback that selects any disconnected slot solely by callsign, regardless of source address. The accompanying rationale treats launcher-assigned unique random callsigns as sufficient safety, but uniqueness prevents accidental collisions rather than proving possession: callsigns are player identifiers exposed to other participants, not secret reconnect capabilities. A valid `UPID_REQUEST` carries no durable credential bound to the prior slot, and its requester-controlled token is not validated by the request-token gate. Once the callsign fallback matches, paired D1 and D2 welcome paths classify the sender as the prior player, preserve that slot's identity and state, rewrite its address to the sender, issue a new player token, announce a reconnect, and start object synchronization.
- Trigger: Observe or learn a player's callsign, wait for that player to become disconnected, then send an otherwise valid same-level join request using that callsign from a different address before the legitimate player reconnects
- Impact: A peer or network observer can claim the disconnected player's slot, score, object and multiplayer identity, receive reconnect state and subsequent packets intended for that slot, act under the victim's identity, and prevent or displace the legitimate reconnect.
- Expected: Reclaiming a disconnected slot proves possession of a stable secret or authenticated player identity bound to that slot and the current game generation; a display name, IP address, or uniqueness guarantee alone is never accepted as ownership.
- Suggested fix: Give each admitted player a cryptographically random reconnect capability or authenticated matchmaking player identity bound to the game generation and slot, carry it across the supported host-migration flow, and require it before restoring prior state. Rotate or invalidate capabilities on generation changes and successful takeover, use constant-time comparison where appropriate, and retain callsigns only for display and collision handling. For LAN admission without matchmaking, establish and return the random capability during the authenticated initial join.
- Validation: Exercise paired D1 and D2 direct, proxy, NAT-rebound, and host-migration reconnects with the rightful capability, then attempt takeover from the same and different IP using the victim's public callsign, a duplicated callsign, another player's capability, an old game-generation capability, and a replayed capability after successful reconnect. Require only the current credential owner to reclaim the slot, preserve legitimate address migration, issue no object sync or replacement token on failure, and leave the real player's slot available.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-27. Android D1 and D2 now authenticate every UDP join and reconnect request with a process-lifetime P-256 identity, a game-generation-bound signed monotonic request counter, and a fresh route-bound challenge before any disconnected slot, address, token, or object-sync state is restored. Slot lookup uses only the replicated public key; callsigns are display and collision data. Full sync and ADDPLAYER replicate public keys for host migration, direct and proven proxy routes are preserved through reconnect sync, generation changes invalidate signed requests, counters reject request replay, and completed challenges reject proof replay. Native request, key, counter, and challenge tests, Kotlin signing and tamper tests, scoped quality, all 25 D1 and 29 D2 host tests, both Windows builds, and JDK 21 debug builds and tests for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. A live multi-device direct, proxy, NAT-rebound, and host-migration adversarial session was not available locally; independent P1 verification remains a campaign closure action.

### BR-0362: P2 - Initialize Android argument defaults before startup

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: performance/configuration-correctness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0253
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:d1/misc/args.c:L124-L231,L246-L274` in command defaults and `InitArgsAndroid`, with paired `d2/misc/args.c:L125-L240,L255-L282`
- Related: `d1/misc/physfsx.c:L29-L52`, `d2/misc/physfsx.c:L29-L61`, `d1/main/config.c:L153-L169`, `d2/main/config.c:L171-L187`, `d1/main/game.c:L452-L460`, `d2/main/game.c:L479-L487`, `d1/main/gamefont.c:L160-L175`, `d2/main/gamefont.c:L159-L174`, `d1/main/net_udp.c:L394-L430`, and `d2/main/net_udp.c:L403-L439`
- Evidence: Both `GameArg` objects begin zero-initialized. Desktop `InitArgs` invokes `ReadCmdArgs`, which is not merely an option parser: it assigns nonzero defaults including `SysUseNiceFPS = 1`, `SysMaxFPS = MAXIMUM_FPS`, high-resolution font availability, graphics defaults, and, under Android, the hardcoded `LogNetTraffic = 1`. `InitArgsAndroid` passes `read_cmd_args = 0`, so `init_args_common` returns before that entire function. The paired Android `PHYSFSX_init` branches manually set only a small subset of fields and return; neither startup path later invokes `ReadCmdArgs`. The comment in D2 acknowledges the bypass but omits NiceFPS and the other still-required defaults. Both game loops default VSync to zero and wait for their frame deadline in a polling loop; `SDL_Delay` runs only when `SysUseNiceFPS` is true. The skipped hardcoded network flag also means `net_log_comment` returns before its Android debug-log bridge despite source comments claiming the flag is always one.
- Trigger: Launch either Android game normally with VSync absent or disabled, or attempt to collect the native network diagnostics that the Android branch declares always enabled; high-resolution font assets provide an additional visible case
- Impact: Ordinary frame limiting busy-spins instead of yielding, unnecessarily occupying a CPU core and increasing mobile power use, heat, and throttling whenever VSync is off. High-resolution fonts are excluded despite being available, and native packet comments and traffic diagnostics silently remain disabled, contradicting the intended Android diagnostic contract and obscuring multiplayer failures. Other future nonzero defaults added only to `ReadCmdArgs` inherit the same silent omission.
- Expected: Android startup applies one explicit, complete set of platform-compatible defaults before any consumer runs, independently of whether desktop INI and command-line overrides are supported; every intentionally disabled option is named and tested rather than becoming zero by control-flow accident.
- Suggested fix: Split default initialization from INI and argument override parsing. Call the shared default initializer for desktop, Android game, JNI, and headless startup, then apply documented Android overrides and parse only the supported raw startup options. Remove the `read_cmd_args` boolean path that bypasses defaults, and make the native network-log policy agree with the user-facing category setting instead of relying on an unreachable assignment.
- Validation: Add paired startup tests that poison or zero `GameArg`, invoke desktop and every Android initializer, and assert the complete expected field table. With VSync off, instrument one capped frame and automap interval and require the intended delay/yield call without a busy loop; repeat with VSync on. Verify high- and low-resolution font selection, Android-specific audio and texture overrides, supported raw pilot/resume/replay arguments, and enabled and disabled native network categories. Fail the test whenever a newly added nonzero `ReadCmdArgs` default lacks an explicit Android disposition.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-28. Paired D1 and D2 argument initialization now clears the complete `GameArg` structure, applies one explicit nonzero default table, parses overrides, and finally reapplies Android invariants before any startup consumer runs. Desktop startup still appends INI arguments, while Android skips only INI loading and now parses its supported raw options; pilot values are applied directly and launcher-only resume and replay arguments remain preserved in `Args`. Android consistently enables NiceFPS yielding, player-directory isolation, high-resolution font admission, native network-log forwarding into the user-controlled Kotlin category filter, 22 kHz D2 audio, and the required OGL texture-merge path. The obsolete partial defaults were removed from both PhysFS initializers. Paired tests poison every `GameArg` byte, compare the complete desktop and Android field tables, exercise high and low resolution options, audio and texture invariants, frame-pacing overrides, pilot parsing, resume and replay preservation, and enabled and disabled native network gates. Both focused tests, all 25 D1 and 29 D2 established host tests, both Windows builds, scoped quality and formatting checks, `git diff --check`, and JDK 21 JVM tests and debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. Direct consumer tracing confirms the initialized NiceFPS flag reaches the game and automap `timer_delay` gates when VSync is off and that the high-resolution fields reach the font and graphics selectors. No emulator power trace or visual font-selection session was run.

### BR-0429: P1 - Bound and offload configuration imports before materialization

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: performance/resource-exhaustion
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0384, R1-CHUNK-0391
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/HumanReadableConfig.kt:L133-L205,L569-L587` in unbudgeted layout and array materialization
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ConfigImportExport.kt:L133-L181`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L3331-L3337`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/TouchLayoutSlotRepository.kt:L26-L37`
- Evidence: Content import uses `BufferedReader.readText()` with no byte or character ceiling, constructs a complete `JSONObject`, and lets every supplied control array grow without an element, string, aggregate-allocation, or work budget. The chooser's initial detection path already reads and parses the selected document on an IO coroutine, but confirmation calls `ConfigImportExport.importFromUri` directly from the Compose `onClick`; that rereads and reparses the whole content, constructs all controls, serializes the complete slot set, and writes both slot and active-layout files synchronously on the main looper. A provider can therefore return an arbitrarily large document or large valid arrays after selection, and the UI has no cancellation or deadline.
- Trigger: Choose a large valid touch or combined configuration, or a content URI whose provider supplies a much larger document at confirmation, then tap Import
- Impact: The launcher can exhaust heap while holding the source text, JSON tree, Kotlin control lists, and serialized slot generation concurrently, or block the main looper long enough to trigger an ANR. Repeated parsing and synchronous persistence make even nonfatal large imports visibly freeze the only recovery UI.
- Expected: Configuration import enforces explicit source, nesting, string, section, element, and aggregate-work budgets before materialization, performs parsing and persistence off the main thread, and remains cancellable with bounded UI feedback.
- Suggested fix: Inspect provider size when available but enforce a streaming hard byte limit regardless; reject excessive nesting, strings, arrays, slots, controls, and total modeled objects with checked counters. Parse and validate once on a lifecycle-owned IO dispatcher, stage one immutable generation, then publish atomically off the main thread and return a typed result to Compose. Defend against providers whose content changes between metadata, preview, and read.
- Validation: Exercise exact-limit and limit-plus-one source sizes, deep nesting, huge strings, many empty and full controls, many slots, slow streams, unknown lengths, changing provider content, cancellation, process recreation, read failures, and storage failures. Measure peak heap and main-thread stalls, require deterministic bounded rejection without ANR or OOM, unchanged prior state on every failure, and one parse and publication for valid imports.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0391): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ConfigImportExport.kt:L133-L156,L203-L277` in whole-document and combined-section materialization, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L318-L327` in direct import dispatch
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0391): The assigned importer confirms that the missing budget applies to every configuration type and all combined sections, not only touch-layout controls. It reads the complete provider stream and builds the complete JSON tree before type dispatch, then combined import materializes unbounded touch and controller slot arrays and their nested models. The Advanced Settings picker calls this complete path directly from its activity-result callback with no background dispatch or confirmation step, so its ordinary Import Config button independently performs the unbounded parse and all persistence on the main looper.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0391): Exercise the Advanced Settings, SetupActivity confirmation, touch-editor, and controller-editor pickers with the same exact-limit, slow-provider, changed-provider, cancellation, and oversized combined-slot corpus. Require every entry point to share one bounded off-main import operation and publish exactly one validated generation.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. All configuration imports now share an interruptible IO worker path that checks provider metadata when available, enforces a streaming 1 MiB hard limit regardless of metadata, strictly decodes UTF-8, rejects nesting above 32 levels and strings above 64 KiB before constructing a JSON tree, and enforces 1,024-entry container, 128-slot, and 20,000-value aggregate budgets before model materialization. Advanced Settings, Touch Editor, Controller Editor, and SetupActivity all enter this path from lifecycle-owned coroutine scopes, so provider reads, JSON parsing, model construction, serialization, and persistence no longer run on the main looper. SetupActivity retains the bounded prepared document through confirmation and therefore neither rereads nor reparses provider content that may change after selection. Focused tests cover exact-limit and limit-plus-one sources, short and zero-length reads, provider failure, malformed and deep JSON, oversized strings, arrays and slot sections, valid type detection, worker dispatch, and cancellation interrupting blocking provider work. Scoped code quality, 540 JVM tests, the debug APK, native CMake builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed. Atomic multi-section publication and storage-failure rollback remain tracked separately by BR-0200 and BR-0438.

### BR-0431: P1 - Quote configuration values before regex replacement

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/input-validation
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0385
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupConfigFiles.kt:L63-L79` in config-line replacement
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ConfigImportExport.kt:L285-L332`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L3331-L3337`, and BR-0200
- Evidence: `updateConfigPaths` constructs the replacement as `"$key=$value"` and passes it to `Regex.replace` without quoting replacement metacharacters. Combined configuration import accepts each managed `descent_cfg` member with `getString` and forwards the caller-controlled text unchanged. On an existing matching line, Kotlin's JVM regex engine interprets dollar references and backslashes rather than writing them literally: a direct JDK 21 probe against the cached Kotlin 2.2.10 standard library reproduced `$9` throwing `IndexOutOfBoundsException`, `$0` expanding to the entire old line, and `foo\bar` becoming `foobar`. Import confirmation invokes this path synchronously on the main thread and does not catch update failures. BR-0200 covers unchecked nontransactional publication after a replacement is constructed, but not this earlier input-dependent exception and transformation.
- Trigger: After a managed key such as `TexFilt` exists in `descent.cfg`, confirm a combined configuration whose matching `app_settings.descent_cfg` value contains `$9`, `$0`, a named group form, or a backslash
- Impact: A selected configuration can throw on the launcher main thread and terminate or destabilize SetupActivity, or silently write a different value from the imported document across root and game configs. Earlier SharedPreferences changes may already have been applied, leaving a partially imported generation.
- Expected: Config values are validated against each key's semantic domain and emitted literally; no user-controlled value is evaluated as regex replacement syntax, and import either commits one complete valid generation or preserves the prior state with an actionable error.
- Suggested fix: Parse `descent.cfg` as key and raw-value lines without treating replacement text as a regex program, or use `Regex.escape` for keys and `Matcher.quoteReplacement` or a transform lambda for values. Add per-key typed validation before constructing staged root, D1, and D2 files, then publish the complete set atomically with BR-0200's failure propagation.
- Validation: Import every managed key with valid boundaries, wrong types, dollar signs, `$0`, valid and invalid numeric group references, `${name}`, lone and repeated backslashes, newlines, controls, long values, and duplicate existing lines. Require literal or typed rejection without exception, exact prior-file preservation on failure, no partial preference or config mutation, and identical validated root and game generations after success.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. Existing configuration lines are now replaced through Kotlin's transform-lambda overload, so the generated `key=value` text is emitted literally instead of being parsed as a JVM regex replacement program. The focused regression first reproduced the pre-fix `$9` `IndexOutOfBoundsException`, then verified literal `$9`, `$0`, `${name}`, lone backslash, embedded backslash, and repeated-backslash values across root, D1, and D2 configuration files. The complete graphics configuration helper suite, scoped code quality, the full JVM test suite, the debug APK build, native CMake builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed. Atomic combined-import publication remains tracked separately by BR-0200.

### BR-0437: P1 - Import exported Boolean preferences with their declared types

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-integrity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0391
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ConfigImportExport.kt:L22-L35,L91-L104,L283-L314` in app-setting export and import type dispatch
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L318-L327,L596-L628`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/EnginePreferencesPage.kt:L100-L125,L503-L515`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L831-L842`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L1957-L1964`
- Evidence: Export preserves each SharedPreferences runtime type and emits both `PREF_ALLOW_ACOUSTID_WEB_LOOKUPS` and `PREF_HEADLIGHT_OFF_BY_DEFAULT` as JSON Booleans. Import's Boolean switch omits exactly those two maintained keys and sends every omitted key through `json.getString` and `editor.putString`. With the frozen `org.json:json:20240303`, a JDK 21 probe confirmed that `getString` on a JSON Boolean throws `JSONException` rather than coercing it. Thus an unmodified file produced by `exportAll` fails during import whenever either preference is present. The import entry points do not catch semantic exceptions, and combined import has already replaced touch and controller slots before reaching app settings. If a future coercive parser allowed the call, the written string would still violate the later `SharedPreferences.getBoolean` contract used by Advanced Settings, engine preferences, mission analysis, and game startup. No focused import/export test exercises either key.
