- Trigger: Enable AcoustID web lookups or save the Headlight off by default preference, export all configurations, then import that unmodified exported file through Advanced Settings, SetupActivity, the touch editor, or the controller editor
- Impact: A supported self-export round trip throws on the launcher main thread instead of returning a result. Earlier combined sections may already have overwritten active touch and controller state, leaving a partial import, and the user cannot rely on the advertised export as a restorable backup of these settings.
- Expected: Every exported preference has one shared declared type used by both export and import, and every unmodified supported export imports without exception or type drift.
- Suggested fix: Replace the independent key list and import switch with one typed preference schema that owns key, type, default, validation, export, and staged import behavior. Reject wrong JSON types with field-specific errors before changing any preference, and publish the validated preference set with the complete combined transaction in BR-0438.
- Validation: Round-trip every exported preference individually and together with both Boolean values absent, false, and true; inspect JSON types and SharedPreferences runtime types after process recreation. Import wrong primitive and container types for every key and require a recoverable field error, no main-thread exception, no type drift, and byte-identical prior configuration on failure.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. `ConfigImportExport` now uses one typed schema for both preference export and import. The schema declares the maintained string and Boolean keys, including AcoustID web lookups and the headlight-off default. Import validates the complete preferences subsection before creating an editor, writes every value with its declared `SharedPreferences` type, and returns a field-specific app-settings error for wrong primitives, arrays, objects, and JSON null without publishing decoded preference values. Focused tests cover all keys absent, false and true Boolean round trips, both affected keys, and invalid primitive and container types. Scoped code quality, the focused and full JVM suites, `:app:assembleDebug`, CMake builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed. Combined-section transactionality remains tracked separately by BR-0438.

### BR-0466: P1 - Preserve existing custom-audio sets when an add imports nothing

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-loss
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0410
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L1597-L1645,L1686-L1716,L1722-L1835` in existing-set destination selection, empty-result cleanup, and archive extraction
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L276-L311,L351-L457`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/CustomAudioSetManager.kt:L87-L128,L149-L150,L227-L256`, `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/CustomAudioSetManagerTest.kt:L7-L46`, BR-0418, and BR-0456
- Evidence: The import dialog explicitly permits choosing an existing set and defaults copied raw audio and every archive to app storage. `importAudioFiles` then assigns `destDir` directly to that existing set's real storage directory and writes entries there. If no selected item contributes a supported audio file, the generic empty-result branch executes `destDir.deleteRecursively()` whenever copying is enabled; it does not distinguish a newly created empty directory from the pre-existing target. A valid ZIP or 7z with no MP3, OGG, or FLAC entries returns an empty list normally, and ordinary provider, archive-open, or per-item exceptions are caught and converted to the same empty aggregate. The complete existing directory is therefore deleted without the set's separate Delete confirmation, while `addFilesToSet` is never called and the saved set record continues to list every now-missing track. Successful additions are also written over final names before metadata merge, so flattened archive basename collisions or a same-named new track can replace existing bytes without an isolated attempt generation. Maintained manager tests exercise add, remove, and reference bookkeeping directly but never call the importer or cover an empty or failed add.
- Trigger: Keep a copied custom-audio set, choose Add Set, select that existing set in the destination dropdown, leave copying enabled, and import a valid ZIP or 7z containing no supported audio; equivalent deletion follows when every selected item is unsupported or fails with an exception caught by the loop
- Impact: One routine failed or mistaken add operation deletes every copied track in the chosen set while the UI and persisted metadata still present the set and filenames. Those app-managed copies may be the only retained bytes after the original media was moved or deleted, and no confirmation, rollback generation, or recovery record exists.
- Expected: An add attempt owns and cleans only files it created, an empty or failed attempt leaves every existing set byte and metadata field unchanged, and successful additions cannot silently replace an existing track through a projected-name collision.
- Suggested fix: Stage every import into a unique owned sibling directory, preflight canonical and case-folded destination names against both the attempt and retained set, and publish only validated new files after all selected inputs reach a defined complete outcome. Track exact attempt-created paths if incremental publication is retained. On empty, failure, or cancellation, delete only the owned staging generation; never recursively delete `setDir(targetSetId)`. Merge metadata only after byte publication succeeds, with rollback or a generation swap preserving the prior set.
- Validation: Seed existing sets with byte-distinct copied tracks and metadata, then add empty ZIP, DXA, and 7z archives, unsupported direct files, unreadable and throwing providers, corrupt archives, mixed success and failure batches, duplicate and case-colliding basenames, same-named replacements, short reads, storage exhaustion, cancellation, and process death at every write and publication boundary. Require the prior directory and metadata to remain byte-identical on every non-success, no replacement without an explicit collision policy, no attempt residue, and one coherent appended generation after success. Exercise both Music Picker and SetupActivity entry points with new and existing targets.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. Copied imports now use a unique sibling staging directory, and empty, failed, or storage-exhausted attempts clean only that owned staging generation. Existing set bytes and metadata remain untouched until an attempt has imported at least one supported file. Publication preflights locale-stable case-insensitive collisions against retained destination names and names within the attempt, then refuses overwrites while moving staged files into the set. Focused tests cover failed and empty attempts preserving existing data, empty new attempts publishing nothing, successful append behavior, and exact and case-folded collision rejection. Scoped code quality, the focused and full JVM suites, `:app:assembleDebug`, CMake builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0413: P3 - Validate AcoustID labels against maintained source metadata

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/metadata
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0373, R1-CHUNK-0396
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/music/D2 vampyro mp3/chromaprint_info.json5:L6` in the false AcoustID artist, title, and album attached to the briefing track
- Related: `game_data/fingerprint_music_packs.ps1:L107-L150,L340-L359`, `game_data/update_known_discs_albums.ps1:L263-L275,L448-L465`, `android/app/src/main/assets/known_discs.json5:L1109-L1116`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L14,L108-L141,L397-L436`
- Evidence: The assigned track is maintained as `01 Vampyro Briefing (Remastered).mp3`, but its generated fields claim `acoustid_name: "Torche - Vampyro"` and `acoustid_album: "Torche"`. The locally available source ZIP contains that exact filename, and its 4,935,675-byte entry is byte-identical to the extracted track with SHA-256 `316f94d30d5c28120da6505313c43450ad4f05f20f41b107dd6d229ad9691b6b`. Its embedded ID3 frames independently identify title `Vampyro Briefing (Remastered)`, artist `Dan Wentz`, and album `Descent II: Vampyro Remastered`; the other two tracks in the same source carry the same artist and album. The generator requests recordings and releases, then returns the first recording with any title and that recording's first release. It never evaluates the result score, retains no AcoustID result or recording identity for review, and does not compare the response with the archive filename or embedded metadata. The merger publishes the false fields unchanged. Both direct disc-name lookup and runtime database flattening prefer any nonempty `acoustid_name` over the maintained `name`, and their placeholder filter rejects only the exact string `[unknown] - [untitled]`, so matching this active fingerprint reports `Torche - Vampyro` rather than the source track's identity.
- Trigger: Fingerprint the assigned Vampyro briefing track or directly look up track 1 of `d2-vampyro-mp3`; equivalently, regenerate any pack for which AcoustID's first titled recording is a plausible but unrelated song
- Impact: The bundled database confidently assigns the wrong artist, song, and album to maintained source audio, and launcher imports, fingerprint-based mission analysis, track maps, and user-visible music names propagate that false identity while hiding the accurate local name.
- Expected: External metadata is accepted only with recorded evidence and a defined confidence and consistency policy, and a conflicting web response cannot silently override authoritative maintained source metadata.
- Suggested fix: Preserve and validate embedded tags during extraction, retain AcoustID result score and stable recording/release IDs, and select or accept a result only when it meets a documented threshold and agrees with normalized local artist, title, album, or an explicit reviewed override. Keep the maintained basename or tag-derived name when results conflict or remain ambiguous. Make runtime naming consume a reviewed preferred-name field rather than automatically treating every cached AcoustID string as authoritative.
- Validation: Mock multiple AcoustID results with varying scores, ordering, missing releases, same-title unrelated artists, and conflicting local tags. Cover the current Vampyro source and the assigned Haunted and remix records. Require deterministic order-independent selection, explicit conflict or no-match states, preservation of the Dan Wentz Vampyro identity, no automatic promotion of partial `[untitled]` results, and unchanged names for corroborated high-confidence matches.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0396): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AcoustIdClient.kt:L113-L139` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L878-L904` in Android response selection and cache publication
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0396): The in-app lookup independently repeats the generator's trust defect: it reads only `results[0].recordings[0]`, never inspects the result score, recording identity, alternate results, releases, source filename, or embedded tags, builds the returned label from that first recording, and persists any nonblank label as successful. Repairing only the maintenance generator would therefore leave explicit mission-browser web lookup able to promote the same unrelated first hit into runtime name sidecars.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0396): Run the same reordered, low-score, ambiguous, and conflicting-result corpus through both the generator and Android client and require one shared selection contract, equivalent typed outcomes, and no successful cache publication for an unverified first result.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. Android and maintenance generation now scan all candidates, require a score of at least 0.8, require a stable recording ID, reject title conflicts and equally scored artist ambiguity, and choose releases and ties deterministically. Accepted score and recording identity are retained in the Android cache and generated metadata, while merging and cached reuse require that evidence. Runtime bundled-name consumers validate external titles against maintained labels, and the false Torche fields were removed from the Vampyro source and bundled database. Focused response-policy and cache-persistence tests, PowerShell parser checks, scoped code quality, 544 JVM tests, the debug APK, all three Android native ABIs, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0408: P2 - Connect the documented AcoustID config to the Android asset consumer

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: integration/configuration
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0362
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/acoustid_config.json5.example:L1-L3,L14-L17` in the documented key placement
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AcoustIdClient.kt:L37-L51`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L833-L843,L878-L908`, `android/app/build.gradle:L345-L363`, `.gitignore:L69-L78`, and the root-config consumers in `android/tests/test_fpcalc_and_acoustid.ps1` and `game_data/fingerprint_*.ps1`
- Evidence: The example tells operators to copy it beside itself as `android/acoustid_config.json5`, and every repository maintenance script reads that root path. The Android client instead calls `context.assets.open("acoustid_config.json5")`. The frozen asset tree contains no such file, the Gradle source set adds only generated resources, and no build or packaging script copies the ignored root config into assets. `configure` swallows the resulting missing-asset exception and returns false, so enabling the preference after following the only documented setup still makes every in-app lookup path return unavailable.
- Trigger: Copy the example to `android/acoustid_config.json5`, set a valid key, build and install the app through the maintained Gradle entry point, enable AcoustID web lookups, and open mission ZIP music identification
- Impact: The advertised optional in-app identification feature is disabled in every normally built APK even though the operator supplied a valid key exactly as instructed; only offline maintenance scripts receive the configuration, and the silent fallback gives no actionable explanation.
- Expected: One documented configuration source deterministically reaches each intended consumer, and a configured release proves the Android runtime can load the reviewed key while an unconfigured release clearly reports that the optional service is unavailable.
- Suggested fix: Add an explicit Gradle generation step that validates the ignored root config and writes a generated asset for configured builds, or document and enforce a separate ignored asset input if the script and app configurations are intentionally distinct. Keep generated secret-bearing files out of source control and build outputs not intended for distribution, and fail configured builds on malformed or placeholder values instead of silently omitting the feature.
- Validation: Build configured and unconfigured debug, internal, and release variants from clean trees; inspect the resulting APK assets, invoke `AcoustIdClient.configure`, and perform a mocked lookup without external traffic. Assert a valid configured build loads the exact intended key, missing and malformed inputs produce an explicit unavailable state, placeholders cannot ship accidentally, and maintenance scripts continue to read the same documented source.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-29. Gradle now validates the ignored root `acoustid_config.json5`, rejects malformed, non-string, non-alphanumeric, short, long, and placeholder keys, deletes stale generated output when the source is absent, and exposes the accepted key only through an ignored build-generated asset used by every Android variant. Runtime loading independently validates the asset and reports available, not packaged, or invalid states in Advanced Settings and mission music analysis. The focused valid, missing, malformed, placeholder, stale-output, and runtime parser matrix passed, as did scoped code quality, 546 JVM tests, configured and unconfigured debug APKs, the configured internal APK, release asset merging, all three debug native ABIs, exact package-to-source key checks without printing the key, and `git diff --check`. Full release APK assembly remains blocked by the unrelated existing `d1/3d/interp.c` release-only `nest_count` compile error; the release merged asset was independently verified to contain the intended key.

### BR-0177: P2 - Parse tracklist strings without regex comment removal

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/api-data-format
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0071
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L383-L387` in `Read-Json5File`
- Related: `game_data/fingerprint_mission_zip_music.ps1:L390-L441,L629-L638` and `game_data/mission_files/*.tracklist.json`
- Evidence: The helper removes `//...` and `/*...*/` with regular expressions before JSON parsing, without recognizing quoted strings. A focused in-memory probe using the supported tracklist schema and title `https://example.invalid/song` was truncated after `https:` and failed `ConvertFrom-Json`. The same corruption applies to literal comment markers in titles, filenames, source paths, and the AcoustID configuration. Current tracklists contain no such strings, so ordinary corpus validation cannot detect it.
- Trigger: Add a valid tracklist or configuration string containing a URL, a double-slash archive path, or literal `/*` and `*/` text, then fingerprint its mission
- Impact: The complete mission is reported as failed before extraction or naming, preventing reproducible soundtrack metadata generation for values that are legal in JSON and in the declared sidecar fields
- Expected: Comment syntax is recognized only outside quoted strings, or strict `.json` sidecars are parsed directly without a lossy preprocessing pass
- Suggested fix: Use one maintained JSON5 parser shared by repository tooling, or keep `.tracklist.json` strict and pass it directly to `ConvertFrom-Json`; parse the separate JSON5 configuration with a string-aware implementation. Add schema validation after parsing instead of transforming string contents.
- Validation: Parse sidecars containing escaped quotes, backslashes, `https://`, double-slash paths, literal block-comment markers, real line and block comments where supported, and malformed unterminated comments; assert valid string bytes survive exactly, malformed input fails clearly, and all current tracklists retain their names.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0094): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/tests/extract_regression_spec_helpers.ps1:L1-L8` in the shared regression-spec `Read-Json5File`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0094): The regression helper independently applies the same line-comment regex without recognizing strings and also removes comma-before-brace patterns without recognizing strings. It parses every extraction spec and `known_discs.json5`, and its canonical writer preserves arbitrary extra fields, so a legal URL, double-slash path, or literal comma-plus-closing-delimiter value in a name, provenance field, or future extension can be truncated or rewritten before validation, test execution, result persistence, or regeneration. An in-memory probe with `{"url":"https://example.invalid/a","literal":"x,}"}` was reduced to an unterminated `{"url":"https:` document. The current disc and extraction corpus contains no such string, so happy-path parsing does not cover the boundary.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0094): Exercise the shared parser and canonical round trip with extraction specs and known-disc records containing escaped quotes and backslashes, URLs, double-slash paths, comma-plus-brace and comma-plus-bracket strings, literal comment markers, and real supported comments. Require exact string preservation, deterministic canonical output, and clear rejection of malformed input.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-30. Mission `.tracklist.json` sidecars now use strict JSON validation and parsing without preprocessing. AcoustID configuration, generated fingerprint metadata, extraction regression specs, and `known_discs.json5` now share one string-aware JSON5-subset parser that removes line comments, block comments, and trailing commas only outside quoted strings and reports unterminated block comments explicitly. Focused regression coverage preserves URLs, double-slash paths, escaped quotes and backslashes, literal comment markers, and comma-plus-closer strings; rejects comments in strict tracklists and malformed block comments; and parses all 7 current tracklists, 34 current fingerprint/config JSON5 files, and 34 CD regression specs. Fingerprint budget, AcoustID regeneration, extraction workflow, regression-spec generation, master regeneration, scoped code quality, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0075: P1 - Publish and access the native fingerprint database atomically

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: concurrency/memory-safety
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0035
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_fingerprint.c:L25-L68,L187-L273`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/chromaprint_db.c:L36-L40,L42-L52,L158-L259,L311-L368`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L23-L59,L193-L209,L442-L467`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCache.kt:L102-L119`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L1597-L1611,L1645-L1678`
- Evidence: Every JNI database operation directly accesses one process-global mutable entry array, count, thresholds, and owned fingerprint pointers without a lock, immutable snapshot, or thread-affinity assertion. Loading begins by freeing the currently published pointers, writes shared entry slots while parsing, and publishes the count only at the end; matching reads that count and dereferences the same pointers, while free destroys them. Kotlin's `dbLoaded` check and assignment are also unsynchronized. Fingerprint initialization and matching are invoked from independent `Dispatchers.IO` workflows for mission-cache identification, music import, and disc import. Two callers can both observe `dbLoaded == false`; a later loader can free the first loader's newly published entries while its caller is already matching, or the loaders can race while overwriting shared pointers and counts. Those C data races are undefined behavior and include concrete use-after-free, leak, and inconsistent-result schedules
- Trigger: Start two fingerprint-backed imports or identification jobs during initial database loading, or overlap a future reload/free with any match or count operation
- Impact: User-driven concurrent background work can crash the Android process in native code, corrupt or leak heap-owned fingerprints, return identities from partially replaced state, or permanently mark an incomplete database as loaded; timing-dependent failures are unlikely to be reproduced by the current single-threaded native tests
- Expected: Initialization constructs a complete private database and atomically publishes it once; matches retain a stable snapshot for their whole call, and reload or teardown cannot free storage still visible to a reader
- Suggested fix: Give the database an explicit owner and lifecycle. Serialize Kotlin initialization with a mutex or single deferred result, build native entries off to the side, and publish an immutable snapshot under a native read/write lock or reference-counted handle; keep the old snapshot alive until readers finish. Synchronize threshold state with the same publication and remove or guard the unused free export
- Validation: Add a JNI stress test that barriers two initial loads and many matches, counts, threshold updates, reloads, and frees at each lifecycle boundary; run it repeatedly under ThreadSanitizer where supported and AddressSanitizer on Android, assert one complete published count and deterministic match results, and verify no race, use-after-free, double free, or leak is reported
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31 after independent native and Kotlin review calls R1-CHUNK-0142 and R1-CHUNK-0381 confirmed the original P1. Kotlin database initialization is now a JVM-synchronized single-flight operation, preventing concurrent asset parsing, configuration, and publication. One native lifecycle mutex serializes threshold and duration updates, complete private database construction and replacement, matching, count access, and teardown. Successful matches copy owned metadata while the database lock is held, so JNI never retains storage that a reload or free can invalidate; JNI releases those copies after constructing the typed Java result. The unused JNI free export was removed. A new barriered stress test races two publishers with six readers, count queries, configuration updates, reloads, and frees, then checks eight parallel readers against one final generation. It passed 100 consecutive executions, stable-result lifetime was also verified across reload and rejected publication, all 20 native CTests passed, Android native builds and Kotlin compilation passed for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, JVM bytecode showed `ACC_SYNCHRONIZED`, scoped code quality and `git diff --check` passed. ThreadSanitizer is unavailable on the Windows host, and the MSVC AddressSanitizer attempt could not link because `clang_rt.asan_dynamic_runtime_thunk-x86_64.lib` is not installed. Full APK assembly and JVM test execution were separately blocked before this code by Play Games v2 requiring minSdk 24 while the app declares 23.

### BR-0074: P2 - Preserve complete fingerprint match metadata across JNI

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: api-data-format
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0035
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_fingerprint.c:L189-L223,L226-L256`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/chromaprint_db.h:L12-L29`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/chromaprint_db.c:L66-L85,L189-L250,L342-L350`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L37-L42,L386-L394,L421-L433`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L1645-L1678`, and `android/app/src/main/assets/known_discs.json5:L745-L761`
- Evidence: Kotlin flattens the complete chosen track name into JSON, but the native database parser silently stores only 63 bytes in fixed 64-byte `name` and `disc_id` arrays. Both JNI match exports then serialize those already-truncated fields into a pipe-delimited string, and Kotlin accepts the result as the authoritative match metadata. This is not merely a future limit: several active checked-in fingerprint records at known-discs lines 745 through 761 choose ASCII `acoustid_name` values from 88 through 136 characters, such as the 89-character artist-and-title at line 745, so successful matches return visibly truncated names today. A future valid name or identifier containing `|` also shifts Kotlin's four split fields and makes the match unparsable or misassigned; the JSON parser removes escape markers rather than implementing complete JSON string decoding, so control escapes cannot round-trip exactly either
- Trigger: Match any current database entry whose chosen name exceeds 63 bytes, or add a valid name or disc identifier containing a pipe, JSON control escape, or multibyte text whose 63-byte cut splits a character
- Impact: Imported music and disc tracks can be labeled and cached with incomplete or malformed names, distinct long names can collapse to the same displayed prefix, and otherwise successful matches can be discarded solely because metadata cannot cross the bridge's undocumented fixed-width delimiter protocol
- Expected: Every admitted database entry preserves its exact validated Unicode name and identifier, and JNI returns independently typed fields without delimiter ambiguity or silent byte truncation
- Suggested fix: Parse the flattened data with a complete JSON implementation into budgeted dynamically sized UTF-8 fields, reject rather than truncate any explicit size limit, and return a Java `MatchResult` object or separate typed JNI fields instead of a formatted string. If a wire encoding is retained, use a length-delimited or standard structured format and validate conversion at both boundaries
- Validation: Add round-trip match cases for the current longest checked-in names, exact-limit and one-byte-over ASCII, multibyte text at the boundary, quotes, backslashes, controls, and pipes in both name and disc ID; assert every accepted value returns exactly or is rejected during atomic database loading, and verify the music picker and cache persist the complete expected label
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. The native database now uses the pinned nlohmann JSON parser to validate every complete flattened entry and stores exact dynamically sized UTF-8 metadata under explicit 4,096-byte per-field and 1 MiB fingerprint budgets. A load that contains malformed, undecodable, embedded-NUL, or over-budget data is rejected before replacing the current database. Matching returns stable field pointers, and both JNI match paths construct the existing typed Kotlin `MatchResult` directly instead of formatting and splitting a bounded pipe-delimited string. JNI converts UTF-8 through a length-aware Java byte-array constructor, preserving supplementary and embedded delimiter characters. Focused native matching preserved a current-style long name, pipes in both fields, quotes, backslashes, a newline, Unicode escapes, exact-limit ASCII and multibyte UTF-8, and rejected one byte over without replacing the prior database. The generated JVM constructor descriptor matched the JNI signature, scoped code quality, all 19 native CTests, Android debug assembly and Kotlin compilation for all three native ABIs, and `git diff --check` passed. BR-0075 subsequently fixed synchronization of publication and readers.

### BR-0505: P2 - Preserve CD track identity when names are missing

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-mapping
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0441
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L1162-L1180` in CD preview row construction
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/FingerprintBridge.kt:L106-L145,L229-L253,L256-L297,L300-L375`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AudioSourceManager.kt:L125-L153,L496-L535`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/CdPreviewBridge.kt:L35-L71`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/cd_preview.c:L445-L518`
- Evidence: `AudioSource.trackNames` is explicitly keyed by the CUE's 1-based physical track number. Both known-disc lookup and all four fingerprint paths omit entries with placeholders or no match, so the map can be sparse. The preview sorts that sparse map, discards its keys, and selects `values.elementAtOrNull(i - 1)` while iterating `1..audioTrackCount`. Native preview interprets the resulting `i` as the 1-based ordinal among audio tracks. If an earlier audio track has no name but a later track matches, compacting the map shifts the later name onto the earlier audio row; each subsequent row remains shifted until names run out. Tapping the mislabeled row passes its row ordinal to native code, which correctly opens that different audio track. The maintained visibility tests cover only source filtering and path resolution, and no test exercises sparse physical-track names or row-to-preview identity.
- Trigger: Import or register a disc whose fingerprinting leaves any nonfinal audio track unmatched while a later audio track matches, then open Preview Track List and tap a row at or after the gap
- Impact: The launcher displays later songs under the wrong track numbers and plays audio different from the selected label. Users can misidentify imported music and cannot reliably validate the disc or fingerprint results through the provided preview UI.
- Expected: Each preview row retains one parsed CUE track identity and derives its optional name by that track's physical number while passing its separate audio ordinal to native preview; a missing name affects only its own row.
- Suggested fix: Parse or persist the ordered audio-track descriptors alongside each source, then build rows from those descriptors with both physical track number and audio ordinal. Look up `trackNames[physicalTrackNumber]` directly and fall back only for that row. If retaining only summary metadata, persist an explicit audio-ordinal-to-physical-track map rather than compacting sparse values.
- Validation: Add focused row-builder and preview integration tests with leading and interleaved data tracks, complete names, no names, and gaps at the first, middle, and last audio tracks. Assert every displayed name stays bound to its physical CUE track, fallback labels appear only at gaps, and tapping each row sends the exact corresponding audio ordinal and plays the expected sector range.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-02. Audio sources now persist the ordered physical CUE track numbers for audio tracks, and local, SAF, and GOG registration populate that identity from parsed CUE data. Preview rows look up optional names by physical track number while retaining a separate 1-based audio ordinal for native playback; an absent identity mapping falls back to unnamed rows instead of compacting sparse names. A focused interleaved-data fixture verifies that a missing middle name does not shift later labels or playback ordinals, persistence round-trips the mapping, and GOG registration records the expected physical tracks. The three focused JVM suites, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, scoped Kotlin quality checks, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0459: P2 - Preserve shared tree grants when removing one audio source

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime/permissions
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0404
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SafUriPermissions.kt:L35-L53` in direct and owning-tree permission release
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AudioSourceManager.kt:L176-L185,L190-L219`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MusicPickerPage.kt:L728-L739`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/AdvancedSettingsPage.kt:L1924-L1940`, `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/SafUriPermissionsTest.kt:L60-L76`, and BR-0419
- Evidence: Persisting a selected document deliberately falls back from the document URI to its owning tree URI, so multiple registered BIN and CUE documents under one selected tree can depend on the same single persisted grant. The assigned low-level release function always attempts both the document URI and its derived tree URI without knowing whether another tracked document still needs that tree. Both visible single-source removal flows pass a `Context` to `AudioSourceManager.removeSource`, which calls that releaser for every URI before deleting the source and never supplies the other sources' retained URIs. Removing either of two sources under one tree therefore releases their shared tree permission and leaves the other source registered but inaccessible. The same manager's `clearAll` already snapshots removed and retained URIs and calls `revokeUnusedPersistedReadPermissions`; the focused helper test explicitly requires the shared tree permission to be kept while a retained tracked URI remains. That retained-aware path is bypassed only by ordinary single-source removal, and no focused removal test covers it. BR-0419 records the opposite custom-audio leak caused by never revoking unused permissions and staged bytes; it does not cover premature revocation of a permission still owned by a retained CD source.
- Trigger: Register two in-place CD audio sources whose document URIs are beneath the same SAF tree on a provider where direct document persistence fails and the owning-tree fallback succeeds, then remove either source from the music picker or Advanced Settings
- Impact: The remaining source stays listed and can remain enabled, but its BIN or CUE documents lose durable access. Playlist generation, preview, or playback can fail immediately or after process restart until the user relinks the source; one removal can break every retained disc source sharing that tree.
- Expected: A persisted document or tree permission is released only when no retained tracked URI is covered by it, regardless of whether cleanup removes one source or all sources.
- Suggested fix: In `removeSource`, snapshot the removed source URIs and all other retained source URIs, perform managed-file cleanup without direct permission release, remove and save the source, then call `revokeUnusedPersistedReadPermissions(context, removedTrackedUris, retainedTrackedUris)`. Restrict `releaseReadPermissionForUri` to explicit user removal of the permission itself or require an ownership decision at its API boundary so ordinary resource cleanup cannot revoke a shared tree blindly.
- Validation: Use a resolver or instrumented DocumentsProvider with two and many BIN/CUE documents under one tree, direct-document grants, tree-fallback grants, mixed grants, duplicate URIs, and sources spanning different trees. Remove sources in every order through both UI entry points and across restart; require every retained source to remain readable, only the final unused grant to be released, exact unrelated grants to survive, and clear-all and explicit permission removal to retain their documented behavior.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Single-source removal now snapshots the removed source's SAF document URIs and every remaining source's SAF document URIs, deletes managed artifacts, durably saves the reduced source registry, and then delegates to the existing retained-aware persisted-permission cleanup. All three UI removal paths provide a context, so none silently bypass final unused-grant cleanup. The blind document-plus-tree release helper was renamed and is now restricted to the explicit permission-removal action. Focused manager coverage proves that removing one of two document sources beneath a shared tree preserves the tree grant, reloads only the retained source from disk, and makes the grant releasable after the final source is removed. All seven focused AudioSourceManager and SAF permission tests, scoped Kotlin code quality, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0456: P2 - Publish imported game files only after an exact copy

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-integrity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0402
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LauncherFileCopy.kt:L24-L63` in stream completion and URI-to-file publication
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupFileImport.kt:L143-L174`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L3935-L4005`, `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/LauncherFileCopyTest.kt:L10-L32`, BR-0418, and BR-0443
- Evidence: `copyStream` treats the first nonpositive read as successful completion and returns the byte count without comparing it with a positive `bytesTotal`. `copyUriToFile` obtains that provider-declared size but opens `FileOutputStream(dest)` on the final game-asset pathname immediately, truncating any previous file, and likewise performs no count check before returning. Direct setup import treats every normal return as success, logs the resulting length, hashes whatever prefix was written, upserts that prefix and its size into the asset manifest, increments the imported count, and reports it in the completion summary. A provider that reports the complete asset size but reaches EOF early therefore publishes and records truncated bytes as a successful import. A read, write, flush, or close exception is caught as failure only after the prior destination has been truncated or partially replaced; the partial file remains under the final name and the prior manifest entry is not rolled back. The sole focused copy test uses a complete in-memory stream and asserts no short-read, exception, prior-destination, cleanup, or atomic-publication behavior.
- Trigger: Select a game asset from a cloud, removable, or custom document provider that reports its expected positive size but returns EOF early, or replace an existing valid asset while the provider read, destination write, flush, or close fails after the final file is opened
- Impact: The launcher can announce a truncated HOG, PIG, HAM, S11, S22, or demo as successfully imported and persist its digest and length as authoritative. A failed replacement can also destroy the prior playable asset while leaving its old manifest metadata, making a working set corrupt or unlaunchable and obscuring whether retry or repair is required.
- Expected: A known-length import succeeds only after exactly that many bytes are written, closed, and validated; no incomplete generation is visible under the final asset name, and failure preserves both the prior file and its manifest entry.
- Suggested fix: Copy each attempt to a uniquely owned sibling temporary file, require the byte count to equal every positive provider size, flush and close successfully, optionally verify stable size or digest according to the import contract, and atomically replace the final file only afterward. On unknown size, still publish only after clean EOF and close. Delete only the owned temporary on every failure and update the manifest after commit.
- Validation: Add streams and providers that return exact data, early EOF at zero and multiple boundaries, a transient zero read if supported, extra bytes beyond declared size, and injected read, write, flush, close, move, and process-stop failures. Repeat with no prior destination and with a valid prior file and manifest; require no successful short copy, no visible partial file, preservation of the prior generation and metadata on failure, exact committed bytes on success, and accurate progress and import counts.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0446): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L3935-L4014,L4251-L4306` in reentrant direct and archive import result actions
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0446): Neither Import All nor Import to Current Set is disabled after its coroutine starts. Each first job updates hashing state and suspends in IO while the same result card and enabled button remain composed, so a second activation can open the same final asset path concurrently. The direct path can let a short or failed later writer replace a complete earlier copy and race manifest publication. The archive path additionally shares one extracted temporary generation; either job calls `cleanupTmpDir` on completion, which can delete a source while the other job is still copying it after already truncating the destination. Per-file catches only reduce an import count and do not restore the prior or first successful generation.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0446): Barrier-synchronize two and repeated activations before open, after destination truncation, during copy, before manifest upsert, and around temporary cleanup for direct and archive result cards, with complete, short, throwing, and slow sources plus a valid prior destination. Require one admitted import generation, disabled or idempotent actions until terminal completion, attempt-owned temporary lifetime, exact atomic destination bytes, one matching manifest generation, and truthful status after recreation.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0449): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L222-L280,L339-L358,L876-L910` in shared network and provider staging copies
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0449): `setupDownloadFile` writes every attempt to the predictable shared `$filename.tmp`, treats the first nonpositive read as complete without comparing `downloaded` with a positive HTTP content length, ignores the result of `renameTo`, and unconditionally reports success. It can therefore announce and consume a short temporary body or claim success while the final file is absent or stale. GOG and SOW staging similarly copy a provider stream directly into a provider-named shared temporary file and proceed after normal return without requiring the copied count to equal a positive provider size; Close remains available during initial staging because `processing` is false and can recursively clean the shared temp directory while the composition-owned IO job is still active.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0449): Add exact, short, oversized, unknown-length, stalled, throwing, and concurrently repeated HTTP and provider sources; inject write, close, rename, dismissal, cancellation, and process-stop failures with and without prior final files and unrelated temporary work. Require exact known-length completion, one attempt-owned temporary generation, checked atomic replacement, preservation of prior destinations, no cleanup of another active attempt, no stale success callback, and complete owned cleanup on failure.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0450): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L1118-L1197,L1402-L1416,L1490-L1509` in temporary and registered CUE copies
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0450): Disc analysis copies the provider CUE directly into one shared provider-derived `filesDir/tmp` pathname, and Close remains available because `processing` is false throughout initial staging and parsing. Dismissal cancels the composition but neither Close nor Done deletes `tempCuePath`, and no dialog-owned cleanup runs after parse failure, cancellation, or successful registration. Audio registration later copies that shared file directly onto the final registered CUE path before registry publication. A short or failed copy can expose partial bytes or destroy a prior same-name generation, while abandoned CUE files accumulate in the shared temporary directory and can be reused or deleted by unrelated cleanup.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0450): Stage and register exact, short, changing, throwing, slow, and unknown-length CUE providers; inject dismissal, cancellation, write, close, registry, and process-stop failures around both copies, with prior final CUEs and unrelated active temporary work. Require unique attempt-owned staging, exact known-length completion, checked atomic final replacement only with registry commit, preservation of prior registered bytes, and complete owned cleanup without touching another attempt.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0451): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDiscImport.kt:L114-L170,L223-L270,L393-L483,L492-L523` in generated GOG CUE, nested-file replacement, path-backed CUE registration, and merged SAF artifacts
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0451): Known GOG registration creates its missing `.inst` directly with `writeText`; interruption can leave a partial file that every retry accepts merely because an `.inst` now exists. Nested-file hoisting deletes an existing root asset before attempting the preferred candidate's rename or copy, so failure destroys the prior generation. Local-path registration copies the selected CUE directly onto its final app-private source name before the source registry commits. The currently uncalled merged-SAF helper likewise truncates its final BIN, appends every provider stream without checking copied bytes against positive per-image sizes, and only afterward writes the final CUE; a later source or CUE failure leaves a partial pair under managed final names. None uses a unique attempt generation, checked exact copy, atomic replacement, or rollback with the source registry.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0451): Generate, hoist, register, and merge game and CUE/BIN sources with exact, short, extra, throwing, and changing inputs; inject delete, rename, copy, write, close, process-stop, second-image, CUE-write, and registry failures with prior valid artifacts. Require exact declared bytes, one validated attempt-owned generation, atomic artifact-and-registry publication, preservation of every prior source on failure, and repair rather than acceptance of partial generated CUEs.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0452): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupFileImport.kt:L195-L334,L378-L467,L469-L621,L623-L662` in archive staging, flattened extraction records, shared cleanup, and progress copies
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0452): ZIP and 7z write flattened entries directly to shared `filesDir/tmp` basenames and append an `ExtractedFile` after each write. A later same-basename entry or nested SOW extraction can overwrite that path while the earlier record retains its prior digest and size, so consumers copy different bytes than the metadata they identify and publish. StuffIt uses a deterministic sanitized work-directory name, 7z uses fixed `.tmp_7z_import`, RAR uses a millisecond directory plus fixed `.tmp_rar_import`, and both cleanup helpers recursively delete the complete shared temp root. Concurrent or reentrant attempts can therefore overwrite stages or delete sources owned by another import. The shared copy helper and archive loops also accept the first nonpositive read as completion without requiring a positive provider or entry size to be copied exactly.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0452): Import duplicate and case-colliding flattened ZIP, nested SOW, 7z, RAR, and sanitized-name StuffIt inputs under synchronized concurrent attempts; inject early EOF, zero reads, extra bytes, write, close, move, cancellation, cleanup, and process-stop failures. Require unique attempt-owned staging, exact declared lengths, immutable result-path-to-digest-and-size relationships, no cross-attempt deletion, prior-generation preservation, and atomic final publication only after complete validation.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0456): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L1541-L1594` in mission external-document cache extraction
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0456): External view first deletes the deterministic cache destination and then either calls `copyTo(overwrite = true)` or opens `FileOutputStream` directly on that final FileProvider-backed pathname. It does not stage to a unique sibling, compare the copied count with the constituent's positive declared size, or remove a partial file on read, write, flush, close, or cancellation failure. Repeated taps can launch concurrent coroutines against the same destination, so a failed or slower writer can truncate or mix the bytes another attempt is about to grant to a viewer. BR-0515 separately covers mutation or eviction after a URI has already been granted.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0456): Extract direct and durable external constituents with exact, short, extra, throwing, slow, and changing sources; inject read, write, flush, close, cancellation, process-stop, and synchronized repeated-open failures with and without a prior complete cache file. Require a unique attempt file, exact declared-length completion, checked close and atomic publication, preservation of the prior complete generation, removal of owned residue, and no FileProvider URI until one immutable complete file commits.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. The shared launcher copy boundary now treats a transient zero read as nonterminal, rejects every short or oversized positive-length stream, writes to a UUID-owned sibling, flushes and syncs the closed generation, and only then replaces the destination while preserving any prior file on failure. Direct imports, file-to-file publication, provider staging, HTTP downloads, registered and generated CUE files, nested game-file hoisting, staged archive publication, and merged SAF BIN/CUE artifacts use that boundary or the matching retained-aware multi-file transaction. Direct and archive result actions are single-flight, initial GOG, SOW, and CUE staging cannot be dismissed during its owned copy, and staged CUE cleanup runs on dismissal and completion. Archive batches use UUID-owned attempt and per-archive directories; ZIP, 7z, and RAR result records own immutable per-entry files, enforce positive declared sizes, and sync before exposure. Mission external viewing already routes through the BR-0515 immutable grant store, which validates the exact constituent size before producing a FileProvider URI. Focused tests cover exact replacement, short and oversized rejection with prior-generation preservation and residue cleanup, transient zero reads, staged publication rollback, and nested-file replacement. All 588 JVM tests, scoped Kotlin code quality, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0086: P2 - Reject incomplete SAF manifests and publish updates atomically

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/input-validation
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0042
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/physfs_archiver_saf.c:L76-L295,L493-L521` in the JSON parser and archive open path
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SafManifest.kt:L27-L57,L59-L93`
- Evidence: The hand-written parser does not require the closing brace for an entry, the closing bracket for `files`, or the root closing brace. If input ends immediately after a complete `size_bytes` number, the current entry is accepted and the accumulated prefix mounts successfully. It also silently skips incomplete entries, accepts malformed separators, implements only a subset of JSON string escapes, and accumulates signed integers without digit or overflow checks. Kotlin writes the whole manifest directly to its final pathname instead of publishing a completed temporary file atomically. A process stop, storage error, concurrent observation, or manual corruption can therefore turn an interrupted JSON document into a valid-looking partial native archive rather than one rejected unit; Kotlin's strict `JSONObject` reader and the native permissive parser can also disagree about the same bytes
- Trigger: Interrupt or partially complete a manifest write after an entry's numeric size, truncate a multi-entry manifest at that point, include unsupported escapes or malformed separators, or supply an overflowing `size_bytes`
- Impact: The native mount can expose an arbitrary valid prefix while omitting later assets, disagree with launcher readiness and pruning, leak superseded field allocations within the capped parse, or invoke undefined signed-overflow behavior instead of reporting a corrupt manifest
- Expected: One schema and validation policy governs native and Kotlin readers, and a manifest becomes visible only after the complete document has been durably and atomically written; any syntax, range, duplicate-field, or incomplete-document error rejects the whole update
- Suggested fix: Prefer a maintained bounded JSON parser or a simpler generated binary/index format shared across the boundary. If the custom parser remains, pass an explicit end pointer through every helper, implement full string escape handling, require digits and checked nonnegative 64-bit conversion, require every delimiter and end of document, define duplicate-field behavior, and free overwritten values. Write to a same-directory temporary file, flush as required, then atomically replace the final manifest; serialize writers
- Validation: Add empty and valid manifests plus truncation at every byte, missing object/array/root delimiters, malformed commas and colons, every JSON escape, invalid and overflowing sizes, duplicate fields and names, interrupted replacement, and concurrent read/write fixtures; assert Kotlin and native parsing agree and any invalid document mounts no entries
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0097): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/tests/test_saf_archiver.ps1:L322-L355` in test manifest publication and verification
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0097): The test pushes a complete local file to one fixed remote temporary name, then uses `cp` directly onto the final `.saf_manifest.json` and verifies only that the resulting text contains `descent2.ham`. It neither publishes atomically nor proves strict parsing, complete delimiters, numeric validity, exact fields, or Kotlin/native agreement. An interrupted copy can leave the same partial final file covered by this finding, while the test offers no failure oracle for it.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0097): Route valid publication through the production writer, inject interruption and partial-copy failures at the test boundary, parse the observed final bytes with both readers, and require preservation of the prior complete generation or complete rejection.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. The SAF schema now has one bounded native parser shared by both Android game targets: it requires the exact root, array, entry fields, separators, closing delimiters, and end of document; handles every JSON escape and valid surrogate pair; rejects null characters, unknown or duplicate fields, case-ambiguous filenames, leading-zero, negative, fractional, and overflowing sizes; and frees the whole generation on any error. Kotlin applies the same field, type, range, null, and filename-identity policy. Manifest writes now use the synchronized `AtomicFilePublication` boundary to write and fsync a same-directory sibling before rename, and read-modify-write operations retain their lock through publication so invalid or interrupted generations cannot replace complete state. The SAF emulator fixture also stages then renames complete manifests, verifies the exact schema, has a focused incomplete-document rejection mode, and restores moved game data on every tested failure exit. The host parser CTest passed empty and valid manifests, every truncated prefix, malformed delimiters and separators, all escape classes, invalid and overflowing sizes, duplicate fields and filenames, and schema errors. All JVM unit tests, scoped code quality, `git diff --check`, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, the native incomplete-manifest emulator rejection, and the valid mount-and-descriptor emulator regression passed.

### BR-0085: P1 - Stage nonseekable SAF descriptors before exposing them as PhysicsFS files

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: compatibility/io-contract
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0042, R1-CHUNK-0043, R1-CHUNK-0097
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/physfs_archiver_saf.c:L311-L350,L548-L568,L603-L618` in `safio_read`, `safio_seek`, `SAF_openRead`, and `SAF_stat`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L766-L795`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SafManifest.kt:L98-L125`, `android/tests/test_saf_archiver.ps1:L322-L354`, [ContentResolver.openFileDescriptor](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/ContentResolver#openFileDescriptor(android.net.Uri,%20java.lang.String)), and [ContentProvider.openFile](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/ContentProvider#openFile(android.net.Uri,%20java.lang.String))
- Evidence: `MainActivity.openSafFile` requests a provider descriptor in read-only mode and detaches it without proving that it is seekable. Android explicitly permits `openFileDescriptor` to return a pipe, and `ContentProvider.openFile` permits pipes or sockets for exclusive `r` access. `SAF_openRead` nevertheless wraps every returned descriptor in an I/O object whose reads use `pread` and whose advertised length is only the cached manifest value. A valid streaming or cloud provider therefore returns `ESPIPE` on the first game read even though Kotlin pruning can open its stream successfully and mark the set ready. The test cannot catch this because it bypasses `ContentResolver` and stores an absolute path to a regular app-private file. Independently, `safio_seek` casts the unsigned PhysicsFS offset to signed before validating it, so offsets above `INT64_MAX` can pass the EOF check and create a negative position
- Trigger: Link an asset from a DocumentsProvider that supplies a pipe or other nonseekable read descriptor, change a linked document after its cached size was recorded, or issue a crafted seek above the signed 64-bit range
- Impact: Supported Android document sources can appear present and ready but fail during archive reads, while stale sizes can truncate data or advertise bytes that cannot be read. The oversized seek path can also violate the PhysicsFS I/O invariant and feed invalid offsets into `pread`
- Expected: Every mounted PhysicsFS object is an independent, random-access byte source with a verified current length, and every seek is range-checked before conversion
- Suggested fix: After opening the URI, determine whether the descriptor is a seekable regular object with a trustworthy current size. Copy pipes, virtual/cloud files, and otherwise unsuitable descriptors into an owned seekable cache file before creating the PhysicsFS I/O object, or reject them during linking with a precise user-facing reason. Derive length from the staged or verified descriptor rather than trusting manifest metadata, and validate `offset <= (PHYSFS_uint64)len` before any signed cast. Ensure duplicate objects use independent positions on the same immutable staged content
- Validation: Add an instrumented provider that returns a pipe for `r`, a seekable provider file, a provider whose reported content changes size, short-read behavior, seeks at EOF and beyond EOF, and `UINT64_MAX`; run mounting and representative HOG/mission reads through the production URI path and assert either correct staged random access or an early actionable rejection without readiness claiming success
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0097): The sole end-to-end script deliberately chooses a regular world-readable file in `/data/local/tmp` and encodes its absolute path as `content_uri`. That guarantees `open` and `pread` behavior and bypasses `ContentResolver` completely, so its level-load and search-path assertions provide no coverage for pipes, virtual documents, revoked grants, changing provider sizes, or descriptor duplication.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0097): Keep the direct regular-file fixture as one control, but require provider-backed seekable, pipe-backed, revoked, and size-changing cases in the same required suite so a passing direct-path run cannot be reported as complete SAF compatibility evidence.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Production URI opens now verify that a descriptor is a regular seekable object with an exact readable length; pipes and other unsuitable descriptors are copied completely into an fsynced, app-owned cache file, reopened read-only, and unlinked before their descriptor reaches native PhysicsFS. Staging is serialized, progress and storage are bounded, partial files are cleaned up, and failures propagate through the existing startup mount failure path. The native archiver revalidates and caches the descriptor's current length instead of trusting manifest metadata, duplicates the verified descriptor for each PhysicsFS object so positions remain independent, limits reads to the verified extent, and rejects oversized unsigned seeks before signed conversion. A debug-only provider exercises both production ContentResolver branches. The native seek CTest passed EOF, past-EOF, negative-length, null-output, and UINT64_MAX cases under MSVC /W4. Scoped quality and `git diff --check`, all JVM tests, and Android debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. Focused emulator runs proved that a provider pipe was staged at its exact 1,307,598-byte length and read through the level-briefing boundary, and that a seekable provider whose manifest size was stale mounted using its verified current 1,307,598-byte length without staging; both restored the displaced game asset during cleanup.

### BR-0076: P1 - Scope PhysicsFS mounts to one level-metadata request

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/resource-lifetime
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0036, R1-CHUNK-0037, R1-CHUNK-0090
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_level_metadata.cpp:L278-L447` in `init_levelmeta_runtime`, `mount_request_extra_dir`, and `mount_requested_hogs`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_level_metadata.cpp:L879-L1008`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L776-L847,L979-L1089,L1266-L1311`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LauncherScriptExecutor.kt:L334-L350,L374-L394`, and `android/ai tool plans/asset management/plan_dxa_multi_mod_assessment_20260518.md:L19`
- Evidence: The native runtime is process-global and, after its first successful initialization, ignores every later request's `data_dir`. Each request then prepends `extra_data_dir` and every HOG to the same PhysicsFS search path, but no success, failure, or partial multi-HOG mount path removes them. The repository's own PhysicsFS design notes confirm that `appendToPath=0` gives each later mount higher priority. Kotlin serializes calls with a single-flight guard, but successful analysis only reads the result, deletes its work directory, and returns; unlike timeout handling, it does not kill the per-game worker process. Android may therefore retain the stopped service process and all native state for the next request, and automation explicitly analyzes multiple targets sequentially. A current source wins when it contains a name, but if it omits a declared level, the later `PHYSFSX_exists` and `load_level` search can fall through to an earlier still-mounted HOG with the same common leaf name. A failed request can also leave only its successfully mounted prefix behind. Every retained archive additionally lengthens lookup and keeps PhysicsFS archive state and underlying resources alive for the process lifetime
- Trigger: In one retained D1 or D2 worker, analyze source A containing `level01.rdl` or `level01.rl2`, then analyze source B that declares the same leaf but omits it; alternatively run a long metadata batch or fail after mounting the first of several HOGs
- Impact: Source B can receive a successful level row calculated from source A instead of the required missing-level failure, making launcher results and regenerated checked-in metadata depend on request order. Long batches also accumulate archive mounts and resources until lookup degrades or the worker reaches process limits
- Expected: The base game runtime may be reused only against a documented stable data root, while every request-specific directory and archive is mounted as one transaction and removed in reverse order on all exits before another request can run
- Suggested fix: Introduce a request-scoped mount owner that records each successful directory and HOG mount, rolls back a partial mount set, and unmounts all request sources after analysis. Reset mission-dependent global state before releasing the single-flight slot and reject a later incompatible `data_dir`; if the engine cannot safely return to a clean baseline, terminate the isolated worker after every result instead of reusing it
- Validation: Add an Android JNI integration test that submits two sequential requests to the same retained per-game process with colliding level names and with the second file absent, repeats them in reverse order, and injects failure on a later HOG mount. Assert the second request reports its own missing file, search-path state returns to the base count after every success and failure, outputs are order-independent, and hundreds of sequential targets do not grow mounted archives, descriptors, memory, or lookup time
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0090): `android/game_scripts/test_mission_zip_batch_import_metadata_launch.json5:L15-L16` and `android/game_scripts/test_mission_zip_batch_import_metadata.json5:L15-L16`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0090): Both mission-batch templates import an arbitrary mission package and invoke `analyze_level_metadata_all`; the launcher executor then analyzes every projected mission target sequentially in one retained per-game worker. A multi-mission archive therefore exercises the leaking request mounts inside one nominal regression item, and later targets can resolve common missing level leaves from earlier targets while the templates accept only aggregate target status and count. Batch success and generated metadata can consequently depend on archive target order.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0090): Run each template on a two-mission archive whose first target contains a common level leaf and whose second declares but omits that leaf, then reverse target order. Require the second target to fail in both orders, stable normalized metadata, and the worker search path to return to its base count between targets.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. The live JNI metadata bridge now binds its reusable engine runtime to the first successful request's exact base data directory and rejects incompatible later roots. A request-scoped owner records each successfully prepended staging directory and HOG before publication, rolls back a partial mount sequence, frees successfully loaded mission state while the request paths are still resolvable, clears the dangling engine mission pointer, and unmounts request sources in reverse order on every result path. Cleanup distinguishes archives already removed by mission teardown from mounts still present in the PhysicsFS search path; a retained mount changes the result to a cleanup failure and poisons the worker so no later request can analyze against contaminated state. The focused launcher integration test declares pinned base assets and the Dr. Moreau's Saga mission ZIP, then analyzes that five-level mission three consecutive times through the retained D2 service/JNI worker. It passed all five automation steps on the final installed APK. Scoped C++ quality, `git diff --check`, all JVM tests, and Android debug builds for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. The repository-wide automation catalog still reports its pre-existing `test_extract_regression_template.json5` first-object parse failure; the new test's `_info` and four dependency declarations were parsed separately and passed.
