### BR-0088: P2 - Use content-backed identity for every mission music cache

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/cache-identity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0044, R1-CHUNK-0045, R1-CHUNK-0048, R1-CHUNK-0084, R1-CHUNK-0085
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicStageManager.kt:L21-L29,L136-L143` in cache lookup and staged-file naming
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusic.kt:L23-L34,L243-L290` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCache.kt:L185-L192`
- Evidence: The staged filename hashes only the source basename, byte length, last-modified time, and track-local ID, then accepts an existing cache entry based only on its output length. It omits the canonical source path and any source or entry content hash. Two archives or extracted roots with equal basenames and metadata can therefore map equal track IDs to the same cache file despite different bytes. An in-place replacement that preserves size and timestamp has the same stale-cache behavior. The fingerprint cache repeats the basename, length, and timestamp identity, so downstream fingerprint reuse does not reliably correct the collision.
- Trigger: Stage matching track paths from two different same-named mission archives or directories whose source length, timestamp, and track length agree but whose audio differs, or replace an archive while preserving those metadata fields
- Impact: Preview, AcoustID lookup, fingerprinting, inferred names, and generated sidecars can silently use music from a different mission or a previous version, producing durable incorrect metadata without an error
- Expected: Cache identity distinguishes distinct source objects and invalidates when the relevant bytes change; a hit is validated against that identity rather than length alone
- Suggested fix: Define one shared versioned mission-source identity using at least the canonical source path plus stable file identity and entry metadata, or preferably a lazily computed content digest for the archive and selected entry. Store and verify that identity beside staged bytes, and use it consistently for staging and fingerprint caches. Keep the full digest or a collision-resistant filename length rather than truncating it to 64 bits.
- Validation: Add two same-named archive and directory fixtures with identical lengths and timestamps but different audio, plus an in-place same-metadata replacement; assert each stages and fingerprints its own bytes and unchanged inputs still reuse their cache
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0045): `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicNames.kt:L14-L24` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ModManager.kt:L452-L495,L724-L770`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0045): The non-extracted name sidecar is keyed only by the sanitized owner filename, and `writeMissionZipMusicNames` accepts any existing regular file without recording or comparing the source archive's size, timestamp, digest, catalog, or schema. Reimport replaces the mission file and extracted owner state but does not delete this sidecar, so even an obviously different archive imported under the same display filename keeps and later copies the previous mission's decoded names.
- Additional trigger (R1-CHUNK-0045): Import a ZIP mission with recognized music, then import different ZIP content under the same display filename without first deleting the old mod
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0045): Reimport same-name missions with changed length, timestamp, track inventory, and same-metadata content; assert the sidecar and every upstream cache are invalidated by one shared identity while unchanged content is reused.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0048): `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCache.kt:L40-L60,L93-L120,L185-L197` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L840-L865,L911-L913,L1225-L1229`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0048): The fingerprint cache does have an exact content-hash lookup, but the details dialog first calls `cachedEntries`, which accepts only archive basename, length, and timestamp and returns records without staging or hashing current audio. `missionZipMusicTracksNeedingLocalAnalysis` then treats the presence of a track ID as proof that analysis is complete, so colliding or same-metadata replacement archives never reach `identifyLocal` and its SHA-256 check. The stale cached fingerprint and decoded name are displayed immediately, and automatic local analysis explicitly skips the track.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0048): Exercise dialog-open and automatic-analysis selection, not only direct `get`, with same-named archives and in-place replacements that preserve length and timestamp; assert no cached annotation is exposed before content-backed validation and changed audio is restaged and re-fingerprinted.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0084): `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicStageManagerTest.kt:L14-L143` and `MissionZipMusicNamesTest.kt:L12-L82`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0084): Every staging case creates a distinct cache root and stages each source only once, so none can observe a same-key cache hit, two same-named sources with equal metadata, or in-place replacement. The sidecar cases write directly to clean output parents and do not exercise owner-filename cache reuse or same-name mission replacement.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0084): Reuse one cache root for same-named, equal-size, equal-mtime archives and directories with different selected bytes, restage after an in-place same-metadata replacement, and exercise sidecar cache reuse through import and launch; require content-correct invalidation while unchanged content still hits.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0085): `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCacheTest.kt:L12-L78` and `MissionZipMusicAnalysisSkipTest.kt:L8-L28`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0085): The relocation test intentionally uses identical archive bytes with equal basename, size, and timestamp, while the invalidation test changes size. Neither uses different source bytes with all accepted metadata preserved. The analysis-skip tests then treat any supplied track-ID record as current without validating archive or content identity.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0085): Preserve basename, size, timestamp, and track ID while changing archive and staged bytes, then exercise `cachedEntries` and pending-analysis selection; require invalidation before stale data is displayed or skipped, while byte-identical relocation remains reusable.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. Mission inspection now computes one versioned SHA-256 source identity from the complete archive bytes or the relevant extracted source files plus parsed track selectors. Staging uses the full content-backed identity and verifies a companion marker before reuse; fingerprint manifests use the same identity under a v2 schema, so `cachedEntries` cannot expose metadata-only collisions before analysis selection; mission-name sidecars carry and verify the identity on import and launch, with fail-closed marker publication. Regressions cover same-named archives and extracted directories with equal metadata but different audio, in-place same-metadata replacement, byte-identical relocation, unchanged staged-file reuse, sidecar invalidation, and analysis-skip behavior. The focused Kotlin suite and main/test compilation passed, scoped formatting and `git diff --check` passed. Validation temporarily used Play Games 21.0.0 because the unrelated current 22.0.0 setting requires minSdk 24 while the app declares 23; the configured 22.0.0 value was restored immediately afterward.

### BR-0089: P2 - Publish mission music cache artifacts completely and atomically

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/transactionality
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0044, R1-CHUNK-0045, R1-CHUNK-0048, R1-CHUNK-0084, R1-CHUNK-0085
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicStageManager.kt:L36-L84,L309-L365,L377-L414` in cache publication and HOG reads
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L950-L977` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MidiBytesPreviewDialog.kt:L78-L92`
- Evidence: HOG extraction copies until EOF and reports success even when fewer bytes than the declared entry size were read; the in-memory HOG path likewise returns a short array without rejecting it. Staging never compares a newly written temporary file with the known track size. Publication uses one deterministic `.tmp` path, deletes the previous final file before rename, and falls back to copying directly into the final pathname. Concurrent requests for the same key can interfere with each other's temporary file, and interruption or fallback failure can leave a partial final file. Tracks with unknown size accept any later nonempty residue as a cache hit.
- Trigger: Supply a truncated HOG entry, interrupt or exhaust storage during the rename fallback, or stage the same uncached track concurrently from independent preview, fingerprint, or sidecar callers
- Impact: Callers can receive or permanently reuse truncated audio or MIDI as if staging succeeded; concurrent operations can fail nondeterministically or publish each other's partial data
- Expected: Declared entries are copied exactly, incomplete input is rejected, and only one fully written and validated file becomes visible at the final cache path
- Suggested fix: Require exact declared byte counts and validate known output size before publication. Write each attempt to a uniquely named file in the target directory, flush and close it, then atomically move it into place without first deleting the valid destination; serialize or safely coalesce same-key writers. On platforms without atomic replacement, retain the old file until a complete replacement is ready and never stream directly to the public cache pathname.
- Validation: Add short HOG payload and MIDI fixtures, forced short reads, write and move failure injection, cancellation at each publication step, and concurrent same-key staging; assert failures expose no partial cache hit, a prior valid entry survives replacement failure, and successful callers receive identical complete bytes
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0045): `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicNames.kt:L55-L69` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/ModManager.kt:L724-L770`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0045): The name sidecar is written directly to its final pathname. A process stop, storage failure, or exception after truncation can leave a partial file, and subsequent import or launch treats `isFile` alone as completion and copies that partial JSON into the active mission directory. The native reader can accept a syntactically complete prefix, so failure may yield incomplete labels rather than a clean rejection.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0045): Inject failure and process cancellation at each sidecar write boundary, then retry import and launch; assert only a completely generated and validated temporary file is atomically published, a prior valid sidecar survives failure, and no partial file suppresses regeneration.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0048): `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCache.kt:L61-L104,L121-L171` in fingerprint-cache read-modify-write and publication
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0048): Every record or local/web update independently loads the complete shared JSON file, modifies its snapshot, and writes directly to the final pathname with no lock or atomic replacement. Concurrent dialog, import-sidecar, or launch-sidecar workers can each read the same old snapshot and let the last writer silently discard the other's new track or newer local/web fields. Process death or a storage failure after truncation leaves invalid JSON; `loadEntries` then converts the entire parse failure to an empty list, and the next successful update overwrites the file with only that one record. A stale `Entry` passed to either update method can also restore old fields while replacing the current record.
- Additional suggested fix (R1-CHUNK-0048): Serialize cache transactions under one process-wide lock, reload and merge the latest record inside that transaction, write a uniquely named same-directory temporary file, flush and close it, and atomically replace the final file while retaining the previous valid generation on failure. Treat parse failure as recoverable corruption requiring quarantine or restoration, not an empty authoritative cache.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0048): Race distinct-track records and local versus web updates from multiple cache instances, inject interruption and write failure at every publication boundary, and submit stale entry objects; assert no update is lost, fields merge monotonically, readers see either complete generation, and a prior valid cache survives.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0084): `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipMusicStageManagerTest.kt:L14-L143,L168-L220` and `MissionZipMusicNamesTest.kt:L12-L82`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0084): All staged HOG payloads are complete four-byte arrays whose declared lengths equal available data, calls are sequential, and the helpers provide no short-read, write, move, cancellation, or same-key race seam. The names test likewise covers only a successful direct final write and deletion, so the registered tests remain green when truncated HOG data or interrupted and competing cache or sidecar publication exposes partial state.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0084): Add declared-longer-than-available HOG fixtures for both compressed audio and MIDI, unique-temp and same-key barrier races, injected temporary write and move failures, and sidecar cancellation after truncation; assert exact failure, preservation of any prior valid generation, and no visible partial file.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0085): `android/app/src/test/java/com/dxxredux/app/MissionZipAudioFingerprintCacheTest.kt:L80-L157`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0085): The JSON ordering and local/web preservation tests perform one cache update at a time from the latest returned entry and inspect only successful final writes. They do not race independent cache instances, update from stale entries, inject truncation or write failure, or start from corrupt JSON, so the shared read-modify-write and direct-publication failure modes remain unobserved.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0085): Barrier-race distinct records and local versus web updates from stale and current entries, inject failure at each write boundary, and seed malformed cache JSON; require merged fields, no lost record, and either a complete old or complete new generation.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. A shared process-wide publication transaction now writes unique same-directory temporary artifacts, flushes and syncs file content, publishes by rename, and retains or restores the previous generation when replacement cannot complete. Staged audio is built as a private directory generation containing both bytes and a size-bound identity marker; known sizes are verified before publication, unknown sizes are recorded in the marker, same-key writers coalesce under the transaction, and short HOG audio or MIDI is rejected. Mission name sidecars now carry their source identity inside one flat JSON artifact and atomically replace the prior file. Fingerprint cache reads and read-modify-write updates share the same lock across instances, stale local and web results merge against the latest record, complete JSON is atomically published, and malformed generations are quarantined rather than treated as authoritative emptiness. Regressions cover truncated HOG payloads, concurrent staging and fingerprint writers, pre-move cancellation, move failure with old-generation restoration, stale-field merging, and corruption quarantine. Ktlint, `git diff --check`, the focused cache suites, and the complete Android JVM unit suite passed. Validation temporarily used Play Games 21.0.0 because the configured 22.0.0 requires minSdk 24 while the app declares 23; 22.0.0 was restored after each run.

### BR-0199: P2 - Apply launcher graphics changes before rewriting config

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/state-synchronization
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0103
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/android_graphics_options.c:L214-L220,L230-L353` in per-option persistence
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L1844-L1873`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupConfigFiles.kt:L39-L60,L90-L117`, `d1/main/config.c:L341-L398`, `d2/main/config.c:L362-L421`, and `android/ai tool plans/overlay, menu, etc/plan_android_original_graphics_options_reuse_study.md:L262-L287`
- Evidence: The launcher writes a changed graphics key and increments a generation while the game task remains alive. On resume, `applyGraphicsSettingsPrefs` reads and applies keys one at a time, beginning with `GammaLevel`. Every config-backed native setter passes `persist=1`; `persist_config_if_needed` immediately calls `WriteConfigFile`, and both game writers replace the complete current-game config from the still-old in-memory `GameCfg`. Thus applying an unchanged gamma first rewrites the newly edited `TexFilt`, `MenuTexFilt`, `HudTexFilt`, `MainViewFov`, `CornerTextInset`, `AnisoLevel`, `MsaaLevel`, `ClassicDepth`, and D2 `MovieTexFilt` values before their later `cfgInt` calls. Those calls prefer the game-specific file, read the restored old values, and their setters mirror those stale values into root and both game configs. The implementation plan requires all changed values to reload on resume and leaves the exact launcher-to-game resume test unfinished.
- Trigger: Leave a D1 or D2 game task running, change any config-backed graphics setting other than gamma in the launcher Graphics page, and resume the game while the ordinary `GammaLevel` key is present
- Impact: The requested setting neither reaches the running renderer nor remains on disk. The resume path silently restores the old value across root, D1, and D2 configuration, so the launcher can display a successful edit that disappears immediately and affects neither the current nor the next launch.
- Expected: A graphics generation is consumed as one coherent snapshot, every changed value reaches runtime, and applying runtime state cannot overwrite launcher-persisted values that have not yet been read.
- Suggested fix: Read and validate every config-backed value into one immutable Kotlin or native request before invoking any setter. Apply that request on the engine thread under BR-0029, with persistence disabled because the launcher already committed it, or persist the complete validated snapshot once after every runtime field has been updated. Advance the applied generation only after the batch succeeds.
- Validation: Keep a live D1 and D2 task, independently and jointly change every graphics key from the launcher, resume once, and assert introspected runtime state plus root, D1, and D2 file values exactly match the requested batch. Include gamma unchanged and changed, absent optional keys, D2-only movie filtering, repeated generations, activity recreation, and injected native-apply failure; assert a failed batch remains retryable.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. `readGraphicsConfigSnapshot` now reads each selected game and root config once and captures every valid config-backed option before any JNI call. `applyGraphicsSettingsPrefs` adds the preference-backed options to that detached snapshot, applies it through `nativeApplyLauncherGraphicsOption`, and advances the consumed generation only when every option succeeds. The dedicated JNI entry point calls `android_graphics_set_option` with persistence disabled, so applying launcher-authored runtime state cannot rewrite the already committed config; read or JNI failures leave the generation retryable. Focused tests cover a complete D2 snapshot, detached values after source rewrite, D1 exclusion of the D2-only key, malformed optional values, and injected mid-batch failure. All eight `GraphicsConfigHelpersTest` tests, scoped Kotlin and C quality checks, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed. BR-0200 subsequently fixed atomic config publication; BR-0029 remains open for engine-thread ownership and BR-0491 for explicit cross-process preference delivery.

### BR-0200: P2 - Publish complete mirrored graphics config updates

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-loss
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0103
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/android_graphics_options.c:L72-L178` in config patching and mirroring
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/android_graphics_options.c:L214-L220,L230-L353` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupConfigFiles.kt:L63-L79`
- Evidence: `patch_config_file` reconstructs each existing file in a fixed 32,768-byte stack buffer. `append_buf` silently drops any complete line or replacement that would make the retained length reach 32,768 bytes, but the helper then opens the public path with `"w"` and replaces the original with that incomplete buffer. It also treats a read failure as an empty file, publishes directly rather than through a sibling temporary file, ignores `fwrite`, `fflush`, `fsync`, and `fclose` results, and returns no status. `mirror_config_key` repeats this independently for root and each existing D1/D2 directory, so interruption or one target failure can leave three advertised app-wide defaults at different generations while the setter still reports success.
- Trigger: Persist a graphics option when any mirrored `descent.cfg` contains more than 32,767 retained bytes, when its read fails transiently but writing succeeds, when storage fills during replacement, or when the process is interrupted after truncation or between mirror targets
- Impact: Unrelated configuration lines can be silently deleted, an existing config can become empty or partial, and root, D1, and D2 can disagree even though the in-game or overlay edit appears successful. Later launches can lose music, display, player, or other settings and select different graphics defaults by game.
- Expected: Patching preserves the complete prior file except for the named key, validates every I/O step, publishes only a complete durable replacement, and exposes any failed or partial multi-file mirror to the caller without destroying the last valid files.
- Suggested fix: Replace the fixed buffer with a checked dynamic or streaming parser that has an explicit supported-size policy. Write each result to a uniquely claimed sibling temporary file, flush, sync, close, and atomically rename only after success; stage all intended targets before publication and retain or restore prior files if the mirror transaction cannot complete. Return a typed result through the setter and JNI or menu caller.
- Validation: Patch files at 32,766, 32,767, 32,768, and larger lengths, with long and unterminated lines, duplicate and absent keys, and unrelated unknown keys; inject read, allocation, short-write, flush, sync, close, rename, interruption, and second- or third-target failures. Assert exact preservation or an explicit failure with every prior file intact, then verify successful root/D1/D2 mirrors survive restart.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. Native graphics persistence now uses the standalone `graphics_config_transaction` component instead of a fixed buffer or `WriteConfigFile`. It reads every intended root, D1, and D2 target completely under an explicit 16 MiB per-file limit; constructs complete replacements without a line-length limit; stages uniquely claimed sibling files and prior-generation backups; checks write, flush, file sync, close, replacement, and parent-directory sync; and restores every already-published target when a later step fails. Graphics setters return `OK`, `UNKNOWN`, or `PERSIST_FAILED`, JNI returns that typed result, and failures are routed to the graphics debug log. Launcher and combined-import updates now use `AtomicFilePublication.writeUtf8Batch`, which stages every complete UTF-8 config and prior generation before publishing and restores or removes earlier targets after a later failure. Regressions cover 32,766, 32,767, 32,768, 65,537, and over-limit files; long and unterminated lines; duplicate, absent, and unknown keys; read, allocation, write, flush, sync, close, and every replace position; root/D1/D2 success; large launcher content; existing-target rollback; and newly created target removal. The focused native and 11-test graphics suites, all 21 native CTests, all 573 JVM tests with one skip, scoped code quality, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0204: P1 - Retain native windows across the EGL handoff

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime/concurrency
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0106
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/android_surface.c:L52-L85,L233-L279` in native-window publication and borrowing
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/android_egl_surface.c:L32-L84,L86-L199`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/MainActivity.kt:L1683-L1743`, and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelPreviewActivity.kt:L209-L238`
- Evidence: `android_set_surface` owns the sole reference in `g_native_window` and, under `g_surface_mutex`, releases the old window before replacing or clearing it. `android_surface_get_native_window` returns that raw pointer without taking the mutex or acquiring a reference. EGL initialization and recreation retain the borrowed value after the getter returns, then call `ANativeWindow_setBuffersGeometry` and `eglCreateWindowSurface`. MainActivity and LevelPreviewActivity execute `surfaceChanged` and `surfaceDestroyed` callbacks on the UI thread while rendering and swapping occur on a native game thread, so a callback can release the last reference between the getter and either EGL call. The associated `volatile` paused and stale flags are also read and cleared without the publication mutex or C atomics, so their accesses are data races and a replacement notification can be lost.
- Trigger: Rapidly resize, destroy, replace, rotate, background, or transition between game and preview surfaces while the render thread initializes or recreates EGL, especially when `surfaceChanged` republishes a new native-window wrapper
- Impact: The render thread can dereference a released `ANativeWindow`, causing a native use-after-free or driver crash. A lost stale notification can instead leave EGL bound to a destroyed surface and produce persistent swap failure, a black frame, or failed resume.
- Expected: Each EGL operation holds a valid native-window reference for its complete use, and surface generations plus pause and recreation state cross threads through a defined synchronized ownership protocol.
- Suggested fix: Snapshot and `ANativeWindow_acquire` the current window under the same mutex that publishes it, return the acquired reference with a generation, and release it only after EGL surface creation or failure is complete. Consume generation changes under that synchronization or through C atomics with an explicit ordering contract; preferably let the render thread own all EGL/window transitions while UI callbacks enqueue reference-counted changes.
- Validation: Add a lifecycle stress fixture that repeatedly interleaves `surfaceCreated`, `surfaceChanged`, `surfaceDestroyed`, preview transitions, rotation, and pause/resume with EGL initialization and swapping. Instrument acquired and released generations, run under native race and address sanitizers where supported, and assert no stale-pointer access, lost recreation, EGL error, black frame, or leaked window reference in both games.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. Surface publication, replacement, destruction, pause, and resume now share one mutex and a monotonic generation. EGL acquires an independent `ANativeWindow` reference with that generation under the publication mutex and retains it through buffer geometry, surface creation or failure, and buffer swap before releasing it. The render thread compares generations instead of consuming a lossy stale bit, recreates EGL against the retained snapshot, and records the active generation for diagnostics. The raw native-window getter and unsynchronized volatile lifecycle reads were removed. The launcher automation harness now handles distinct background markers, and the paired D1/D2 lifecycle test performs two consecutive background/resume cycles, waits for each actual EGL recreation, and asserts the post-resume generation. Both game runs passed without a crash, stale generation, or failed resume; scoped code quality, Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0205: P2 - Pack software-rendered pixels in RGBA byte order

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/rendering
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0106
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/android_surface.c:L136-L151,L168-L193` in palette conversion and native-window blitting
- Related: `android/app/build.gradle:L290-L315`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/CMakeLists.txt:L453-L461`, `d1/arch/sdl/gr.c:L30-L41`, and `d2/arch/sdl/gr.c:L30-L41`
- Evidence: The blitter requests `AHARDWAREBUFFER_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, whose native-window bytes are red, green, blue, then alpha, but constructs each palette word as `0xFFRRGGBB` and stores it through a `uint32_t *`. On Android's supported little-endian ABIs, that integer is written as blue, green, red, alpha. For example, opaque palette red becomes integer `0xFFFF0000`, bytes `00 00 FF FF`, and is interpreted as opaque blue. The paired SDL renderers reach this function when the supported Gradle `useOpenGL` option is false, so every non-gray color in software mode has red and blue exchanged.
- Trigger: Build either Android game with `-PuseOpenGL=false` and render any palette containing unequal red and blue components
- Impact: The complete game image has red and blue channels swapped in the supported software backend, corrupting level textures, sprites, UI art, and palette effects while geometry and grayscale elements can make the backend appear superficially functional.
- Expected: Palette colors produce the same visible RGBA result in software and OpenGL builds on every supported Android ABI.
- Suggested fix: Pack according to destination byte order rather than ARGB numeric notation: write explicit RGBA bytes, use a format-aware mapping helper, or on the supported little-endian contract store `0xAABBGGRR`. Validate the locked buffer's reported format before writing and fail clearly or convert when it differs.
- Validation: Run paired D1 and D2 software builds with pure red, pure blue, asymmetric mixed-color, alpha, and grayscale palette sentinels; inspect locked bytes and compare captured-frame hashes against an RGBA reference and the OpenGL backend on arm64 and x86_64. Retain a focused packing test that is independent of host integer endianness.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. The Android software blitter now builds its palette as explicit red, green, blue, alpha bytes and copies those bytes into each RGBA8888 destination pixel, eliminating host integer-endianness assumptions. After locking a native window it verifies the reported format is `AHARDWAREBUFFER_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM`; an unexpected format is logged and posted without writing incompatible pixels. A host-native regression verifies pure red, pure blue, asymmetric RGBA with nonopaque alpha, transparent alpha, grayscale, and surrounding guard bytes. The focused test and all 22 native CTests passed, scoped code quality passed, and the production blitter compiled in Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64. Direct x86_64 configuration with `ANDROID_USE_OPENGL=false` reproduced BR-0394's separate failure where the Android parent mutates omitted `d1_arch_ogl` and `d2_arch_ogl` targets, so paired software-mode emulator and OpenGL frame comparison remains blocked by that independently tracked P1. `git diff --check` passed.

### BR-0215: P2 - Bound synchronous metadata analysis work and memory

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: performance/resource-exhaustion
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0110, R1-CHUNK-0208, R1-CHUNK-0209, R1-CHUNK-0211, R1-CHUNK-0214, R1-CHUNK-0215, R1-CHUNK-0217
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_scan.c:L780-L865,L974-L1020` in per-candidate analysis and output generation
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_scan.c:L20-L43`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_scan.h:L8-L13,L89-L115`, `d1/main/gameseq.c:L690-L692`, and `d2/main/gameseq.c:L871-L873`
- Evidence: The scanner allocates one file-static `candidate_secret` for every supported segment even though it can publish at most 30 results. With 9,000 segments, the frozen structure layout makes that table 30,384,000 bytes; the persistent output state adds approximately 1.2 MiB before the other graph scratch arrays. More importantly, each raw candidate independently invokes hidden-reachability, progress-item, and label-position helpers that rescan all segments, and the item helper additionally enumerates objects. The code performs this work before rejecting a final count above 30, so a legal custom level with thousands of small candidate components drives quadratic segment work and touches much of the oversized table synchronously during level load and metadata scanning.
- Trigger: Load or inspect a near-9,000-segment custom mission arranged as thousands of distinct item-bearing hidden candidate components connected to the progressive region
- Impact: Level load, JNI metadata extraction, or headless inspection can consume tens of MiB of native memory and tens of millions of callback iterations on the calling thread, causing long stalls, Android watchdog failures, or process termination under memory pressure.
- Expected: Scanner memory and execution time are linear in level topology with a modest constant, and exceeding the 30-result contract terminates with an explicit deterministic outcome without constructing or repeatedly rescanning thousands of full candidate records.
- Suggested fix: Aggregate reachability, item, label, and boundary facts per component in linear passes; store sparse candidate summaries rather than per-segment maximum-capacity item and entrance arrays; and stop after detecting `SECRET_AREA_MAX_GENERATED + 1` valid results with a clear disabled reason. Apply an explicit work and memory budget to metadata entry points.
- Validation: Generate 900- and 9,000-segment worst-case graphs with increasing component counts and measure wall time, callback counts, and peak RSS in load, JNI, and headless callers. Require near-linear scaling, bounded candidate storage, exact normal results up to 30, and a prompt deterministic overflow result above 30.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0208): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/route_planner.cpp:L390-L502,L504-L629,L1080-L1166` in visibility caching, detailed sampling, and source selection; `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_game_adapter.c:L432-L444,L1511-L1548`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0208): The final optimization stores one center result and one detailed result for every distinct source-wall and reachable-segment pair, and a miss tests one center plus 32 face, vertex, and edge positions. The supported limits allow 254 opener walls to target one side and 9,000 reachable segments. If none of those switches is visible, pruning never establishes a finite best distance, so one selection pass can retain 4,572,000 unordered-map entries and invoke 75,438,000 engine visibility queries. The cache, progress callback, and loop have no entry, byte, callback-work, deadline, or cancellation ceiling. The checked-in Uneasy4 trace still performs 863,768 wall-shot requests after the optimization and averages 3.83 seconds on the host, establishing that substantial synchronous work is reachable well below the declared maxima.
- Additional trigger (R1-CHUNK-0208): Load or inspect a supported large custom level with many reachable segments and many shootable trigger-source walls opening the same required side, arranged so no sampled position can shoot those walls.
- Additional impact (R1-CHUNK-0208): Route planning can allocate hundreds of megabytes of native hash nodes and perform tens of millions of collision and intersection queries on the calling thread. A game level load, launcher metadata worker, or headless scan can stall far beyond the measured large-level case, exhaust the Android process heap, or be killed before the C facade can convert allocation failure into a route error.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0208): Add synthetic route snapshots that scale opener sources and reachable segments independently through the supported limits, with visibility always false and with early and late successes. Count cache entries, callback invocations, elapsed time, and peak RSS in D1 and D2 game, JNI, and headless paths. Require explicit bounded failure or near-linear capped work, prompt cancellation, bounded memory, stable ordinary route output, and no regression in the existing Uneasy4 result.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0211): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/route_planner.cpp:L2871-L2955` in the C facade's transparent-shot preference replan
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0211): After a successful plan uses a transparent shot following a key, the facade can invoke a second complete synchronous `plan_route` with the strict callback and an incremented cache namespace. The namespace correctly prevents semantic cache aliasing, but it also prevents the expensive source-by-segment visibility entries from being reused, so one public end-level request can perform both full planner workloads before returning and has no cancellation, deadline, callback, or aggregate cache budget.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0211): Exercise the maximum-work snapshots through `route_planner_plan_view` with a transparent first result and a preceding key so the strict pass runs. Measure combined ordinary and strict callback counts, cache entries, peak RSS, cancellation latency, and wall time, and require one aggregate request budget rather than independently unbounded passes.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0214): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_game_adapter.c:L109-L126,L226-L328,L330-L385` in the process-global engine visibility cache
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0214): The callback cache starts with 4,096 open-addressed entries, doubles whenever occupancy exceeds 70 percent, and has no maximum entry count, byte budget, request ownership, deadline, or cancellation check. World changes zero the entries but deliberately preserve the largest allocated capacity for the lifetime of the process. The planner's already-unbounded source-by-position visibility requests therefore also drive a second native allocation whose peak cannot be reclaimed after leaving the pathological level; failed growth merely bypasses additional stores and does not bound the synchronous uncached collision work.
- Additional impact (R1-CHUNK-0214): One adversarial metadata request can permanently raise the game's native resident-memory floor until process exit, so later ordinary levels remain under memory pressure even after the route and snapshot that caused the growth are replaced.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0214): Include the engine callback cache in the maximum-work route fixtures, assert explicit entry and byte ceilings, measure peak and post-level-change RSS, and require both prompt cancellation and release or bounded reuse of excess capacity after the request ends.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0215): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_game_adapter.c:L1013-L1131,L1134-L1192,L1243-L1398` in connected-visibility confirmation and engine collision callbacks
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0215): A visibility cache miss first performs the full source-to-target FVI query. When FVI's advisory segment list is not credible, the fallback divides the ray into 64-world-unit spans and synchronously invokes another `find_vector_intersection` for every span. The maximum fixed-point distance permits hundreds of additional collision traversals for one requested pose, and only the final Boolean result is cached. This loop has no per-ray or aggregate callback budget, deadline, progress callback, or cancellation check, so the planner's millions of distinct source-position misses can each multiply into many engine traversals before returning.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0215): Extend the visibility stress matrix across short and maximum-length rays whose primary FVI segment list is deliberately noncredible. Count both public visibility callbacks and nested engine intersections, enforce one aggregate request budget and cancellation path across primary and confirmation work, and require bounded latency without accepting disconnected rays.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0217): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/secret_area_scan.c:L7-L41,L230-L274` in static candidate storage and marginal-opener classification
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0217): The assigned declaration confirms one full item-and-entrance-bearing candidate record is reserved for every supported segment before any scan. Independently, `side_has_only_marginal_reachable_trigger_openers` iterates every opener and calls `triggered_side_opener_is_marginal`; for each reachable pass-through opener that helper calls `progression_pass_through_opener_count`, which rescans every opener for the same target side. A supported target with `k` all-marginal opener records therefore performs `k²` callback and edge checks merely to classify one entrance, before the scanner's existing per-candidate whole-level passes. The paired adapter permits up to the fixed trigger-link population, so this work has no local deadline, cancellation, or aggregate budget.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0217): Add scanner fixtures with one triggered candidate entrance and 1, 10, 100, and maximum supported all-marginal openers. - Resolution: Fixed on 2026-07-31. Secret-area scanning now classifies component reachability, progression items, ordinary items, labels, and entrances in linear whole-level passes; materializes only 31 full candidates; and returns the deterministic too-many-candidates result as soon as the 31st valid component is identified. Marginal opener classification precomputes the pass-through count once per side. Result segments use one shared 9,000-entry pool instead of one maximum-sized array per secret, and a regression enforces a result-state size below 512 KiB. Route planning now shares a two-million-callback work budget and 65,536-entry sample-cache cap across the normal and strict transparent-shot passes. Engine collision work, including segmented confirmation FVI calls, has a one-million-call aggregate cap, and the retained engine cache cannot grow beyond 65,536 entries. Cancellation propagates through the metadata view into planner callbacks and nested collision work; level-preview close requests use that path and stop preparation explicitly. Synthetic tests cover 30, 450, and 4,500 candidates, the 9,000-segment ceiling, deterministic overflow, linear callback counts, 10, 100, and 254 marginal openers, state size, planner budget exhaustion, and immediate cancellation. Exact D1 and D2 base-game metadata matched the checked-in 181-secret and 234-secret fixture. Scoped code quality, `git diff --check`, paired D1/D2 Windows builds, all native maths CTests, the focused scanner test, and Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. The broader extraction suite passed 21 of 23 tests; unrelated graphics transaction and Chromaprint configuration tests crashed in their existing code paths, while the BR-0215 test passed.
