### BR-0046: P2 - Reject incomplete CD reads instead of publishing partial hashes

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/data-integrity
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0019, R1-CHUNK-0020, R1-CHUNK-0027, R1-CHUNK-0035, R1-CHUNK-0395
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_cd.c:L231-L248,L498-L515`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_cd.c:L228-L245,L361-L405`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_disc_import.c:L74-L127,L145-L208`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/jni_fingerprint.c:L118-L166`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_cd.c:L455-L472`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/fingerprint_cd.c:L320-L338,L421-L434`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/pcm_decoders.c:L258-L275`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/cue_parser.c:L135-L164`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DiscImportBridge.kt:L51-L67`, `game_data/extract_all_cds.ps1:L119-L153`, `game_data/fingerprint_disc_tracks.ps1:L176-L202`, and `game_data/hash_disc_tracks.ps1:L76-L95`
- Evidence: The duplicated CLI CUE readers ignore both seeks, accept a negative `ftell`, and ignore `fread`. If length discovery fails, `len` can be `-1`, making the allocation size zero, the requested read size enormous after unsigned conversion, and `buf[len]` a write before the allocation. A short read of a regular file instead leaves uninitialized bytes for the parser to consume. The JNI bridge bounds positive CUE lengths, but `nativeParseCue` merely logs a short read and parses the terminated prefix, while `nativeGetCueTitles` does not compare the read count at all. Kotlin invokes those exports separately, so one logical parse can combine track fields and titles obtained from different file snapshots. After parsing, both CLIs ignore the per-track seek result; when any hashing-sector read is short or fails, they only print a warning, finalize SHA-1 over the prefix, emit that digest as an ordinary track record, and leave `errors` unchanged. `fingerprint_cd` then seeks again without checking, tracks the bulk audio `bytes_read` but never compares it with the allocation size, and passes the declared full sector count to a decoder that copies every byte, including the uninitialized tail, before Chromaprint consumes it. The Android fingerprint JNI path explicitly treats a short BIN read as usable, reduces `numSectors` to the complete prefix, and returns an ordinary fingerprint and shortened duration; callers can therefore match and persist an identity for a source that changed or was truncated after CUE sizing. An audio track with zero computed sectors falls through the CLI data-track branch and is emitted as a successful `type: data` SHA-1 of empty input. The scripts save these records, and the database generator promotes any track with a number, type, and SHA-1 without knowing whether its declared sector range was fully read
- Trigger: Pass a non-seekable CUE stream, encounter a CUE short read, truncate or replace a CUE between length discovery, read, or the JNI bridge's two parses, truncate or replace a BIN after its size pass, inject a sector read error, or make the computed track seek fail
- Impact: The host tool can corrupt memory while reading the CUE, derive a fingerprint from uninitialized heap contents, misclassify an empty audio track as data, or publish a stable-looking SHA-1 for an empty or partial track and exit successfully; Android can accept a truncated CUE prefix, compose fields and titles from inconsistent snapshots, or identify a partial BIN prefix as though it were a complete track; and generated values can enter checked-in fingerprint and track manifests, runtime caches, and the canonical known-disc database, causing false, nondeterministic, or irreproducible disc identities
- Expected: CUE loading succeeds only after an exact bounded regular-file read, and a track hash is emitted only after a validated seek and every declared sector has been read exactly once
- Suggested fix: Replace the duplicated seek-and-`ftell` readers with one checked bounded file loader that rejects non-seekable, oversized, changed, or short inputs; have JNI return track fields and titles from one parse result; validate every track has a positive in-file byte range; check `lseek`; and convert any short sector read into a nonzero command result without emitting a success SHA-1. Share the exact loader and hashing helper across Android, extraction, and fingerprint paths
- Validation: Add seek-failure, negative-length, CUE short-read, CUE mutation between JNI passes, BIN truncation-between-stat-and-read, mid-hash and mid-audio read errors, zero-sector audio, and complete valid-track cases for both CLIs and the JNI bridge; poison unread allocation tails under MemorySanitizer or equivalent, assert failures emit no promotable digest or fingerprint or partial Android parse, exit nonzero without memory errors, preserve the declared track type in diagnostics, and leave canonical manifests unchanged
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0395): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/DiscIdentifier.kt:L158-L176` in `sha1Hash`, with direct consumers at `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDiscImport.kt:L413-L435` and `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupDialogs.kt:L1370-L1396`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0395): The Kotlin track hasher also stops on any nonpositive read or a true cancellation callback, finalizes the bytes consumed so far, and returns the result as an ordinary SHA-1 string with no complete, canceled, or failed status. Both path and SAF import callers pass the CUE-declared byte count and immediately submit that digest for physical-disc identity. A source whose CUE span exceeds the readable file but whose available prefix equals a maintained track therefore receives the maintained digest despite never satisfying the declared span. Frozen BR-0410 currently empties the identity database before matching, but repairing that schema alone would expose this already-published incomplete-read contract.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0395): Exercise the Kotlin hasher and both registration paths with exact reads, EOF at zero, one sector short, a maintained-track prefix followed by a CUE-declared missing suffix, provider exceptions, zero-byte reads, and cancellation before and after the final byte. Require a typed complete result before identification, distinct canceled and failed outcomes, no persisted known identity for any incomplete span, and ordinary identity after BR-0410's mixed-schema database is repaired.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-03. Added one shared exact bounded CUE loader and positive in-file track-span validator, then applied them to both host CLIs and the Android JNI paths. Extraction and fingerprint utilities now validate file ownership, range, and seek results, require every declared sector, preserve the declared type in error records, return failure, and emit no SHA-1 when a span is invalid or a read is incomplete. JNI CUE parsing now returns fields and titles from one exact snapshot, and disc fingerprinting rejects spans outside the descriptor before streaming the exact range. Kotlin hashing now returns typed complete, canceled, or failed outcomes, performs exact offset traversal and exact-length reads, and both path and SAF registration submit only complete hashes for identification. Focused native contract and CLI tests cover valid ranges, invalid and zero ranges, missing and oversized inputs, incomplete audio images, nonzero exits, declared-type diagnostics, and absence of SHA-1 output; Kotlin tests cover exact offsets, short and zero-byte reads, incomplete skips, cancellation before completion, and completion on the final byte. Both focused native CTests, the complete Android unit suite, scoped source quality, CMake script formatting and lint, `git diff --check`, and Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0047: P2 - Report successful HFS extraction as success

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: api-data-format
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0019
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_cd.c:L558-L586`
- Related: `game_data/extract_all_cds.ps1:L129-L153`, `game_data/hash_disc_tracks.ps1:L76-L95`, `game_data/CD images/Descent - Mac macplay/track_hashes.json:L2`, `game_data/CD images/d1 mac 2nd bin+cue/track_hashes.json:L2`, `game_data/CD images/d2 mac/track_hashes.json:L2`, and `android/ai tool plans/CD, installer parsing/on_device_mac_extract.md:L53,L94,L160,L470`
- Evidence: After detecting HFS and extracting a positive file count, the tool increments both success counters and exits successfully but deliberately emits `"error": "ISO listing failed"` to avoid changing old fixtures. All three checked-in Mac track manifests therefore describe their successfully extracted data track as an error. Repository planning claims both that HFS is reported instead of a false ISO error and, elsewhere, that the false error is correct. Current promotion scripts silently discard the error while accepting the SHA-1, so the field is neither a truthful status nor an enforced compatibility contract
- Trigger: Successfully process any supported HFS BIN/CUE image through `extract_cd` and save its JSON lines with `extract_all_cds.ps1`
- Impact: Machine-readable output contradicts the actual result and exit status, checked-in provenance permanently claims a failure, and any caller that correctly treats an `error` field as failure will reject supported Mac extraction while existing callers hide genuine status mistakes by ignoring it
- Expected: A successful HFS record contains no error and explicitly identifies the filesystem and extracted count; an error record is emitted only when HFS detection or extraction actually fails
- Suggested fix: Restore a truthful success schema such as `filesystem: "hfs"` plus `files_extracted` and optional partition metadata, update the three generated manifests and contradictory planning text in the publication series, and make the batch script validate that successful records lack `error` while failed records cause a nonzero run
- Validation: Run the CLI against the three known Mac discs, parse stdout strictly, assert the HFS data record has the exact SHA-1, filesystem, and positive extracted count with no error, verify failed ISO and HFS fixtures still emit errors and exit nonzero, and regenerate consumers without changing disc identity
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-04. Successful HFS data tracks now emit `filesystem: "hfs"` and the positive `files_extracted` count with no error, while detected HFS extraction failures retain an explicit HFS filesystem tag, emit `error: "HFS extraction failed"`, and produce a nonzero command result. The CD batch now parses every captured JSON record before publication, rejects malformed or error-bearing output even when the child exits zero, and requires a positive extracted-file count for every HFS success record. Its focused batch regression covers the valid HFS schema and a zero-exit error record. The three maintained Mac manifests were regenerated from the real images: both D1 images extracted 8 files and D2 extracted 20, with all existing track SHA-1 identities unchanged. The contradictory Mac extraction planning note was corrected. Direct success runs of all three images and an unwritable-output HFS failure behaved as required; the HFS, STi2, exact CD-read, incomplete-read CLI, and batch tests passed; scoped code quality, strict manifest parsing, `git diff --check`, and Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0057: P2 - Recognize GOG Galaxy metadata only after full pattern validation

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: compatibility/parser-correctness
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0023, R1-CHUNK-0024
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/inno_reader.c:L900-L925` in `parse_header_stream1`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/inno_reader.c:L1943-L2054,L2104-L2116`, `android/ai tool plans/CD, installer parsing/INNOSETUP_FORMAT_SPEC.md:L603-L619`, and `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/test_gog_fd.c:L1-L103`
- Evidence: Any nonempty standard Inno `BeforeInstall` script sets `gog_galaxy = 1` before the parser proves it is the expected three-argument Galaxy convention. The subsequent quote search neither checks the function name nor requires all arguments, hash, part count, or complete syntax; if it finds a second quoted value, it also replaces the destination. Extraction routes every marked entry through an additional zlib layer, so an unrelated ordinary callback is decoded as Galaxy data
- Trigger: Open a supported installer with an extension-matching file that has a nonempty ordinary `BeforeInstall` callback, or a malformed or different quoted script in that field
- Impact: A valid ordinary file fails extraction or is written under a script-argument name; malformed metadata can take the wrong decoder path while callers receive no reason that the Galaxy heuristic was responsible
- Expected: Ordinary Inno scripts remain opaque, and Galaxy mode and destination replacement are enabled only after one exact documented convention is completely and unambiguously validated
- Suggested fix: Parse the full expected function call with bounded arguments, escaping rules, hash form, destination, and numeric part count into a temporary result; set `gog_galaxy` and replace the destination only after the complete parse succeeds. Otherwise preserve the original destination and ordinary extraction path
- Validation: Add empty and ordinary callbacks, quoted unrelated functions, missing and extra arguments, escaped quotes, overlong values, and genuine single- and multipart Galaxy metadata; assert only exact valid Galaxy records take inner zlib and every other file retains its original path and bytes
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-04. Replaced the quote-search heuristic with a bounded transactional parser for the complete maintained Galaxy callback. Recognition now requires the case-insensitive `before_install` function, exactly three arguments, a single-quoted 32-digit hexadecimal hash, a single-quoted valid relative destination with doubled-quote decoding, an unquoted decimal part count from 1 through the extraction entry limit, a closing parenthesis, and no trailing statement. Parsing occurs into temporary storage, so the destination and `gog_galaxy` flag are committed together only after every check succeeds. Oversized `BeforeInstall` fields are skipped as opaque script metadata rather than rejecting the installer or entering Galaxy decompression. Focused regressions cover empty and ordinary callbacks, a similarly named dependency function, missing and extra arguments, malformed and overlong hashes, invalid and escaped paths, quoted, negative, zero, oversized, single, and multipart counts, case and spacing, trailing syntax, atomic file-entry publication, and preservation of the original ordinary destination. The maintained D1 and D2 installer fixtures retained their expected game-file catalogs, all seven expected D1 Galaxy game entries, and successful regular and Galaxy checksum extraction behavior. The format specification was corrected to document the observed unquoted part-count convention. Warning-clean host builds of `test_gog_fd` and `extract_gog`, the focused real-installer CTest, scoped code quality, `git diff --check`, and Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0066: P1 - Reject Windows separators in PKG output names

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/path-traversal
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0029, R1-CHUNK-0030
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L325-L345,L466-L488` in `is_game_file` and `pkg_extract_all`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.c:L16-L33`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/pkg_reader.h:L22-L29,L58-L66`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/extract_gog.c:L158-L188`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/extract/jni_gog_import.c:L307-L355`, and `game_data/extract_all_gog.ps1:L121-L170`
- Evidence: After requiring the expected CPIO prefix, `is_game_file` rejects only `/` in the remaining name. It accepts Windows `\`, dot components, and any extension from the game list. Extraction concatenates that untrusted remainder after `output_dir` and opens it with `fopen("wb")`. On Windows both slash forms are separators, so an entry named `./payload/Contents/Resources/game/..\escape.hog` becomes `<output_dir>/..\escape.hog` and resolves in the parent directory. The host extractor and batch workflow are built and run on Windows
- Trigger: Process a crafted PKG whose odc CPIO payload contains an extension-matching entry such as `./payload/Contents/Resources/game/..\escape.hog`
- Impact: Extracting a selected installer can create or truncate a game-extension file outside the requested output directory, including a sibling data set or another repository file used by the host generation workflow
- Expected: Every accepted CPIO destination is exactly one safe platform-neutral basename, and the normalized final path is proven to remain directly beneath the selected output directory
- Suggested fix: Reject empty, `.`, `..`, slash, backslash, drive-like, absolute, control-byte, and truncated names before extension matching; construct output through the shared checked archive-path helper; and verify canonical containment before opening. Keep basename flattening explicit if nested package paths are intentionally unsupported
- Validation: Add synthetic odc-in-gzip-in-XAR fixtures with slash, backslash, dot, dot-dot, drive-prefix, absolute, control-byte, and ordinary basenames; extract on Windows and POSIX; assert every unsafe entry fails without creating or changing any path outside a temporary root; and retain exact output for both real GOG packages
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-04. PKG cataloging now classifies output candidates before extension matching and hard-fails Windows separators, empty and dot names, drive-like colons, control bytes, Windows-invalid characters, trailing dots or spaces, and overlong or embedded-NUL CPIO names. Legitimate nested POSIX package content remains explicitly ignored rather than flattened, preserving the maintained D2 package layout. Extraction revalidates the basename and uses a checked join that rejects empty output directories and truncation before opening a file, so every published name is one direct child of the selected directory. Synthetic odc-in-gzip-in-XAR regressions cover ordinary and unrelated names, slash nesting, backslash traversal, dot and dot-dot names, drive and absolute Windows forms, control and invalid characters, overlong fields, and embedded-NUL truncation. The focused PKG CTest, scoped code quality, `git diff --check`, complete extraction of the maintained D1 seven-file and D2 fifteen-file catalogs, and Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed.

### BR-0484: P1 - Cancel the isolated level-metadata worker with its dialog request

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime/cancellation
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0424
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L776-L884` in `LevelMetadataAnalyzer.analyze`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L2480-L2523`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L1202-L1207,L1251-L1311`, BR-0076, BR-0215, and BR-0468
- Evidence: `analyze` creates an owned request directory, starts a per-game isolated service, then polls its files from a cancellable coroutine, but it has no `try/finally` around either resource. Worker termination occurs only after the polling loop reaches an explicit crash or deadline branch, and directory deletion occurs only on handled setup failures, ordinary result return, or that same terminal branch. The dialog owns the call in `LaunchedEffect(target)`, so dismissal, target replacement, navigation, or composition teardown can cancel it at the post-start progress callback or polling `delay`; cancellation bypasses both cleanup operations. The service has already detached work onto a raw `Thread`, does not receive the coroutine cancellation, and holds its process-local single-flight flag until native analysis returns. No other production caller invokes `killWorkerProcess`, and focused tests parse checkpoints and exercise deadline and worker helpers without canceling a live request. Consequently cancellation also removes the only observer that would enforce the timeout against the unbounded work in BR-0215 or the nonterminating arithmetic in BR-0468.
- Trigger: Open metadata for a slow or hostile mission, then dismiss the dialog, navigate away, replace the selected target, or recreate the Activity after the isolated worker starts and before it publishes a result
- Impact: The invisible isolated process can continue consuming CPU and memory indefinitely after the user cancels, its single-flight guard rejects a later metadata request as busy, and its request directory remains until age-based pruning. For a mission that triggers BR-0468, ordinary dialog dismissal permanently disables the watchdog that otherwise kills the hung process, so recovery requires process or app termination.
- Expected: Cancellation of one analysis promptly terminates only its owned worker generation, releases native single-flight state, and deletes its owned request directory; timeout containment remains active independently of whether the presenting Compose dialog survives.
- Suggested fix: Wrap post-directory and post-worker ownership in a noncancellable `finally` that deletes the exact request directory and terminates or cooperatively cancels the exact request generation. Carry a request token through the service and process-control path so cleanup cannot kill a newer request, and prefer a lifecycle-independent bounded worker protocol whose own deadline terminates native work even if the UI observer disappears.
- Validation: Cancel before request construction, during each progress callback, immediately before and after service start, during every poll state, while reading a result, and during slow and hung native work; repeat through Close, Back, outside tap, target replacement, navigation, and Activity recreation for D1 and D2. Assert the exact worker exits promptly, single-flight is released, owned files are removed, an immediate retry succeeds, a newer generation is never killed by stale cleanup, and BR-0215/BR-0468 fixtures remain bounded after UI cancellation.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0425): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L1197-L1222` in worker-process observation and termination
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0425): The watcher identifies ownership only by the shared per-game process suffix. If request A's process crashes without a result and request B starts the same-game service before A observes an absent process, A treats B's process as proof that its own work is still alive. When A's older deadline expires, `killWorkerProcess` kills B's process without checking either request ID. The UI and independently launched automation executors can overlap these calls, and no focused test replaces a crashed process while an older watcher is polling.
- Additional trigger (R1-CHUNK-0425): Let a D1 or D2 request crash late in its deadline window, then immediately start another request for the same game before the older poll observes the process gap
- Additional impact (R1-CHUNK-0425): A healthy replacement analysis can be terminated by stale cleanup for a different request, so its caller reports a crash or timeout and retry reliability depends on process-start and polling timing.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0425): Use distinct request generations and barriers around process death, restart, process lookup, result publication, and deadline expiry; require an old watcher to terminate only its correlated worker and never a newer same-game process.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-05. `LevelMetadataAnalyzer.analyze` now owns every created request directory through a noncancellable `finally`, propagates coroutine cancellation instead of converting it to a result, publishes a request-local cancellation marker, terminates only the correlated worker, waits briefly for that generation to exit, and deletes the exact owned directory on every return or cancellation checkpoint. The service publishes a locked per-game owner record plus a request-local identity containing the request ID, PID, and Linux process-start generation before entering native analysis; observation and termination require the request identity and current owner to agree, so stale cleanup cannot target a newer same-game request or a reused PID. Owner replacement and conditional clearing are serialized both within and across processes. The isolated process also runs its own checkpoint-aware deadline and cancellation watchdog, preserving timeout containment if the presenting UI observer disappears. Focused tests cover identity serialization, malformed identities, request, PID, and process-generation mismatches, `/proc` parsing with complex command names, and replacement-owner protection. The complete Android unit suite, focused post-audit worker and deadline tests, scoped Kotlin quality, `git diff --check`, Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and the maintained three-request D2 metadata mount-scope emulator regression passed. The emulator ended with no request directories and no level-metadata worker process, and an empty owner record.

### BR-0485: P2 - Keep the metadata service alive until its accepted request finishes

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: resource-lifetime/android-service
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0425
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/LevelMetadata.kt:L1266-L1295` in `LevelMetadataAnalysisService.onStartCommand`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupActivity.kt:L201-L230`, `android/app/src/main/java/com/dxxredux/app/SetupSections.kt:L2480-L2523`, BR-0284, BR-0484, Android `Service.stopSelfResult` at `https://developer.android.com/reference/android/app/Service#stopSelfResult(int)`, and the process-lifecycle contract at `https://developer.android.com/guide/components/activities/process-lifecycle`
- Evidence: Every service start creates an independent raw `Thread`. After request A enters the single-flight guard, a concurrent request B fails the guard, writes a busy result, and calls `stopSelf(startId)` with B's newer start ID even though A is still running. Android documents that stopping with the most recently received ID stops the service immediately and specifically warns that separately threaded start IDs must be completed in receive order. Once B stops this unbound, non-foreground service, Android no longer treats the component as started; A's raw thread does not preserve service or process importance, and the platform may destroy or kill the per-game process while its native analysis still owns the single-flight flag and request. `START_NOT_STICKY` provides no restart or redelivery. The focused test checks only `AtomicBoolean` admission and never drives overlapping Service start IDs or destruction.
- Trigger: Start two same-game analyses after the first thread has entered single-flight, for example by opening metadata while a debug automation analysis runs, sending two automation broadcasts, or dismissing and immediately retrying while BR-0484 leaves the first worker active
- Impact: The second caller receives a busy failure, while the accepted first analysis loses its started-service lifetime and can be killed or deprived of execution under process pressure. Its caller then reports a crash or timeout, so one overlapping request can make both operations fail and waste the completed native work.
- Expected: Rejecting or queuing a later request cannot stop the Service that owns active work; the Service remains started until the accepted generation has published its result and released single-flight, then stops exactly once after all delivered start IDs are accounted for.
- Suggested fix: Serialize start commands through one service-owned executor or explicit queue, retain the latest delivered start ID, and call `stopSelfResult` only after the active request and every rejected or queued command have completed in receive order. Do not detach lifecycle-critical work onto independently stopping threads; if only one request is supported, correlate the busy response without stopping the active owner.
- Validation: Instrument the Service with barriers before admission, during native work, while writing busy and successful results, and before every stop. Deliver A, then B and C after A enters; vary thread scheduling and destroy or pressure the process. Require B and C to receive deterministic busy or queued results, A to retain Service ownership and publish successfully, `onDestroy` to occur only after A exits and single-flight clears, no restart loss under `START_NOT_STICKY`, and identical D1 and D2 behavior.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-05. Replaced the per-start raw threads and per-command `stopSelf(startId)` calls with one service-owned FIFO executor. Every delivered start ID is registered before submission, commands complete in receive order, and the service calls `stopSelfResult` exactly once with the newest delivered ID only after the queue drains; a new start racing that call therefore keeps the service started through Android's start-ID contract. Later requests publish request-specific queued process identities before waiting, allowing their observers to distinguish accepted queued work from a crash without replacing the active native owner. Cancellation deletes only that queued request, and its eventual command exits without disturbing the accepted generation. The focused barrier regression holds A active while B and C arrive, verifies neither runs early, verifies FIFO execution, and observes one drain callback carrying C's start ID; lifetime tests also cover a newer start following a previously drained generation. The complete Android unit suite, focused single-flight and lifecycle tests, scoped Kotlin quality, `git diff --check`, Android debug assembly for D1 and D2 on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64, and the maintained three-request D2 metadata mount-scope emulator regression passed. The emulator ended with an empty owner record, no request directories, and no level-metadata worker process.

### BR-0468: P1 - Bound level-metadata distance arithmetic

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: security/resource-exhaustion
- Found by: Luna versus Sol blinded trial, R1-CHUNK-0189
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/level_metadata_scan.c:L203-L228,L370-L388` in `distance_sq`, `sqrt_ll`, and `collect_energy_center_stats`
- Related: `android/app/src/main/cpp/shared/level_metadata_scan.h:L211-L223`, `android/app/src/main/cpp/headless/headless_metadata_dump_main.cpp:L835-L850`, and `android/tests/test_level_metadata_scan.c`
- Evidence: Disconnected energy-center coordinates are exposed as signed 32-bit fixed values. `distance_sq` squares their differences into a signed `long long`, and `sqrt_ll` searches the complete nonnegative `int` range. For the still-representable value `INT_MAX * INT_MAX`, the search reaches `mid == INT_MAX`, accepts its square, and evaluates `low = mid + 1`, which overflows signed `int`; larger multi-axis differences can overflow the signed sum before the search. The result feeds both imported-mission metadata and headless regeneration. A separate frozen-code verification reproduced the exact boundary arithmetic and found no mission-coordinate admission limit that excludes it.
- Trigger: Analyze a user-selected mission with at least two disconnected energy-center components whose nearest centers differ by `INT_MAX` fixed units on one axis, or use larger extreme signed coordinates that overflow the accumulated squared distance
- Impact: Signed undefined behavior can make mission metadata scanning loop indefinitely, return corrupt distances, or fail under sanitizers. A hostile or malformed mission can stall its analysis worker and block import or regression generation rather than receiving a bounded validation error.
- Expected: Every admitted coordinate combination either produces a deterministic bounded distance or fails analysis before arithmetic overflow; the integer square root terminates for its complete declared input domain.
- Suggested fix: Compute differences and sums with checked unsigned or wider arithmetic, define and enforce the maximum representable metadata distance, and implement an overflow-safe square root whose upper bound derives from the admitted value and never increments past its type maximum.
- Validation: Add native scan-view fixtures with zero, ordinary corpus maxima, `INT_MAX - 1`, `INT_MAX`, opposite signed coordinate extremes, and one-, two-, and three-axis overflow cases. Run under ASan and UBSan with a short timeout, require deterministic success below the documented bound and prompt structured rejection above it, and retain paired D1/D2 corpus regeneration.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0490): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/helpers/regenerate_all_mission_metadata_host.ps1:L509-L543` in archive and built-in headless invocation
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0490): Both frozen host paths invoke the metadata executable synchronously and wait without a deadline. The admitted arithmetic loop can therefore retain the complete regeneration process before its catch, summary, or later archives run; the source shown in the older related-path entry is `android/helpers`, not `android/tests`. Live later added a 120-second process wrapper at these exact call sites, but the frozen native arithmetic remains the primary correctness root.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0490): Exercise the arithmetic fixtures through the host runner as well as the native suite and require bounded termination, per-item failure accounting, later-archive continuation, and no retained child process.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-05. Level-metadata energy-center distances now use checked unsigned accumulation capped to the complete representable `int` distance domain. Values above `INT_MAX` use a distinct squared-distance sentinel for grouping, so they report the documented saturated value without becoming falsely eligible for an `INT_MAX` grouping threshold. The integer square root operates entirely in unsigned arithmetic and handles its upper boundary without incrementing a signed maximum. Focused scan-view fixtures cover zero, an ordinary 3-4-5 distance, `INT_MAX - 1`, exact `INT_MAX`, opposite signed extremes, and two- and three-axis overflow, including grouping behavior across the reporting boundary. Both complete Windows D1 and D2 builds and their focused test executables passed under a 10-second test timeout; the focused D1 executable also passed with MSVC AddressSanitizer. Scoped C/C++ quality, `git diff --check`, and Android debug assembly for arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, and x86_64 passed. This environment has no UBSan-capable Clang installation, and full D1/D2 corpus regeneration was not rerun.

### BR-0568: P1 - Use macOS-compatible temporary-file templates

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: compatibility/build-release
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0486
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/get_deps/helpers/get_sdk.sh:L16-L24,L47-L59`; `android/get_deps/helpers/get_shellcheck.sh:L40-L48`
- Related: `android/get_deps/helpers/get_ndk.sh:L18-L30`; `android/get_deps/get_all.sh:L104-L135`; `android/get_deps/helpers/platform.sh:L3-L8,L19-L43`; macOS `mktemp(1)` at `https://keith.github.io/xcode-man-pages/mktemp.1.html`
- Evidence: The platform helper explicitly recognizes Darwin and derives macOS SDK, NDK, JDK, and formatter assets, and the aggregate bootstrap invokes them on every non-Linux host. However, SDK uses `mktemp ... sdk-XXXXXX.zip`, NDK uses `ndk-XXXXXX.zip`, and the Darwin ShellCheck branch uses `shellcheck-XXXXXX.tar.xz`. macOS `mktemp(1)` requires the replaceable `X` run to be appended to the template; suffix characters after that run are not accepted. Thus the essential SDK step fails while creating its temporary archive, before any download, on a stock macOS host; NDK and ShellCheck retain the same failure if reached. GNU mktemp accepts these templates, which is why Linux validation does not expose the Darwin break.
- Trigger: Run the one-shot dependency bootstrap or the SDK, NDK, or ShellCheck helper on macOS with the platform's standard `mktemp`.
- Impact: A required Android dependency bootstrap fails before installing the SDK command-line tools, preventing macOS Android configuration, builds, emulator setup, code quality, and tests despite the branch explicitly selecting macOS assets.
- Expected: Temporary-file creation uses a template accepted by every declared host, with the unique `X` suffix at the end or an explicitly portable helper; archive tools must not depend on the temporary filename extension.
- Suggested fix: Centralize temporary file and directory creation and use final `XXXXXX` templates such as `sdk.zip.XXXXXX` or extensionless private names. Apply it to every dependency helper, including related NDK and optional archive installers, and keep cleanup traps independent of filename suffixes.
- Validation: Run the complete aggregate bootstrap and focused temp-creation tests with stock macOS and GNU mktemp, including custom `TMPDIR` paths with spaces, SDK, NDK, ShellCheck, failed download, and interruption. Require private unique files, correct cleanup, and progression beyond each download/extraction step on Intel and Apple Silicon hosts where the selected asset architecture is supported.
- Additional location (R1-CHUNK-0487): `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:android/get_deps/helpers/get_cmake_format.sh:L84-L92`
- Additional evidence (R1-CHUNK-0487): The required cmakelang step uses `mktemp ... virtualenv-XXXXXX.pyz` on the Darwin fallback path. Stock macOS rejects the suffix after the replaceable `X` run, so a macOS host whose Python lacks `venv` and `ensurepip` reaches the fallback specifically designed for that condition and fails before downloading the pinned virtualenv bootstrap.
- Additional validation (R1-CHUNK-0487): Include a macOS Python fixture without `venv` or `ensurepip` in the aggregate matrix and require the virtualenv fallback to create and clean a portable private temporary file before cmakelang installation completes.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-05. `platform.sh` now owns temporary file and directory creation through quoted full-path templates whose six replaceable characters are always final, which works with both GNU and stock macOS `mktemp` and preserves `TMPDIR` paths containing spaces. Every dependency helper now uses that API, including SDK, NDK, ShellCheck, the cmakelang virtualenv fallback, JDK staging, formatters, CMake, PowerShell, DOSBox, unar, and the soundfont installer; no helper retains GNU-only `-p` or a suffix after the replaceable run. Archive consumers select their format explicitly rather than by temporary suffix. The Windows VC redistributable retains its required `.exe` name inside a uniquely created private directory and cleans that directory on success, failure, or interruption. The focused fixture exercises real GNU creation and a strict BSD-compatible `mktemp` model, unique files, directories, `TMPDIR` and custom parents containing spaces, final-X templates for `.zip` and staged JDK names, and a scan preventing helper bypass. That fixture, the existing dependency runtime-update contract, Bash syntax checks for every helper, scoped shellcheck, shfmt, PowerShell analysis, BOM checks, and `git diff --check` passed. Physical Intel and Apple Silicon macOS hosts were unavailable, so the full aggregate bootstrap and download-failure matrix were not run there.

### BR-0633: P1 - Emit the sound format that D1 actually loads

- [x] FIXED
- Type: defect
- Confidence: high
- Category: correctness/compatibility
- Found by: R1-CHUNK-0508
- Location: `c01d8fe4686c63d931b1e543a6305bbafaa944a9:game_data/mods/d2x-xl/convert_d2xxl_sounds.ps1:L122-L190,L192-L200,L254-L270` in common D1 and D2 sound conversion and archive naming
- Related: `d1/main/bmread.c:L269-L305`; `d2/main/bmread.c:L268-L303`; `game_data/mods/d2x-xl/convert_all.ps1:L384-L395`; `android/game_scripts/test_mod_loading.json5:L1-L5,L21-L32,L63-L89`; `android/ai tool plans/asset management/plan_d2x_xl_hires_mods.md`
- Evidence: The converter unconditionally resamples both games to 22,050 Hz and names every payload `Sounds/<name>.r22`. Frozen D2 selects `.r22` when its configured sample rate is 22 kHz, but frozen D1 has no `.r22` branch: `ds_load` always constructs `Sounds/<name>.raw` and registers external bytes at 11,025 Hz. Frozen-tree search finds no D1 `.r22` consumer or alias. The maintained generated D1 archive corroborates the deterministic script output with 113 `.r22` entries and zero `.raw` entries. The implementation plan nevertheless calls the D1 conversion complete, and the only mod-loading automation is D2-only, asserts texture coverage, and does not assert that even its mounted sound archive replaced a sample.
- Trigger: Enable the generated `d1-hires-sounds.dxa`, start D1, and play any base sound having a converted replacement
- Impact: D1 never opens any of the 113 generated replacements and silently falls back to its original PIG sounds. The complete advertised D1 high-resolution sound pack is inert even though conversion and aggregate commands report success and the archive is listed as a finished output.
- Expected: D1 output uses the exact pathname and sample representation consumed by frozen D1, or paired D1 runtime support deliberately adds and validates a 22-kHz replacement convention; D1 integration proves replacement bytes are selected and played.
- Suggested fix: For the minimal engine change, parameterize target rate and extension by game, resample D1 to 11,025-Hz unsigned mono, and emit `.raw`; retain 22,050-Hz `.r22` for D2. If 22-kHz D1 playback is desired, implement it in D1 and its mixer under the repository's paired-engine compatibility rules rather than publishing an unrecognized filename. Validate every emitted basename against the admitted game sound table.
- Validation: Generate both packs from controlled WAV tones, mount each in its game, and instrument the selected PhysFS path, byte count, source rate, and played sample identity. Require D1 to open `.raw` at 11,025 Hz and D2 to open `.r22` at 22,050 Hz, reject wrong extensions and unmatched names, and assert audible/sample-hash replacement rather than only archive mount or unrelated texture state.
- Resolution: Fixed on 2026-08-07. The shared D2X-XL pack library now defines one explicit sound output contract per game, and the converter uses that contract for both resampling and archive entry naming. D1 now converts source WAVs to 11,025-Hz unsigned mono bytes named `Sounds/<name>.raw`, exactly matching its native loader, while D2 retains its existing 22,050-Hz `Sounds/<name>.r22` behavior. A focused regression ties both generated contracts and converter call sites to the paired native loader conventions and rejects unsupported game IDs. Complete conversion of the real source archive produced 113 nonempty D1 `.raw` entries with no `.r22`, and 191 nonempty D2 `.r22` entries with no `.raw`; both archives had no duplicate sound paths and both owned extraction stages were absent after success. For representative 44,100-Hz stereo 16-bit source WAVs, the generated D1 and D2 entry lengths exactly matched conversion to 11,025 and 22,050 Hz respectively. The focused regression, PowerShell parser validation, scoped repository quality checks, `git diff --check`, and complete Windows D1 and D2 builds passed. CTest reported no registered tests in either host build tree. An instrumented emulator assertion of the selected PhysFS path and played sample identity was not added; the fixed generated pathname, target-rate byte count, and unchanged native loader contracts were validated directly instead.
